# Implementation Plan

Private implementation ledger and authoritative product specification for **Marktub**. This document follows the structure of `MohamedElashri/agentic/templates/plan.md`: Goal, Scope, Constraints, Current State, Commands, Acceptance Criteria, Test Plan, and Phases. Keep the plan specific and current as implementation proceeds. The project is not implemented merely because a feature is described here.

**Project:** Marktub  
**Product:** Offline-first Markdown reader, hybrid editor, filesystem workbench, and optional agent-document reviewer  
**Stack (locked):** Rust + **Tauri 2** + TypeScript + **Svelte 5** + **CodeMirror 6**; Vite for bundling  
**Platforms:** macOS (Apple Silicon and Intel) and Linux (Wayland/X11); Windows is not a target  
**License:** MIT for original code, subject to third-party license audit  
**Document version:** 3.7 — release review and qualification ledger, 2026-11-14  
**Implementation state:** Phases 0–8 implementation and automated acceptance are complete for the tested macOS ARM64 admitted scope (2026-11-13). Phase 8 adds native selected-revision PDF export and separately enabled/trusted/confirmed shell, SQLite and local TeX jobs to the earlier reader/author/library/Git/Review features. Phase ledgers record admissions, safe refusals and remaining platform, input, durability, security and performance certification. No general OS sandbox or interactive-latency SLA is claimed. Phase 9 release review is in progress; public release remains gated. Phase 10's initial and modern UX polish checkpoints are complete for the tested macOS ARM64 scope, retaining those gates.  
**Prior decisions superseded:** egui/eframe; absolute prohibition on HTML/CSS/JS/WebViews; native-only Mermaid/math/diff renderers. All other user requirements remain in scope unless this plan explicitly refines them.

## Goal

Build **Marktub**, a polished, responsive, resource-conscious Markdown application that works fully offline on macOS and Linux. Its default experience is a calm, typography-first **reader**; on demand it becomes a source editor, synchronized split editor, visually formatted inline editor, file manager, Git review surface, or optional local command runner.

### Product goals and identity

1. **Desktop application, not an online service.** Tauri is a Rust desktop shell with an **OS-provided WebView**: WKWebView on macOS and WebKitGTK on Linux. This is the *deliberate pivot*. Do **not** describe Marktub as WebView-free or as having native GPU widget rendering. Do not bundle Chromium/Electron or host a website. Bundle all HTML/CSS/JS/fonts/assets locally; the app functions with networking disabled.
2. **Fast startup and low memory, measured honestly.** One main Tauri window and one WebView by default. Small Svelte application, lazy modules, viewport virtualization, bounded caches, no global full-document DOM and no idle polling loops. One editable authority; immutable saved snapshots, parser-worker inputs and bounded recovery data are legitimate measured costs, not forbidden copies. Avoid a second continuously synchronized full working-text buffer in Rust. Measure **the complete application process tree**, including attributable WebKit helper processes, not merely the Rust process or app bundle size.
3. **Markdown files remain authoritative.** Work directly on paths and folders without a vault, account, proprietary format, or imported database. Open/close without changes is byte-identical; edits preserve unrelated source. No editor mode may silently reformat the whole file.
4. **Four first-class modes over one working document:** `read`, `source`, `split`, and `visual`. Read is default; split is editable source + synchronized preview; Visual hides Markdown syntax contextually and supports rich interactions without maintaining a separate independently authoritative rich-text tree.
5. **Native desktop integrations:** filesystem permissions and dialogs, watching, search/index, Git, printing/export, window and OS actions run through Rust/Tauri. HTML/CSS/JS exist solely in the installed local UI.
6. **Technical Markdown built in:** CommonMark/GFM, headings, links, tasks, tables, footnotes, code highlighting, callouts, images, TeX math with broad coverage, and Mermaid diagrams. No marketplace or third-party user plugin API.
7. **Superior file organization and review:** multiple ordinary folders; responsive search/preview/outline/quick-open; Git with Pierre-quality presentation; optional review of changes made by external agents, including feedback export.
8. **Optional power tools remain inert:** explicit opt-in shell and **local SQLite-only** execution. Read, preview, export, or reload must never execute document commands.
9. **Simple user settings:** GUI settings and command palette; optional readable settings/sidecars in the user's requested `$option=$value` format. Developer Tauri/Vite configuration is allowed to use JSON, TOML, or JavaScript: the restriction applies to **user-facing configuration**.
10. **Incremental, evidence-led development:** ship useful vertical slices early, maintain reproducible benchmarks, and stop when a correctness/security gate fails instead of inventing a superficial workaround.

### Successive product milestones (not release dates)

- **MVP 0.1 — Reader:** Phase 0 + 1 + the ordinary-reader/renderer/HTML slice of 2 + basic folder/recent-file browsing from 3. Real files, virtualized reading, focus, outline/find, Markdown/GFM, local math/Mermaid, clean-file reload and basic light/dark preferences. G4 is required for shipped math/diagrams; full G3 context-correct paging can continue as a separately labeled experimental slice until verified. Minimal source editing in Phase 1 proves file integrity; the complete Author experience is not an MVP requirement.
- **Alpha 0.2 — Author:** Phase 5a + 5b, with basic settings/palette from 4. Source/Split, safe save/recovery, stable positions and core Visual editing. Advanced table/image controls and paged editing are separate completion gates.
- **Beta 0.3 — Workbench:** Phases 3–6 have implemented primary-platform scope with the gates/limits in their ledgers; cross-platform/input/performance certification remains. Rich Visual blocks, safe bulk file operations, Git status/diffs and selected-hunk staging, complete preferences/palette. HTML export is already available; PDF is delivered in Phase 8b.
- **Release 1.0 — Complete core:** Phase 7, remaining Phase 2 large-file parity work, Phase 8b/8c and Phase 9; Phase 5d is implemented for its documented primary-platform range scope. Agent review, optional SQLite/shell/local TeX, range editing, PDF, and verified macOS/Linux distribution/accessibility/security/performance gates. Every retained requirement remains tracked; a deferred item does not become complete through a milestone rename.

Phase numbers identify work areas, not a requirement to finish every feature in numerical order. Follow the dependency table under Phases. This is a substantial desktop-product roadmap, not a single implementation sprint. Estimate the next slice after Phase 0 evidence; do not assign release dates before platform access, parser feasibility and reference hardware are known. A smaller release may ship under its milestone label without claiming 1.0 scope is done.

### Representative user journeys

- Double-click a local `README.md`; a useful rendered viewport appears quickly, in focus mode, without a setup screen or network traffic. Return tomorrow at the same section.
- Navigate two project folders, filter/sort files, preview one with Space, quick-open another, inspect its outline, and follow relative Markdown links. Every file is still in its original directory.
- Switch Read -> Source -> Split -> Visual; edit a heading, list, table, image, and math expression; save; reopen; view a Git diff containing only intended source changes.
- Open a 100 MB stress fixture; browse headings and rendered sections progressively, with heavy diagrams deferred independently and no hard cutoff that forces the whole document to raw text.
- An external coding agent atomically replaces `plan.md`; Marktub detects the change, preserves reading position, shows changed paragraphs since the last review, collects anchored comments, and copies a feedback report, without embedding an AI service.
- Inspect a repository's working-tree diff, compare a file to HEAD/index, stage one selected hunk safely, and verify that unrelated lines remain unstaged.
- Export a standalone HTML file without other software; configure a local Pandoc/Typst/TeX installation for PDF. Run a trusted shell or local SQLite block only after explicit opt-in and explicit Run.

### Non-goals

Marktub is not an online Markdown editor, Obsidian/SiYuan-style knowledge graph, IDE, remote database client, AI chatbot, cloud sync platform, user-plugin host, full TeX IDE, or Git hosting client. No Windows commitment. It need not imitate every obscure Mermaid.js directive or support arbitrary HTML/CSS execution in untrusted Markdown. Offline app HTML is allowed; document HTML is always treated as untrusted content.

## Scope

### In: modes, interactions, and features

#### 1. Read mode — clean by default

- Open `.md`, `.markdown`, and optionally other explicitly recognized Markdown files; user can open arbitrary UTF-8 text as source.
- Reader-first default; reading focus enabled. Narrow readable text column, optional wide blocks for tables/diagrams, high-quality font stacks, adjustable paragraph/heading/code typography, smart scrolling, and contrast-aware themes. Avoid permanent IDE chrome and busy controls.
- Useful first viewport without waiting for rich blocks: worker parsing for ordinary files, lazy mount math/Mermaid/highlighting/images; outline and workspace index may complete in background. Paged large files may show explicitly provisional sections while document context is indexed. No full-source HTML injection into a giant container for large files.
- Heading TOC, fuzzy heading search, in-document find, bookmarks, history back/forward, cross-file relative links, tabs, quick open, line/heading deep links, drag file into window, and context menus.
- Persistent reading position keyed by file identity + semantic anchor and local offset; reflow/theme/window-size changes should not lose the selected section. Back/forward navigation independent of tab order.
- Manual reload and native directory watching; detect atomic rename replacement by external agents, preserve reader anchor, and avoid overwriting dirty edits.
- Reading focus is default, without forced paragraph dimming; optional focus on current heading/paragraph and temporary toolbar reveal.

#### 2. Source and Split modes

- **CodeMirror 6** is the source editor: visible Markdown syntax, its contenteditable/input and IME handling, syntax color, soft wrap, optional line numbers, folding, indent and list continuation, find/replace, multiple selections, undo/redo, keymap customization, markdown formatting shortcuts. Test WebView spellcheck as a best-effort option; no new offline spelling engine is implied.
- Split mode: CodeMirror source pane + rendered preview pane; resizable divider, selected-source-to-preview mapping, preview click-to-source, scroll sync anchored by headings/block ranges (never only scroll percentages).
- Do not re-create CodeMirror state when switching among Source, Split, and Visual. Persist/restore the `EditorState`, undo stack, selection, folded ranges and scroll anchors; detach costly DOM views for inactive tabs when necessary, retaining bounded state.
- Correct UTF-8, emoji, combining characters, Arabic/Hebrew BiDi, CJK IME composition and input accessibility must be tested in the actual macOS/Linux WebViews, not assumed from Chrome screenshots.
- Do not send a full editor snapshot through Tauri IPC on every keystroke. Local UI transactions stay in the WebView; see the explicit ownership protocol below.

#### 3. Visual editing — source-preserving, not a second Markdown serializer

- Implement Visual mode as a **CodeMirror-based live formatted editor** with decorations, hidden markers, inline widgets, and source-backed block controls; **do not start with a second TipTap/Milkdown/ProseMirror document model**. This is an intentional simplification that reduces DOM, RAM, double-editing semantics, and destructive Markdown reserialization.
- In inactive regions, present rendered headings, emphasis, links, lists/tasks, block quotes, code, math, Mermaid and images. Reveal the active block's necessary source tokens contextually or in a compact popover when editing. Keyboard/caret operations and undo go through the same CodeMirror transaction pipeline as Source.
- GFM tables are visually readable and **cell-editable** with keyboard movement, row/column add/delete, alignment, escaped-pipe handling, and minimal table-span source patches. Use a specialized table widget/overlay, not an entirely separate document authority. Maintain source range and undo boundaries.
- Local image drag/drop from Finder/Nautilus/Dolphin, choosing copy to relative assets directory (default proposed) or link in place. Editable alt text, title, path, click selection, block drag/reordering with insertion feedback, pointer resizing with aspect-ratio lock and numeric dimensions. Use Pandoc-style `{width=... height=...}` attributes when opted in, preserve original syntax and surrounding whitespace, and group each action into one undo step.
- Inline math is rendered and editable via a source popover, with error diagnostics and unsupported-source fallback. Mermaid code blocks render as diagrams with source-edit action, zoom/pan, and safe error messages. Unsupported Markdown constructs must remain visible and editable in Source, not lost when switching modes.
- **Honest Visual contract:** It is in-place visual Markdown editing, not an HTML document designer. Arbitrary embedded HTML and arbitrary LaTeX package commands may stay source-only. Explain these boundaries in docs. Prove minimal-diff editing for headings, emphasis, nested lists, tables, image attributes, math and Unicode before claiming Visual complete.

#### 4. Markdown, TeX, diagrams and code

- Support **CommonMark and GFM** plus practical extensions: tables, task lists, strikethrough, autolinks, fenced language blocks, syntax highlighting, footnotes, heading IDs, front matter (preserved), callouts/alerts, definition lists when feasible, math delimiters and Pandoc-compatible image sizing. Document extension interactions and parser ambiguity, especially escaped dollar signs/currency and fences.
- Initial rendering pipeline: `markdown-it` plus a small, **audited, explicitly selected** set of local extensions; raw HTML is disabled in v1 and preserved as source. Tokens provide line-level block maps, not exact inline byte ranges or stable identities across edits. Maintain revisioned block IDs and a tested source-range adapter for editable constructs; use Lezer syntax ranges only when they agree with the display grammar. Ambiguous constructs reveal source instead of guessing a patch. No canonical Markdown reserialization.
- **Math:** KaTeX is the default lazy-loaded fast renderer; MathJax 4 is a local lazy secondary renderer for its documented additional syntax, not an automatic retry for every malformed expression. Neither is a full TeX engine. Use an allowlist of bundled math packages, safe URL/HTML settings, finite size/expansion limits and no document-directed module loading. Default to expression-local macros plus literal configured macros; document-wide macro definitions, labels and numbering require ordered context/indexing and cache invalidation independent of viewport order. Never share mutable macro state between documents. Cache by source + renderer/version/config/context/scale/theme. Offer manually configured local TeX only through the explicit trusted workflow in Phase 8c.
- **Mermaid:** bundle Mermaid.js locally; render visible blocks through a serialized queue with `startOnLoad=false`, locked `securityLevel=strict`, HTML labels disabled, finite text/edge limits and an allowlist of supported diagram families. Ignore document directives/front matter that try to override security, CSS, fonts, loaders or layouts. Sanitize SVG with local fragment references only; keep source editable. DOM-dependent layout may run on the main WebView: a timer/Promise cannot interrupt synchronous work. Enforce admission limits before rendering; defer rejected blocks to explicit retry or source. Cancellation drops queued/stale results and terminates workers where applicable, not an already running DOM layout. No CDN, CLI server or Chromium process.
- Code syntax highlighting: CodeMirror/Lezer in source; efficient code block highlighter for rendered view with lazy grammar loading and visibility-driven work; do not eagerly tokenize thousands of blocks. Prefer one highlighting infrastructure where practical and avoid importing an entire language catalog.
- Mermaid and TeX render errors must not compromise surrounding document rendering or inject arbitrary HTML. Remote images never auto-fetch. An explicit external-link action may open an allowlisted `https`/`http` URL in the OS browser; that is user-initiated external navigation, not an app asset fetch. Block WebView navigation, automatic redirects, unsafe schemes and Mermaid-generated command links.

#### 5. Filesystem library and document navigation

- All roots are **real selected directories**, not vaults; multi-root sidebar, nested folder tree, pinned and recent files, favorites/bookmarks, fuzzy quick-open, tabs, session restore, history and relative cross-file links.
- **List, compact-list, and grid** file views; sort by name/date/type/size; filters for Markdown/other supported files; useful metadata, Git badges, file change indicators and folder counts when cheap.
- Spacebar quick preview without a permanent tab; multi-selection, rename, move, copy, create folder/file, safe bulk move/delete with confirmation and trash where supported. Preserve metadata where feasible, avoid conflicts/overwrites/symlink loops, and update links conservatively (never blindly rewrite arbitrary text).
- Background incremental search/index (SQLite FTS5 as an optional disposable local index) with bounded work and clear cancel/clear controls. A library reset must never remove Markdown files or durable user bookmarks/settings/review records.
- Filesystem operations and indexing stay in Rust; frontend receives paged/filtered lists and relevant metadata, **not the entire directory tree**. Watch directories, debounce change storms, handle atomic saves, permissions, symlinks and moves.
- No automatic recursive indexing of the entire home directory without explicitly added roots. No daemon, cloud account, or import requirement.

#### 6. Git integration and review-quality diffs

- Rust `git2` for repository discovery, status, branch-name display, bounded history, index/HEAD and file versions; native diff computation (`git2` patches and optional `imara-diff`) stays in Rust. No commit, checkout, branch mutation, merge/rebase or remote operation in v1. Make index mutations race-safe, especially partial staging.
- Use **`@pierre/diffs` vanilla JS** as the initial **candidate** renderer in the local Tauri WebView; evaluate accessibility, licensing, output security, WebKit compatibility, workers, memory and virtualization in Phase 6 before adoption. Its Shiki-based highlighting must be lazy and justified against the shared-highlighter preference. Use unified/split/intraline/scroll features if it passes. **Fallback:** a minimal virtualized frontend renderer from Rust-provided hunks; Pierre is not mandatory and its editable mode is not a second source editor.
- Show working tree vs index, index vs HEAD, working tree vs HEAD, arbitrary selected commits, and optional saved-file snapshot/last-read baseline; label baselines unmistakably. Git badges in sidebar, compare actions, jump by hunk, word highlights, context folding, unified/split layouts, accessible keyboard navigation.
- **Staging must not trust UI diff pixels or a generic text diff.** Hunk IDs are scoped to repository, path and baseline revisions. Rust obtains the index lock, revalidates the on-disk index under that lock, builds the selected patch against index bytes and commits using Git lock semantics; do not check first then blindly write a stale in-memory index. Read a coherent worktree snapshot; a later worktree edit invalidates the displayed selection, without changing which captured bytes are staged. Verify exact index content against Git CLI in integration tests.
- Treat Git clean/smudge/process filters, LFS, working-tree encodings, EOL attributes, external diff/textconv and repository-configured tools as part of the execution boundary. Never launch these automatically. Support plain text and tested built-in EOL conversions first; refuse selected-hunk mutations for unmerged entries, submodules, binary/symlink/rename/filter/encoding cases until their semantics are proven. Display an explanation and preserve read-only comparison; detection is required even when mutation is unsupported.
- Git stays read-only except explicit stage/unstage/discard actions. Confirm destructive discard, preserve a recovery copy and block it on a dirty editor; ordinary stage/unstage need an explicit action and stale checks, not a repetitive destructive-action dialog. No GitHub/GitLab login, pull/push or background network requests. Git comparisons/staging use saved disk/index bytes; label unsaved editor changes and offer Save before staging them.

#### 7. Optional Vantage-like agent review

- Off by default. Detect external edits (without inferring their author), including write-to-temp-and-rename. Maintain an explicit last-reviewed baseline and optionally current-view snapshots, section/block-level rendered additions/removals and source diff; annotate text with source range, content fingerprint and revision/hash. Viewing alone never advances the review baseline.
- Comments, review status (approve/request changes), distinctly labeled changed-since-last-viewed and changed-since-last-reviewed baselines, orphaned comment handling, clipboard feedback report and portable JSON/Markdown export. Include path, revision IDs, relevant quotes, line/source ranges and comments, without fabricating stable anchors when source changes. “Viewed” never claims every paragraph was read.
- No embedded model, MCP server, browser remote service, or forced collaboration stack. Keep durable review records separate from disposable search caches; allow reset/export/deletion.
- On dirty local buffer + external disk edit, block blind overwrite and offer Compare, Reload, Keep Local or Save As; preserve both snapshots. Add Merge only when implemented and verified; a disabled/fake merge button is not a required first slice.

#### 8. Optional command execution

- Disabled globally and per workspace by default. Explicit opt-in + trusted root + explicit per-block Run; **never execute during open, preview, render, file watch, export, or document link activation**.
- **Shell:** opt-in local script run with chosen working directory/interpreter, native confirmation of the exact command, stdout/stderr truncation, timeout/cancel and best-effort process-group termination. Shell evaluation is intentional for this feature; settings/paths are never accidentally interpolated into it. This is not an OS sandbox. Do not grant generic Tauri shell-plugin permissions to rendered content.
- **SQLite only:** local user-selected database file; open readonly initially; no implicit selection of app settings/session databases; explicit confirmation and separate permission for writes. Statement validation and SQLite authorizer rules must block unexpected `ATTACH`, extension loading and side-effecting statements in readonly mode. Parameterize app-generated SQL, cap row count/output/time and allow cancel. Other database engines explicitly out of scope.
- Isolate runner implementation behind private Rust traits/interfaces for maintainability **without** shipping a user plugin SDK. Document limits: a trusted shell command is not sandboxed unless an OS-specific sandbox is actually implemented.

#### 9. Settings, themes and command palette

- GUI controls first. Command palette with fuzzy commands, discoverable and rebindable keyboard shortcuts, recent commands, searchable preferences, contextual file actions, quick-open and heading search.
- At least two curated light and two dark themes. Follow system appearance by default. Separate UI/reading/source/code font choices; font enumeration and reliable Arabic/CJK/emoji fallback, line spacing, width, ligatures where supported, zoom and contrast checks. Support custom themes with live preview; load fonts only when used.
- User configuration **must** use a simple validated line format, e.g. `$reader.width=760` or `$reader.font="Literata"`. Specify the grammar in Phase 1: UTF-8, blank lines and full-line `#` comments, `$` plus dotted ASCII key, `=`, then boolean/finite decimal/bare enum or double-quoted string; only quoted strings accept escapes (`\"`, `\\`, `\n`, `\t`). No inline comments, duplicate keys, evaluation or interpolation. Reject invalid files atomically with line diagnostics; keep last-good settings. Validate ranges, unknown keys and schema migrations explicitly.
- Precedence: global -> workspace -> optional file -> session override. Per-file settings default to app-managed local metadata, not silently inserted Markdown front matter; opt-in portable sidecar export uses the same `$key=value` grammar. Changes in settings do not rewrite Markdown.
- Workspace overrides are app-managed by default. Importing a repository `.conf`/sidecar never grants trust, executable paths, extra filesystem access or execution permission. Security-sensitive settings and trust grants are global app-managed records; session/file/workspace layers cannot escalate them. Custom themes accept typed tokens, never arbitrary CSS or resource URLs. Font selection may use OS enumeration; do not imply WebView APIs alone enumerate every installed font.
- Save sensible defaults: Read mode with focus, system theme, auto-reload clean files, no auto-save initially, no telemetry, no network fetch, execution/review off, bounded/cancelable Git status for visible repositories, local HTML export available from MVP. Crash-recovery writes do not save the user's Markdown.

#### 10. Export and printing

- Standalone **HTML export** works with no external tooling, assembled from an explicit saved or dirty revision through the sanitized renderer/export adapter. Embed CSS and approved local images; use static KaTeX HTML + embedded fonts or MathJax SVG/MathML for math and sanitized SVG/image for Mermaid. KaTeX does not natively emit SVG. Include semantic headings, links/footnotes and accessible source alternatives; no executable JavaScript or network resources. Export all sections through a sequential job, independent of which virtual blocks are mounted. Report missing/unsupported assets and allow cancellation before replacing an output file.
- **PDF via user-installed Pandoc** and selected local Typst/TeX backend. GUI detection/path configuration/version diagnostics, page size/margins/templates/presets, TOC, code highlighting, math, SVG diagrams, footnotes, image sizes and links. Never auto-download tools, use hidden remote converters, or invoke untrusted Pandoc filters by default.
- Phase 8b builds an allowlisted Pandoc JSON AST/intermediate from the selected document revision, excluding raw HTML/TeX/Typst, executable filters, repository defaults/templates and document-controlled engine options. Stage validated local assets in a private temporary directory. Permit only app-owned templates and user-selected tool paths; pass arguments without shell interpolation. Use Pandoc `--sandbox` where supported, but it does not sandbox PDF engines or filters. Typst/TeX inputs need separate escaping/resource tests; pre-render unsafe/unsupported math or report it. Do not feed arbitrary original Markdown directly to a TeX engine and call it safe.
- Explicit PDF export authorizes the configured converter/engine for that job; it does not enable document shell/SQL blocks. Arbitrary trusted local TeX rendering in Phase 8c requires separate authorization. Disable package/network downloads; verify tool behavior instead of assuming a timeout or no-shell-escape is a sandbox. Offer system Open/Print of generated PDF when configured; direct WebView printing is not a cross-platform promise.
- Print/export correctness should be tested on installed local tools and report degraded or unsupported features before export, rather than claim universal equivalence among MathJax, KaTeX and LaTeX/Pandoc.

### Expected GUI, defaults and shortcuts

**Window:** one WebView; narrow toolbar; optional left library panel; central document (Read/Source/Split/Visual); optional right outline/Review panel; minimal status line. In Read focus, hide panels/toolbar until deliberate action. Avoid nested WebViews, browser iframes for ordinary content, ribbon/IDE layout, and dozens of mounted hidden tabs.

**Opening:** path passed on CLI or OS association -> open in Read with no welcome page. No path -> restore session when enabled or show a compact Open File/Open Folder/Recent screen. Folder tree appears without waiting for indexing.

**Focus:** reading focus on by default; independent writing focus and optional typewriter scroll. Easy exit without losing mode, position or selections.

**Shortcuts** (OS modifier = Cmd macOS, Ctrl Linux; audit conflicts during implementation):

| Command | Suggested default |
|---|---|
| Open file | Mod+O |
| Quick open | Mod+P |
| Command palette | Mod+Shift+P |
| Find in document | Mod+F |
| Search workspaces | Mod+Shift+F |
| Outline/heading search | Mod+Shift+O |
| Save | Mod+S |
| Save As | Mod+Shift+S |
| Read / Source | Mod+Shift+R / Mod+Shift+E |
| Toggle reading focus | Mod+Alt+F |
| Settings | Mod+, |
| Next/previous tab | Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab, adapted to OS |
| Toggle file browser | Mod+Alt+B (Phase 4; avoids layout-dependent Backslash) |
| Mode switches, focus, Git/review | Command palette + explicit visible controls; configurable shortcuts |
| Next/previous diff hunk | F7 / Shift+F7 (test OS binding) |

**User settings example** (`.conf`-style files, not JSON/TOML/YAML):

```ini
# All keys start with '$'; values are literals, never commands.
$view.default=read
$reader.focus=true
$reader.width=760
$reader.font="Literata"
$editor.font="Iosevka"
$editor.line_numbers=false
$editor.visual.syntax_visibility=active_block
$theme.mode=system
$theme.name="Marktub Light"
$git.diff_layout=split
$review.enabled=false
$execution.enabled=false
$execution.sql_engine=sqlite
$export.pdf_engine=typst
```

### Out (explicitly excluded)

- Electron, CEF, an independently bundled Chromium, Node.js server at runtime, a remote-hosted UI, CDN assets, mandatory login/telemetry, automatic network retrieval of document content or scripts.
- egui/eframe, native custom GPU widget engine, a second full rich-document editor, default ProseMirror/TipTap/Milkdown authority, and a framework rewrite after bootstrap without an ADR and user approval.
- Windows support, phone/mobile builds, knowledge graph, vault import format, cloud sync, collaborative CRDT, AI chat/model/agent integration, marketplace/plugin system, background scripts on opening documents.
- Complete arbitrary HTML/CSS rendering of untrusted Markdown; full TeX engine inside JS; complete Mermaid parity if unsupported grammar can be shown faithfully as source.
- Remote databases, non-SQLite SQL backends, general notebook kernels, arbitrary shell access by default, built-in IDE or repository-hosting client.
- Arbitrary file-size marketing cutoffs, automatic full-source reserialization, silent loss of unsupported syntax, or unbounded rich DOM for huge files. Real resource ceilings may stop an operation with a recoverable diagnostic; no application can guarantee opening every possible file on finite hardware.

### Proposed repository structure and architecture

Start with a **single Rust backend crate** and a modest Svelte/TypeScript frontend. Split into crates/packages only when code and tests demonstrate a need. Avoid dozens of empty crates, overly generic plugin interfaces, and duplicating the Markdown AST in Rust and JS.

```text
marktub/
  plan.md                      # this document; update status/evidence as work proceeds
  AGENTS.md                    # short project instructions derived from this plan
  README.md
  LICENSE
  package.json
  pnpm-lock.yaml
  vite.config.ts
  tsconfig.json
  src/                         # local bundled Svelte + TypeScript frontend
    app/
    components/
    editor/                    # CodeMirror instance, transactions, live Visual decorations
    reader/                    # virtual blocks, markdown-it renderer, focus, anchors
    renderers/                 # lazy KaTeX, MathJax, Mermaid, code, SVG sanitization
    git/                       # Pierre adapter and alternative virtualized diff view
    library/
    review/
    settings/
    bridge/                    # typed Tauri IPC client + revision protocol
    styles/                    # CSS variables, themes; no remote CSS
  src-tauri/
    Cargo.toml
    Cargo.lock
    build.rs
    tauri.conf.json
    capabilities/              # minimal explicit Tauri permissions
    src/
      main.rs
      lib.rs
      commands/                # narrow typed IPC surface
      files/                   # safe read/write, identity, watcher, recovery
      library/                 # filesystem tree, indexing, searching
      git/                     # status, patches, staging
      settings/                # $option=$value parser/persistence
      review/                  # snapshots, comment metadata
      execution/               # opt-in shell and SQLite only
      export/                  # HTML/Pandoc/TeX subprocess control
  tests/
    fixtures/                  # real Markdown, Git, unicode and file-race fixtures
    e2e/                       # actual app WebView scenarios; test drivers excluded from releases
  benchmarks/                  # process-tree memory, responsiveness, large-file tests
  docs/
    evidence/                  # machine-readable evidence only; phase prose stays in plan.md
  .github/workflows/
```

**Logical layers**:

```text
 Rust backend                          One local Tauri WebView
 -------------------------------       -----------------------------------
 Files / watcher / Git                 Svelte shell / settings / commands
   -> authorized saved revisions  ---> Reader worker -> virtual Read view
   -> range/index/asset services         |
                                       +-> CodeMirror state (on edit)
 Save / byte provenance / recovery <---     Source / Split / Visual
   -> immutable bases + patch journal      one current editable revision

 Optional review / disposable search <- revisioned requests/results -> UI
 Export job: explicit saved/dirty revision -> finalized sequential output
 Runners: separate Rust/native trust + exact-action authorization
```

#### Ownership and IPC protocol — critical correction to v2

The previous egui plan assumed a Rust-owned rope as the single editor source of truth. In Tauri this would tempt the agent to keep another full working copy in JS and synchronize on every keystroke. **Do not do that.** Instead specify the authority by operation:

- **Disk authority (Rust):** saved bytes/revision, permissions, BOM/newline provenance, write validation, watcher, snapshots, Git/index and durable metadata. A document session ID survives an atomic replacement; an inode is evidence about a revision, not permanent document identity. Do not expose native paths as frontend authority: use opaque authorized document/root/asset handles, including non-UTF-8 OS filenames where encountered.
- **Unsaved editing authority (CodeMirror 6):** one `EditorState` per actively edited ordinary-sized document, containing current working text, selections, undo transactions, and dirty revision. The reader and Visual mode derive from this state; they never own a second independently editable AST. `Svelte` state stores UI metadata, not an additional full text string.
- **IPC snapshot on open:** capture a coherent saved revision, then return descriptor plus ordinary-document text once to create reader state or CodeMirror on demand. Read-only opening never constructs CodeMirror automatically. A bounded worker input or immutable saved base is allowed, with explicit accounting for UTF-8/UTF-16/IPC copies. Recheck/retry if a file changes during snapshot capture.
- **Source fidelity adapter:** CodeMirror uses logical line breaks; its string serialization alone cannot round-trip mixed LF/CRLF/CR. Keep an immutable original-byte snapshot (app-private snapshot file when needed), a piece/source provenance map, BOM state and original line-terminator records. Editor text normalizes logical breaks; source patches splice untouched original byte pieces verbatim and encode inserted pieces as UTF-8. New breaks use the surrounding line's style or the document's dominant style; normalization is an explicit separate command. Track terminator deletion/insertion through undo/redo and saved-base rebasing. Byte output, not normalized string equality alone, determines whether the file changed. Reject invalid inserted surrogate sequences visibly. A full `doc.toString()` save is insufficient.
- **IPC changes:** use a versioned schema with `session_id`, `generation`, `operation_id`, `base_revision`, `new_revision` and an ordered changeset. Define all `from`/`to` positions in the changeset against its base normalized UTF-16 revision; disallow overlaps and split-surrogate boundaries. A source-provenance map converts these to byte-piece operations. Rust validates contiguous revisions and stores operations with inserted text; it need not maintain a full live text rope. Duplicate operation IDs are idempotent; gaps request retransmission or an explicit checkpoint. Never coalesce patches by concatenating coordinates from different revisions. Hash snapshots/checkpoints, not the entire text after every keystroke.
- **Recovery/ACK:** debounce journal batches (initial target 500 ms, flush at 1 s during continuous input), bound queued bytes and apply backpressure/checkpointing. Distinguish received ACK from durable ACK after journal flush; expose recovery status/failure. Only durably acknowledged edits are guaranteed after a crash; recent unacknowledged input can be lost. Preserve dirty text on disk-full/IPC failure and offer Save As. Checkpoints compact long sessions off the input path; test crashes before/after ACK and save/journal rotation. Store recovery privately with documented retention, explicit discard and no content in routine logs.
- **Save:** capture revision N and wait for its journal/checkpoint barrier; Rust streams original pieces + acknowledged edits into a same-directory temporary file, validates bytes/hash/metadata and follows the save protocol below. Later typing may create N+1: a successful N save advances only the saved base, keeps later changes dirty and preserves undo/provenance. Serialize saves per session; never clear dirty state on an ACK for an older revision. Stream/checkpoint explicit-save payloads when useful, but do not bypass the fidelity adapter with a normalized whole-text snapshot.
- **Race/conflict:** external writes never mutate a dirty buffer underneath the editor. Keep base / unsaved / current-disk inputs for Compare, Reload, Keep Local and Save As; automatic three-way merge is a later enhancement. Keep Local does not itself overwrite a changed disk revision; Overwrite is a separate explicit decision. Recovery snapshots remain available on failure.
- **Modes:** Read and Split read the current unsaved editor state when a document is dirty (not the last disk version); no redundant persisted copy. In Read-only documents with no active editor, use lean reader-backed data without constructing CodeMirror until edit is requested.
- **Inactive tabs:** cap mounted editor/preview/diagram DOM. In-session dirty tabs retain the same EditorState/undo; warn and offer explicit close/save/recovery choices if memory is tight. Clean inactive tabs may unload and reload from a validated revision, with a documented undo-reset boundary. Cross-restart recovery restores text, not a promised full undo history. Never silently evict unsaved state or call an unacknowledged journal durable.

#### Safe saves and filesystem limits

- For local regular files, create an exclusive same-directory temp, stream the new bytes, preserve required mode/metadata, flush it, revalidate target identity + expected hash, then rename/replace and sync the directory where supported. Preserve a recoverable pre-save snapshot; do not report success before the persistence steps actually succeed. Distinguish Save As, overwrite, permission failure and partial/durability failure. No-op Save does not replace the file or touch metadata.
- Atomic rename prevents a partially visible file; it is **not a portable compare-and-swap against unrelated writers**. A writer can change the path between revalidation and replacement. Watchers and advisory locks cannot remove that race for noncooperating apps. Revalidate as late as possible, retain both captured disk bytes and pending user bytes, detect/report races when observable, and document the residual window. Do not claim universal prevention of concurrent overwrites. Prefer Save As in uncertain cases; strongest guarantees apply to supported local filesystems and cooperating writers.
- Resolve symbolic links to an explicitly authorized target and keep the link intact; reject retargeting/escape before writes. Hardlinks require an explicit choice to break the link with an atomic replacement or Save As; do not silently switch to unsafe in-place writes. Preserve mode bits; define tested ACL/xattr/ownership handling per OS and warn/refuse if required metadata cannot be preserved. Read-only files remain read-only unless the user chooses a new destination.
- Network/FUSE/cloud-backed filesystems are best-effort until tested; disclose weaker rename/durability semantics. File watchers are invalidation hints: revalidate on focus and before save, coalesce storms, detect dropped events and rescan incrementally. Self-write suppression uses resulting identity/hash, not a time window that could hide external writes.

#### Large document architecture — adaptive, no arbitrary hard limit

A Tauri WebView has an inherent baseline memory cost. A naive `markdownIt.render(entire100MBFile)` + `innerHTML` + all Mermaid/MathJax would exhaust memory and freeze. Likewise loading all files into CodeMirror and Rust simultaneously defeats the resource goal. Implement two cooperating strategies:

- **Ordinary documents:** full `markdown-it` parsing in a cancelable worker is initially acceptable; DOM and rich rendering are virtualized. `markdown-it` is not an incremental parser, so do not promise O(changed-range) parsing. Debounce preview work, transfer/coalesce revisioned changes, cap worker inputs/caches and publish only matching-generation results. Measure full reparses; add incremental invalidation only after correctness evidence. A derived worker copy is allowed and measured; Svelte must not keep another full string for convenience.
- **Large-document reading:** a Rust byte/line scanner supplies range access and conservative candidate boundaries, not a second authoritative Markdown parser. `markdown-it` is a whole-input parser: arbitrary chunks can misparse nested lists, open fences, Setext headings and references defined at the end. Phase 2 must implement a resumable/context-aware adapter or demonstrate an alternative bounded parsing strategy within the same display grammar. A line/heading regex index alone does not satisfy this gate.
- **Document-wide context:** maintain link-reference/footnote definitions, duplicate heading-ID order and optional math labels/macros independently of mounted pages. Early sections may be explicitly provisional until the context scan completes, then rerender without losing position. Export waits for a finalized coherent index. Cross-page semantic results must match whole-document parsing on the parity corpus. If correctness cannot be established, show source for the affected span with a diagnostic; do not certify the paged renderer as complete.
- **Costs and bounds:** a complete index/TOC necessarily costs at least one O(file size) scan; first visible sections may precede it. Store compact/paged indexes, use segmented scrolling where WebView scroll-height precision requires it, and handle a single giant list/paragraph/fence as bounded source segments rather than one enormous rich block. Measure fixture structure as well as bytes. A resource failure leaves already read sections available, not a frozen UI.
- **Large-document editing (Phase 5d):** explicit range-edit sessions use a bounded CodeMirror window against a captured base revision and the same provenance/journal/save service. Flush acknowledged edits before changing windows; identify the loaded range and scope find/replace/selection/undo to it. Prevent overlapping stale patches; any disk conflict blocks commit. Stream a whole-file rewrite atomically from original pieces + edits. Ordinary four-mode editing remains supported within measured resources; a full 100 MB rich editor and cross-window undo are not promised. Range editing is a separate deliverable, not something CodeMirror gains from virtualization automatically.
- **Adaptive selection:** choose based on measured byte size, line/block complexity and explicit budgets; portable free-memory detection is not assumed. Allow users to choose a lean reader. No arbitrary 10/100 MB rejection; actual resource/OS limits produce visible recoverable errors. Benchmark 1, 10 and 100 MB and larger fixtures, never call the benchmark tier a supported maximum.
- **Reader interactions:** app-level find/heading navigation operates on source/index, reveals offscreen matches and handles selection/copy across pages explicitly. Native browser find/select-all cannot search unmounted content. Provide keyboard section navigation and a bounded paginated accessible reading option; test screen-reader continuity. Print/export run their own sequential renderer, not DOM capture of the visible viewport.
- **Heavy work:** impose per-block source/edge/pixel/output admission limits, placeholders and explicit retry. Bound image decode dimensions as well as compressed bytes. Workers can be terminated; a Promise timeout on synchronous main-thread layout cannot enforce a hard deadline. Do not promise immediate cancellation of Mermaid DOM work or hard resource isolation without a separate process. Virtualize a bounded block list; `IntersectionObserver` alone does not bound DOM/layout.

#### Security boundaries for a webview reading local files

- Make Rust Tauri commands narrow and validate authorized root/document/asset handles on every operation, including background workers and export. Configure app-command permissions explicitly (Tauri custom commands are not denied merely by omitting plugin permissions); list enabled capabilities explicitly. Use restrictive CSP, no remote-origin grants and no generic filesystem/shell command surface.
- **Threat model:** inert document data must not become executable JS. Capabilities scope windows/WebViews, not individual DOM subtrees; content injected into the main document shares its execution context. A fully compromised main WebView can call its granted commands. Sanitization/CSP plus backend validation reduce that risk; a frontend-supplied `user_clicked=true` or token held by JS cannot prove intent. Require Rust/native confirmation for granting trust and launching shell/local TeX/SQL writes, bound to exact command/database/revision. Root trust alone is insufficient. Do not claim the one-WebView design isolates arbitrary hostile scripts or provides an OS sandbox.
- Markdown and agent-generated documents are untrusted. Raw HTML is source-only in v1; sanitize generated HTML/SVG, strip scripts/events, disallow `javascript:`, `file:` and unexpected rendered-link schemes, prevent traversal/arbitrary `asset:` reads. No inline JS, document iframe, automatic remote images, Mermaid command links or embedded HTML execution.
- Never inject raw Markdown, settings, filenames or user text as HTML. Only audited renderer output may enter a single sanitized HTML/SVG sink (for example DOMPurify configured for HTML/SVG/MathML); test it against URI/CSS/ID-clobbering vectors. Prefer DOM construction/text nodes for interactive widgets. Namespace diagram IDs and preserve only approved local fragment references.
- **Path grants:** opening one file grants that document, not its directory tree. User-selected roots grant scoped file operations, not execution. Relative navigation/assets outside existing grants need a native grant action; never auto-expand scope through `../`, symlinks or document links. For single-file use, offer an explicit containing-folder asset grant with clear scope. Validate real path components and use handle-relative/no-follow operations where supported to resist symlink swaps, not lexical prefix checks alone. Custom asset URLs identify approved handles, not arbitrary absolute paths; validate again at resource load and enforce MIME/byte/pixel limits. Local SVG images receive the same external-reference policy as diagrams.
- Bundle all JS/CSS/math fonts/diagram assets inside the app. CSP should prohibit external script/connect/font/frame sources; only allow necessary local IPC/custom protocol sources. Avoid enabling dangerous Tauri broad filesystem/shell scopes for convenience.
- All optional shell, local TeX, Pandoc, Git mutations and SQL writes are Rust-owned commands with explicit UI intent, validation, timeouts, cancellation and logs. Unknown untrusted TeX compilation is never implicit; local TeX isolation is limited unless a genuine sandbox exists.
- Do not use the browser's localStorage as durable source or metadata authority; use Rust-managed files/SQLite. Test on WKWebView and WebKitGTK, whose behavior and process model differ from Chrome.
- Keep durable settings/bookmarks/reviews, disposable index and sensitive base/recovery snapshots separate. Define per-store schema versions, transactions/migrations, retention/storage quotas, clear/export controls and crash-safe recovery. If a quota is reached, stop background capture and notify; never evict uncommitted edits silently. Opening a file does not authorize indefinite full-content snapshots when Review is disabled.

### Architecture risk register (v2 weaknesses and decisions)

| Risk / weakness found in prior plan | Severity | Resolution / mandatory gate |
|---|---|---|
| Native-only mandate conflicts with pivot | High | Explicitly accept OS WebView; forbid bundled Chromium/remote UI; update product claims and tests |
| Full Rust rope + JS editor duplicates memory and IPC copies | Critical | CodeMirror working authority; immutable base + provenance/journal; allow and measure derived worker/snapshot copies |
| Separate rich-text editor silently rewrites Markdown | Critical | CodeMirror decorations/live inline editing; minimal source-range edits; no ProseMirror authority in v1 |
| Huge full-DOM reader + many diagrams consume unbounded RAM | Critical | Virtual blocks, conservative admission limits, byte-budget caches and a separately gated paged parser |
| Tauri low package size mistaken for low total RAM | High | Whole process tree RSS/PSS/footprint including WebKit helpers; compare idle baseline |
| Linux WebKitGTK behavior differs from macOS WKWebView | High | Primary-OS bootstrap and explicit second-OS access gate; both before platform claims; native IME/drag/drop/print checked |
| TeX 'full support' cannot be delivered by one JS package | High | KaTeX fast default + MathJax compatibility fallback + explicitly trusted external TeX; test corpus |
| Mermaid heavy startup/unsafe generated SVG | High | Lazy import/viewport jobs, strict mode, sanitizer, no remote content, perf fixtures |
| Pierre Diffs dependency unknown cross-platform cost | Medium | Time-boxed licence/compat/virtualization spike; fallback custom renderer; Git stage Rust-side |
| Diff visualization incorrectly used to stage Git patches | Critical | Validate Git index and patch OIDs in Rust; recheck with Git CLI; reject stale changes |
| File watcher loses agent changes/overwrites dirty buffer | Critical | Watchers are hints; validate revisions before save, preserve recovery, compare conflicts and disclose noncooperating-writer race |
| Shell/SQL/TeX risks from untrusted Markdown in WebView | Critical | Default-off execution, backend trust records/native confirmation, narrow commands, process limits, SQL protections; no same-DOM isolation claim |
| User config grammar ambiguous | Medium | Formal parser + precedence + GUI editing + escaping tests; no config evaluation |
| Images and math export differ from preview | Medium | Unified semantic fixtures and export adapter; asset conversion, fallback diagnostics, real PDF tests |
| Renderer/parser mismatch (Lezer vs markdown-it) | Medium | One canonical display grammar + source maps; syntax-only Lezer; parity fixture suite |
| UI accretion turns reader into an IDE | Medium | Default focus mode; module mounts only when invoked; UX snapshot checks |
| Agent review baseline/identity too vague | Medium | Explicit Mark Reviewed baseline; no attribution of writer identity; ambiguous/orphaned anchors and portable schema |
| Claims of performance unsupported by measurements | High | Repeatable release-mode benchmarks, hardware/environment metadata and negative-result ledger |
| CodeMirror newline normalization breaks byte preservation | Critical | Byte-piece provenance + logical-offset mapping; undo/redo and saved-revision rebase fixtures before editing ships |
| Line token maps mistaken for exact editable ranges | Critical | Range adapter validates constructs against source; ambiguous spans reveal syntax; no guessed inline patches |
| Chunk parsing disagrees with whole Markdown | Critical | Context-aware paging, global definitions and differential tests; source fallback only for affected spans |
| Phase 0 becomes most of the product | High | Small launch/file-integrity boundary first; stage G2-G5 evidence before the dependent feature, not before all coding |
| Native WebView automation assumed identical on both OSes | High | Spike a test-only driver; Linux native route or embedded provider; macOS embedded route/manual native checks; no test server in release |
| Repository attributes execute tools or stage wrong bytes | Critical | No external filters/textconv/hooks; detect unsupported Git attribute/index cases and refuse unsafe mutations |
| PDF/TeX export bypasses execution trust | Critical | Allowlisted intermediate/assets/options; explicit converter action; separate trusted arbitrary-TeX workflow |

## Constraints

### Non-negotiable technical constraints

1. **Stack is fixed:** Rust + Tauri 2 + TypeScript + Svelte 5 + CodeMirror 6. macOS and Linux only. Use native OS WebViews; do not add Electron, Chromium, an HTTP service, or a new editor framework without a documented owner-approved exception.
2. **Small dependency footprint:** use pnpm lockfile, pinned major versions and selective imports; measure bundle tree-shaking, startup allocations and shared-library footprint. Resist adding a comprehensive UI component library. Prefer Svelte components with CSS variables and established accessibility primitives where useful.
3. **No online runtime dependencies:** no CDN, analytics, auto-updater, telemetry, remote fonts, remote render services, crash uploader, npm install at runtime or analytics endpoints. Build tooling may access registries during development; resulting installed app works offline.
4. **No user plugin architecture:** internal parser extensions, Tauri dependency plugins and small private Rust traits are allowed. Do not build user extensions, marketplaces, dynamic module loading or arbitrary JS evaluation.
5. **Filesystem fidelity:** Markdown remains ordinary files. No-op open/save preserves bytes without rewriting. Changed documents use byte provenance to preserve untouched mixed line endings/BOM and source. Detect binary/non-UTF8; show read-only or explicit conversion, not mojibake writes. Define metadata/link policy before writes ship.
6. **Save safety:** scoped handles, expected-base validation, same-directory atomic replacement/flush where supported, private base/recovery snapshots and conflict UI. The safe-save protocol documents residual races with noncooperating writers; no portable atomic hash-and-replace is assumed. Never silently replace a symlink or break hardlinks; never claim that a watcher guarantees no concurrent overwrite.
7. **Unicode:** distinguish UTF-16 (CodeMirror), UTF-8 byte offsets (Rust), Unicode graphemes (user cursor), and line endings. Test Arabic mixed BiDi, Hebrew, CJK IME, emoji ZWJ and combining marks with both WebKit versions.
8. **Web security:** document data stays inert; raw HTML is source-only, render output sanitized, no remote active content or broad shell/filesystem capabilities. Explicit application-command ACLs and Rust validation are required. Same-WebView code execution is a compromise of that WebView, not something per-element capabilities isolate; native execution confirmation is part of defense in depth.
9. **Performance correctness:** do not use artificial file-size limits to declare success. A progressive section or a fallback is honest only when its feature degradation is visible and localized. No unbounded DOM growth or long blocking IPC calls.
10. **Tested behavior, not mock UI:** each phase must end with a real path -> open -> interaction -> save/reopen/diff test, as applicable. No placeholder 'implementation complete' without evidence. Run meaningful tests on both macOS and Linux where available.
11. **Licensing:** use MIT for original code; audit third-party libraries, fonts, icon packs, themes, Mermaid/Pierre assets, code borrowed from Ferrite and exported assets. Maintain NOTICE/attribution and avoid unreviewed copy-paste.
12. **Security before convenience:** shell/SQL and external TeX stay optional; manually requested operations only; known dependency vulnerabilities and security upgrades receive high priority. No writable database trust based merely on syntactic keyword tests.

### Performance and resource budgets

All numbers below are **aspirational engineering targets**, not supported size limits and not claims about existing measurements. Optimize for smooth interaction and memory stability over synthetic maximum throughput. Record hardware, OS/WebKit versions, build profile, document fixture, measured process tree and measurement method. Regressions relative to the current released baseline must be explained.

| Metric | Target / measurement |
|---|---|
| Cold startup to first interactive UI | Aim < 800 ms on modern reference hardware; record both OSes and compare to an empty production Tauri baseline |
| Opening a 1 MB normal Markdown file to useful rendered viewport | Aim < 300 ms after file selection; exclude unrelated workspace indexing from critical path |
| Opening 10 MB to useful rendered viewport | Aim < 1.5 s; rich blocks deferred if needed |
| 100 MB stress reading | First useful rendered section and continuous navigability without allocating full rich DOM or freezing; report actual numbers, not a hard SLA |
| Source input latency | Aim p95 < 16 ms for ordinary editing fixtures; measure while preview/outline are active |
| Read/scroll responsiveness | Aim p95 frame < 16.7 ms and no sustained long tasks > 100 ms during normal reading |
| Idle memory | Measure full process-tree footprint **and** incremental cost above a blank Tauri shell; initially aim to keep incremental cost under ~60 MiB on an ordinary empty workspace, then refine with measured baseline |
| Warm 1 MB document memory | Track per-document incremental memory; set OS-specific absolute targets only after empty-shell measurements; avoid arbitrary unverified < 100 MB claims |
| Memory after close | Document/render caches, workers, image resources and CodeMirror views must release; steady-state retained growth below a measured regression budget |
| CPU at idle | Near-zero activity with no file/index/animation work pending; inspect wakeups and polling |
| Browser bundle | Monitor compressed and unpacked JavaScript chunks; Mermaid/MathJax/Pierre not included in startup chunk; no duplicate highlighting frameworks without justification |
| Saving under concurrent external modification | Refuse detected stale bases; retain recoverable revisions; test documented race/failure boundaries rather than assert impossible portable CAS |
| Source preservation | Byte-identical no-op open/save; localized changed-span diffs for audited Visual actions |

**Measurement:** Linux: record RSS **and PSS** where possible for Rust + WebKitGTK helper processes (`/proc/*/smaps_rollup`, `ps`, `time -v`); macOS: process tree and footprint/physical memory from native profiling tools (Activity Monitor, `vmmap`, Instruments or equivalent). Report peak and steady state. Avoid double-counting shared pages when summarizing PSS; never compare only bundle size to Electron's runtime memory. Benchmark a blank Tauri shell, a loaded reader, a Source editor, a split editor, a diagram-rich file, and a multi-tab session on the same machine.

Distinguish process-cold/warm from filesystem-cache-cold runs; label OS cache state instead of assuming a relaunch is truly cold. Record at least 20 ordinary latency samples with median/p95; stress fixtures may use fewer repetitions if the count is disclosed. Define first interactive UI and first useful text viewport separately from first completed math/diagram. Log input-to-paint latency, not dispatch duration alone; frame budgets assume a named display refresh rate. Warm allocators need not return RSS immediately after close: compare repeated-cycle plateaus after a fixed settling interval. Phase 0 sets actual numerical regression thresholds from baseline; aspirational targets alone cannot block correctness work or count as a performance pass.

### Feasibility gates — evidence due before the dependent feature

| Gate | Required evidence | Due / blocking scope |
|---|---|---|
| G0. WebKitGTK/WKWebView viability | Actual app open/type/scroll/fonts and test harness route; native drag/drop/IME added before those features ship | Phase 0 on primary dev OS; second OS before MVP platform claims |
| G1. State/IPC/fidelity | Normalized UTF-16-to-byte provenance, coherent revisioned edits, ACK/save ordering, no-op/mixed-ending round-trip, no per-keystroke full source copies | Small prototype in 0; full gate in 1 before user editing |
| G2. Source-preserving Visual | Hidden syntax, nested list/table cell/Unicode selection and reversible mode switch yield exact intended patches | Early risk probe in 0; full gate before 5b/5c completion |
| G3. Large-file reader | 1/10/100 MB, bounded DOM/cache, provisional-to-final context, cross-page semantic parity, giant block fallback and navigability | Initial parser-boundary probe in 0; full gate in 2 before claiming paged reading complete |
| G4. Math and Mermaid | Fully local assets/extensions/fonts, injection corpus, macro context, lazy loading and measured admission limits | Phase 2, before math/diagram support ships |
| G5. Git staging | Candidate/fallback renderer, exact hunk index validation under lock, stale-base rejection and attribute/filter restrictions | Phase 6, before index mutations ship; not a reader blocker |
| G6. External file conflict | Atomic-replace watcher invalidation, dirty separation, recoverable base/journal and observed stale-save refusal | Small probe in 0; full gate in 1 before user editing |
| G7. Privileged boundary | Explicit custom-command ACLs, scoped handles, malicious data stays inert, disabled runners rejected directly by Rust | Phase 0/1 baseline; extend before each privileged feature |
| G8. Resource baseline | Whole-process cold/warm/close measurements vs blank shell, named fixtures/hardware and baseline-derived budgets | Phase 0 primary OS; repeat on both before release claims |

Record `Pass / Fail / Not Run` per gate, feature scope and platform. A gate blocks its dependent feature, not independent work. Time-box early probes to a reproducible experiment plus a decision; finishing math, Git or large-range editing is not a bootstrap requirement. On failure, retain diagnostics and make the smallest correction within the locked stack. A failed essential product goal requires a measured owner decision, never a fabricated pass. A second unavailable OS remains Not Run; provisional development may proceed, but cross-platform release completion cannot.

## Current State

**Pre-bootstrap baseline (2026-11-11, historical):** The directory contained only `plan.md`, with no Git repository, code, lockfiles, build scripts, tests or local `AGENTS.md`. Phase 0 preserved that document and added the implementation/evidence described below.

**Phase 0 update (2026-11-12, historical):** Git initialized on `main`, with no commit or remote. The single-crate Tauri/Svelte/TypeScript project, lockfiles, native read grants, bounded preview, lazy scratch editor, isolated probes, checks/CI configuration and local app bundle were added. See [Phase 0 evidence](#phase-0-detailed-evidence-decisions-and-handoff), [decisions](#phase-0-decisions) and [performance measurements](#phase-0-performance-evidence). Production saves were absent at this handoff.

**Phase 1 update (2026-11-12, historical handoff):** Real Source editing, byte-preserving undo/save/reopen, native Save As, external-change comparison, durable recovery and scoped literal settings are integrated. The [Phase 1 gate ledger](#phase-1-completion-and-integrity-gates) records macOS ARM64 verification, resource limits, failure behavior and untested platforms. The reader remains the bounded Phase 0 scaffold. No optional runner, Git mutation, full Author mode or later phase is implemented. Git remains uncommitted, without a remote.

**Phase 2 update (2026-11-12):** Read now uses the context-aware worker index and bounded section window, with focus, outline/find, semantic anchors, explicit image/link grants, local lazy rich renderers and standalone HTML export of a selected saved/working revision. See the [Phase 2 gate ledger](#phase-2-reader-completion-and-gates). Phase 1 byte provenance, saves, recovery and command grants remain intact. At that checkpoint Phase 3 and later work had not started; the tree remained uncommitted without a remote.

**Phase 3 update (2026-11-12):** selected roots, paged list/compact/grid browsing, bounded preview/search, four document tabs, history/bookmarks/session hints, reviewed file operations and native drop-offer authorization are implemented. Fresh native grants remain necessary after restart. See the [Phase 3 gate ledger](#phase-3-verification-and-gates) for safe refusals, primary-platform evidence and Not Run cases. No new Markdown files or commits were created; Phase 4 was pending at that historical handoff.

**Phase 4 update (2026-11-12, historical handoff):** searchable native-validated preferences, four/custom themes, font/typography controls, contextual fuzzy commands, recent palette actions and rebindable window shortcuts are complete for the tested macOS ARM64 scope. Preview preserves the existing CM state/provenance and visible reading paragraph. The [Phase 4 ledger](#phase-4-settings-gui-command-palette-shortcuts-and-theming) records validation, release evidence and platform/input gaps. Phase 5 is not started.

**Phase 5 update (2026-11-13, historical handoff):** Source/Split/Visual, rich source-backed author controls and bounded native range editing pass the macOS ARM64 automated gates. Exact-byte 100 MiB save/recovery and full working Read have real native evidence. See the [Phase 5 ledger](#phase-5-author-experience--separately-accepted-slices) for implementation, admissions, release verification and open cross-platform/physical-input gates. Phase 6 remains pending.

**Phase 6 update (2026-11-13, historical handoff):** Explicit local Git grants, bounded source/history comparisons, Library badges, lock-validated whole-hunk staging/unstaging and backed-up discard pass the tested macOS ARM64 gates. Pierre was measured and rejected; the bounded native-hunk fallback ships. The [Phase 6 ledger](#phase-6-git-workbench-and-pierre-quality-diffs) records evidence and open platform/input/performance limits. Phase 7 is not started.

**Review findings (historical plan review):** v3.1 corrects overlarge bootstrap gates, milestone/phase mismatch, mixed-newline fidelity, insufficient token source ranges, non-incremental/chunk parsing assumptions, save/ACK races, same-WebView security overclaims, document-controlled settings, Git filters, export trust and test/packaging realism. Requirements were pending at that review; current implementation and evidence follow the phase ledgers.

### Review evidence and remaining empirical decisions

The upstream facts below were checked on 2026-11-11. Their application to Marktub is an engineering decision in this plan, not proof that a prototype passed. Recheck APIs against pinned releases during implementation.

| Finding / primary evidence | Consequence for this plan |
|---|---|
| CodeMirror separates text into logical lines and supports a configurable separator: [EditorState source](https://github.com/codemirror/state/blob/main/src/state.ts), [Text source](https://github.com/codemirror/state/blob/main/src/text.ts) | Preserve original terminators in a provenance adapter; do not trust normalized text serialization to preserve mixed bytes |
| markdown-it token maps are line ranges: [Token source](https://github.com/markdown-it/markdown-it/blob/master/src/token.ts) | Exact inline edits require additional verified source ranges |
| CommonMark resolves reference definitions before inline interpretation: [parsing strategy](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#appendix-a-parsing-strategy) | Paging needs document context and whole-parse parity tests |
| Tauri capabilities scope windows/WebViews; registered app commands need explicit permission configuration: [capabilities](https://v2.tauri.app/security/capabilities/) | Configure custom-command ACLs and retain Rust checks; same-DOM injection is not isolated |
| Atomic rename replaces a directory entry: [rename semantics](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/rename.2.html) | Atomic visibility does not supply hash-based compare-and-swap; preserve recovery and disclose the remaining writer race |
| libgit2 writes an in-memory index with an atomic lock, while its transaction API is for references: [index write](https://libgit2.org/docs/reference/main/index/git_index_write.html), [reference transactions](https://libgit2.org/docs/reference/main/transaction/index.html) | G5 must prove a lock-held validation/serialization path; a generic `Index::write` is not sufficient stale-index protection |
| Git attributes control filtering and encoding/EOL conversion: [gitattributes](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes) | Detect these cases before mutation; never start repository-configured tools automatically |
| KaTeX emits HTML/MathML and mutable macro state can persist: [options](https://katex.org/docs/options), [API](https://katex.org/docs/api) | Export static HTML/fonts or use a separate SVG renderer; isolate/cache math context deliberately |
| MathJax has local deployment and safe-content components: [hosting](https://docs.mathjax.org/en/latest/web/hosting.html), [safe component](https://docs.mathjax.org/en/latest/web/components/misc.html#ui-safe) | Bundle extension/font assets and restrict user-input packages/URLs; local top-level JS alone is insufficient |
| Mermaid offers security/text/edge/HTML-label controls: [config schema](https://mermaid.js.org/config/schema-docs/config.html) | Lock app policy, reject document overrides and measure admitted diagram complexity |
| Pandoc's sandbox excludes PDF-engine/filter execution: [manual](https://pandoc.org/MANUAL.html#option--sandbox) | Safe intermediate/assets/options and engine-specific tests remain necessary |
| Actual-app Tauri testing has an embedded-provider route for macOS; direct native-driver support differs: [WebDriver guide](https://v2.tauri.app/develop/tests/webdriver/) | Spike the provider and exclude test servers/evaluators from distribution; native-input checks still require real OS testing |
| SQLite recommends defensive limits/authorizer/interruption: [security guide](https://www.sqlite.org/security.html) | Enforce runner policies in the connection and test cancellation/side effects |

**Decisions requiring experiments, not more prose:** G3 bounded context-correct paging, G2 rich caret/input behavior, G5 index mutation under lock, G0 native automation/input on both OSes, and G8 actual memory/latency. Each has a due phase and a failure path; none is assumed solved. Phase 0 must name reference hardware/OS access and size the next slice. Public signing credentials and optional PDF/TeX installations are external prerequisites, not available resources inferred from this document.

**Prior document:** Marktub plan v2.0 used egui/eframe, native HTML-free rendering, Rust-owned rope, native Mermaid and native diffs. This plan **replaces** those assumptions with a Tauri WebView UI and a lean Rust backend. No feature is implied implemented by the plan revision.

**Source template:** [`MohamedElashri/agentic/templates/plan.md`](https://github.com/MohamedElashri/agentic/blob/main/templates/plan.md). Preserve its tracking conventions: every phase has Deliverables, Acceptance criteria, Status, Completion notes and Deviation notes.

### Decisions recorded (ADR candidates)

| ID | Decision | Why / qualification |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-001 | Tauri 2 + system WebView | Easier rich Markdown, math, Mermaid and diff UI; accepts WebKit runtime and helper-process RAM |
| ADR-002 | Svelte 5 + TypeScript + Vite | Lean compiled UI, no SSR/runtime server, avoid large SPA component library |
| ADR-003 | CodeMirror 6 | Mature virtualized text editor, syntax/IME/undo/transactions, usable live decorations |
| ADR-004 | JS editor owns dirty text; Rust owns saved bytes/provenance journal | One working authority; allow measured immutable/derived copies and serialize save barriers |
| ADR-005 | Visual mode extends CodeMirror | Source fidelity, shared undo, less RAM and complexity than two independent rich editors |
| ADR-006 | markdown-it as display grammar | Line-level maps need an inline range adapter; full parsing is initially worker-based; paging requires parity evidence |
| ADR-007 | KaTeX + lazy MathJax; optional local TeX | Fast normal math and broader advanced coverage without falsely claiming universal TeX |
| ADR-008 | Local lazy Mermaid.js | Better grammar coverage than hand-coded renderer; safeguard SVG and document scripts |
| ADR-009 | Measured native-hunk diff fallback | Phase 6 rejected development-only Pierre after native stalls/timeouts and graph cost; Git mutation stays native and renderer-independent |
| ADR-010 | SQLite-only optional SQL runner | Narrow scope, local-first; read-only default, write confirmation |
| ADR-011 | Git/folders/files are source of truth | Never build a proprietary vault or auto-sync service |
| ADR-012 | Read mode and focus default | Product identity is readable documents, not an IDE |
| ADR-013 | Adaptive paged large-document reader | No arbitrary file cap; avoid O(file size) DOM/layout/IPC for first paint |
| ADR-014 | MIT original code | License third-party assets/dependencies separately, retain notices |
| ADR-015 | Byte provenance below normalized editor text | Preserve BOM/mixed terminators and original pieces through edits, undo and saves |
| ADR-016 | Gates follow feature dependencies | Small bootstrap, usable reader early, independently verified author/workbench/runner slices |
| ADR-017 | One-WebView threat model | Sanitization/CSP + explicit command ACLs/backend grants; no arbitrary-script isolation claim |
| ADR-018 | Portable save guarantees have limits | Atomic replacement + last-moment validation/recovery; disclose noncooperating-writer and filesystem limits |

### Technology candidates and selection rules

| Layer | Initial choice | What must be verified |
|---|---|---|
| Desktop | `tauri` 2 / WRY | macOS WKWebView, Linux WebKitGTK dependency matrix, release packaging |
| UI | Svelte 5, TypeScript, Vite | mount behavior, bundle weight, keyboard/accessibility, no SSR |
| Source/Visual | `@codemirror/*` 6, `@codemirror/lang-markdown` | large-file performance, decorated editing, IME/BiDi, undo, active-block reveal |
| Markdown | `markdown-it` audited compatible release + selective extensions | GFM coverage is tested, not implied by defaults; line maps vs inline ranges, full-parser worker cost, paged context parity |
| Code highlighting | CodeMirror/Lezer source; lazy highlighted reading blocks | no redundant heavy runtime; supported languages |
| Fast math | KaTeX | syntax, SSR-free rendering, fonts locally bundled, sanitization |
| Broad math | MathJax 4 SVG output | local extension/font-data loading, safe package allowlist, macro/numbering context, accessible alternatives and initial lazy cost |
| Diagrams | Mermaid.js local lazy import | securityLevel strict, SVG safety, worker/DOM and WebKit support |
| Diff UI | `@pierre/diffs` vanilla (candidate) | license, CSS/Shadow DOM WebKit, virtualization, memory; fallback if unsuitable |
| Rust Git | `git2`, optional `imara-diff` | index lock/revalidation, attribute semantics and disabled external filters; Git CLI is test oracle, not required for ordinary app use |
| File watcher | `notify` | rename/atomic replace, macOS/Linux behavior, storm coalescing |
| Search/cache | `rusqlite` + FTS5 if justified | incremental indexing, no cache ownership of actual files |
| SQLite execution | `rusqlite` separate connection | read-only flags, authorizer, time/row caps, cancellation |
| Export | HTML renderer adapter; local Pandoc + Typst/TeX | working offline; SVG/math fidelity and file/resource handling |
| Security | explicit Tauri app/plugin permissions + CSP + vetted sanitizer | backend grants, same-WebView limitations, SVG/HTML injection, URI handling and native runner confirmation |

Before adding any dependency: record license, maintained release/version, binary+startup impact, transitive dependencies, and a removal path. Use ordinary internal library modules rather than adding a user-facing plugin system. Do not blindly pin version numbers from documentation; lock actually resolved compatible releases when bootstrapping.

## Commands

The commands below are the **intended developer interface**, not a claim they already run. In Phase 0 install the official Tauri 2 macOS or Linux prerequisites (Linux must have compatible WebKitGTK development/runtime packages) and freeze Node/pnpm/Rust versions. Update commands if the created repository differs, with evidence.

```bash
# Bootstrap once: install a pinned pnpm by the method supported by the pinned Node
# release; do not assume Corepack is bundled. Create package files, then resolve
# the initial lockfile with pnpm install. These files do not exist yet.
# Reproducible setup AFTER lockfiles have been created and committed:
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
cargo --version
```
