```bash
# Dev desktop app (Tauri launches Vite + Rust backend)
pnpm tauri dev

# Production build / runnable install artifacts
pnpm tauri build

# Frontend checks
pnpm typecheck
pnpm lint
pnpm test
pnpm test:e2e      # test-only actual Tauri WebView driver; manual native checks separately
pnpm format:check

# Rust backend checks
cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all -- --check
cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml

# Performance: scripts and target fixture names to implement
pnpm bench:startup
pnpm bench:memory
pnpm bench:open
pnpm bench:scroll
pnpm bench:edit
pnpm bench:git

# Dependency/security audits (add and document pinned tooling)
pnpm audit
cargo audit --file src-tauri/Cargo.lock
```

**Phase 0 command availability:** current runnable commands and required benchmark `--binary` arguments are documented in [README.md](README.md). `bench:scroll`, `bench:edit`, `bench:git` and `cargo audit` are not implemented/installed or claimed as passing in Phase 0. pnpm 12 project settings live in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`; an explicit frozen offline install has been verified.

Provide scripts for fixtures that create: ordinary Markdown, deeply nested syntax, tricky Unicode, GFM tables, many equations, Mermaid-rich source, binary/invalid UTF-8, mixed line endings, pathological long lines, 1/10/100 MB files and a reproducible Git staging repository. `pnpm` frontend scripts may orchestrate Rust tests; ensure CI covers both. Never claim a command passes until it has actually been executed.

Pin Node/pnpm/Rust toolchains and lockfiles together; choose compatible versions at bootstrap, not speculative versions in this document. No root Cargo workspace is assumed: Rust tools receive the backend manifest/lockfile explicitly. Git CLI is required for Git correctness tests, and pinned optional tools are required only for their integration tests. Missing tools/platforms produce Not Run with a reason, not a pass. Audit commands may need registry access in development; runtime offline guarantees are separate.

## Acceptance Criteria

### Release-wide acceptance gates

- [ ] Starts, opens a selected Markdown file into Read focus and remains functional offline with network blocked. It uses the system WebView on macOS/Linux, does not bundle Chromium/Electron, and does not require a runtime web server.
- [ ] Reader is typography-first, scrollable, keyboard-accessible, supports TOC/outline, search, anchors, persistent position, and shows only nearby rendered blocks in the DOM.
- [ ] Within ordinary whole-document editing, all four modes reflect the same unsaved revision; Source, Split and Visual preserve selection/undo in-session without an independent rich-text authority. Large range sessions expose their documented window/undo limits.
- [ ] Unmodified file remains byte-identical across open/close and no-op save. Localized Visual edits affect only expected spans; mixed line endings, Unicode and unknown Markdown survive unchanged outside those spans.
- [ ] CodeMirror source editor supports editing, multi-selection, undo/redo, find/replace, folding, formatting shortcuts, syntax and documented IME/BiDi constraints on **actual WKWebView and WebKitGTK**.
- [ ] WYSIWYG can edit common inline formats, headings, lists/tasks, GFM tables, images (drag/drop, move, resize and alt/path edits), math and Mermaid source popovers; unsupported blocks are preserved, not discarded.
- [ ] GFM/CommonMark extensions and supported TeX/Mermaid are rendered from local bundled assets with visible source-backed errors; no document-triggered script or network execution.
- [ ] Local file browser supports multiple roots, sort/filter, list/compact/grid, quick preview, bulk safe operations, tabs, recent/bookmarked files, quick open, cross-file links and workspace search.
- [ ] Git displays accurate working-tree/index/HEAD comparisons and unified/split/intraline diff; chosen-hunk stage/unstage preserves unrelated index content on supported text cases. Stale/unsupported index, filter and encoding cases are refused visibly, with read-only inspection retained.
- [ ] Agent review is optional and inert when disabled; when enabled it handles external atomic replace, meaningful since-last-read changes, anchored comments, conflict resolution and portable feedback export.
- [ ] Shell and local SQLite execution are off by default and never auto-run; readonly SQLite prevents writes, unauthorized file/database access is rejected, and explicit trusted actions have output/time limits.
- [ ] GUI and command palette cover discoverable settings, local `$option=$value` configuration, per-file settings, sensible defaults, 2+ light and 2+ dark curated themes, custom fonts/themes and readable RTL fallbacks.
- [ ] Built-in standalone HTML export functions offline; PDF via discovered local Pandoc with configured Typst/TeX, with tested math/Mermaid/images/footnotes/code/TOC and error handling if absent.
- [ ] Large document benchmark tiers (1/10/100 MB) can show useful progressively rendered reading without hard size rejection, massive DOM, or uncontrolled persistent memory growth; expensive blocks degrade individually.
- [ ] Production benchmark results cover whole application process tree, cold/warm startup, CPU at idle, startup bundle, viewport DOM count, memory after closing tabs and typed input latency on both macOS and Linux.
- [ ] CSP, sanitization, explicit Tauri app/plugin permissions and Rust grant validation pass malicious-data tests. Unsafe paths/links are denied, default-off commands fail when called directly, and native runner confirmation is exercised. Document the impact of a full main-WebView compromise rather than claiming DOM-subtree isolation.
- [ ] Accessibility smoke tests include keyboard-only navigation, high contrast, focus/selection, ARIA semantics, reduced motion, screen reader exposure and math alternative text where possible.
- [ ] Licenses, portable packages, Linux WebKitGTK requirements, known limitations and third-party notices are documented; CI reports actual passes and untested targets separately.

This is the inherited requirement ledger, not independently verified access to the original conversation. Keep `Status` updated with **Pending / In progress / Pass / Blocked** and link to test evidence (not a checkbox based on code existing). Refinements in this plan define technical limits; deleting a product requirement or claiming an unsupported platform complete still needs an explicit owner decision.

Phase 0 foundations for requirements 1, 3, 6 and 9 have [scoped evidence](#gate-ledger); Phase 1 adds tested integrity/settings foundations for 3, 12 and 14. Those requirements remain In progress. None is marked as a complete cross-platform product feature.

### Definition of done for individual features

A feature passes only when (1) user-visible interaction is implemented, (2) Rust/JS authority and persistence are correct, (3) relevant negative/security/cross-platform cases are tested, (4) memory and latency cost have been measured for heavy features, and (5) documentation records known unsupported behavior. A button that does nothing, a static mockup, or a source file that cannot round-trip is **not** an implementation.

### Operational quality targets / release matrix

- Platforms: macOS ARM64 and x86_64, Linux Wayland and X11; Phase 0 selects explicit minimum OS/WebKitGTK versions, reference Linux distribution/architecture and test access. Both macOS architectures remain targets; a missing test machine is Not Run and blocks claiming that target supported. Broader distro/ARM Linux support is uncommitted until tested.
- Markdown: at least CommonMark core, GFM tables/tasks/autolinks, footnotes, strikethrough, nested quotes/lists, diagrams, math, code fences with recognized languages, image attributes, front matter preservation and unusual whitespace.
- Source preservation: no-op and localized edits over LF, CRLF, mixed line endings, BOM, Unicode, no terminal newline, path spaces/non-ASCII, source-only HTML and unknown directives.
- Git: clean/dirty, staged/unstaged combined changes, one-hunk stage, merge/conflict detection, repository in subfolder, detached HEAD, untracked/binary/LFS pointer and concurrent index write.
- Security: untrusted Markdown with XSS, SVG event handlers, `javascript:` links, Mermaid HTML tags, external URLs, path traversal, symlink escapes, SQL side effects, oversized resource consumption, shell/TeX attempts.
- Resource use: long code fences, deeply nested syntax, one huge line, thousands of headings/footnotes, 10k line diff, many tabs, many images/formulas, giant SVGs, repeated open/close and watch storms.

## Test Plan

Test at the **smallest affected layer first**, followed by integration and full release checks. Never use only jsdom/Chrome for OS WebView behavior. Record exact commands, platform, fixtures, revisions, pass/fail and environment.

1. **Parser/source mapping tests:** CommonMark/GFM golden files, revisioned block identities, inline range adapter, Lezer/display differences, normalized UTF-16/original-byte provenance, undo/redo of mixed terminators, minimal patches and unknown syntax. Compare paged output with whole parsing across randomized boundaries, forward references/footnotes, nested containers, duplicate headings and unterminated/giant blocks; validate finalized output separately from provisional output.
2. **Rust file tests:** coherent snapshots, read-only/invalid encoding, no-op metadata, atomic writes and failure injection (disk full, permission loss, temp/flush/rename failure), ACL/xattr/link policy, journal checkpoint/rotation and crash recovery. Exercise external replace at each save boundary and document the residual noncooperating-writer limitation.
3. **CodeMirror tests:** transactions, undo grouping, source/split/visual transitions, image/table mutation patches, active-block syntax reveal, IME events and decorated selection mapping.
4. **Renderer tests:** SVG/HTML sanitization, strict Mermaid, KaTeX/MathJax corpus, multiline math and broken formulas, lazy loading, local assets and math/diagram render fallbacks.
5. **IPC protocol tests:** base-coordinate changesets, delayed received/durable ACK, idempotent retries, gaps, stale generations, invalid UTF-16 boundaries, streaming/backpressure and session shutdown. Edit N+1 while N saves; save twice; crash before/after durable ACK and base rotation; prove later edits remain dirty and replay restores the acknowledged revision exactly.
6. **Git tests:** compare authoritative repo statuses and index contents with Git CLI; test selected patch exactly, stale hashes, renames, CRLF, binary and concurrent file/index edits.
7. **Security tests:** Tauri capability fail-closed, CSP, no network assets, shell/SQLite disabled at startup, path scoping, SQL readonly authorizer, executable timeouts, TeX/Pandoc command control.
8. **Filesystem manager tests:** list/compact/grid, sort/filter, quick preview, multi-root, workspace search, bulk safe delete/move, invalid symlinks, cancellation and migration/session restore.
9. **Export integration tests:** dirty-vs-saved revision selection, all virtualized sections, no-script standalone HTML, embedded fonts/images, KaTeX HTML vs MathJax SVG, sanitization and finalized context. Real installed-tool PDFs include formulas, diagrams, dimensions, footnotes, anchors, TOC and Unicode; hostile raw TeX/Typst/metadata/paths cannot inject engine code/options or fetch resources.
10. **Actual WKWebView and WebKitGTK tests:** Phase 0 spikes a pinned WebdriverIO Tauri embedded provider or Linux `tauri-driver` route. The direct native-driver route does not cover macOS; the embedded provider is a current candidate requiring test-only Rust plugins. Compile all driver/backend-evaluation features out of distribution builds, bind test servers to loopback only in test builds and verify absence in release artifacts. Browser mocks help unit tests but do not establish app integration. Manually verify native dialogs, OS drag/drop, real IME, screen readers and print flows on each advertised platform; synthetic DOM events alone are insufficient.
11. **Performance/regression:** repeated cold/warm startup, 1/10/100 MB read, 10 MB editing, 10k line diff, thousands of diagrams, 20+ open/close tab cycles; inspect DOM counts, event-loop long tasks, worker activity, system process memory and idling.
12. **Full checks:** pnpm formatting/lint/typecheck/tests, cargo fmt/clippy/tests, dependency audits, packaged app smoke tests, crash/recovery validation and full diff review.

### Phase completion rules

For each phase capture:

```text
Status: Pending | In progress | Blocked | Complete
Completion notes:
  - Commits/files changed:
  - Tests/commands actually run (OS + result):
  - Evidence/fixtures/metrics:
  - Known limitations:
Deviation notes:
  - None | ADR link + reason + user-approved scope exception
```

A phase that has not passed its **functional** criteria is not Complete. If only Linux was tested, report macOS as Not Run, not Passed. If blocked, preserve current work and document the smallest viable correction without silently broadening permissions or relaxing document fidelity.

## Phases

### Dependency order and delivery slices

| Work item | Prerequisites / stopping point |
|---|---|
| 0 bootstrap | Existing plan only; inspect, launch and measure a small vertical slice |
| 1 integrity + minimal Source | 0; G1/G6/G7 must pass before editable files are offered |
| 2 reader + 8a HTML | 1; implement ordinary parsing/rendering first, then separately verify G3/G4 |
| 3 basic browsing | 1 + ordinary-reader slice of 2; MVP adds selected roots, paged tree/list, quick-open and recents; bulk operations/grid/index can follow |
| 4 basic settings | Parser from 1 + reader from 2; theme/font/focus controls and basic palette before Alpha; full editor/theme UX later |
| 5a Source/Split | 1 + ordinary-reader slice of 2; may proceed before remaining large-reader/3/4 work |
| 5b core Visual | 5a + G2; early feasibility probe from 0 is evidence, not production completion |
| 5c rich blocks | 5b + scoped asset/file services from 3; finish before Workbench milestone |
| 6 Git | 1 + 5a for source navigation; status/diffs first, then G5 before any stage/unstage |
| 7 agent review | 1 + 2 + diff adapter from 6 (no dependency on Git mutation) |
| 5d range editing | G3 reader/index + 1 journal/fidelity + 5a; independent of rich Visual, required before complete-core claims |
| 8b PDF | 2 finalized render/export model + 1 scoped assets; may precede runners |
| 8c runners/local TeX | 1 + renewed G7; independent of PDF and always optional/default-off |
| 9 release gates | Start packaging/accessibility/security smoke tests in 0; repeat per milestone, full completion after retained 1.0 scope |
| 10 workflow polish | Existing Phase 0–8 implementation + Phase 9 qualification findings; independent polish does not waive Phase 9 release gates |

Default delivery path: 0 -> 1 -> ordinary-reader/HTML 2 + basic 3 -> 5a + basic 4 -> 5b -> remaining 3/4 + 5c + 6 -> remaining large-reader 2 + 7 + 5d + 8b/8c -> final 9. Owner-authorized Phase 10 refines usability on the implemented core while Phase 9 remains open. A phase with partial milestone delivery stays In progress; track completed slices separately. Early macOS/Linux install smoke tests are mandatory before calling MVP distributable; packaging is not postponed wholesale to Phase 9.

### Phase 0: Orientation, Tauri bootstrap, and architecture-risk spikes

Deliverables:

- Re-inspect the directory; preserve existing work. Establish project metadata, lockfiles/toolchain pins, explicit target OS/WebKitGTK matrix and available test machines. Initialize project version control if still absent, without fabricating earlier history. Use one Tauri WebView and the locked stack.
- Launch a minimal Svelte window with a real native file dialog, scoped read, CodeMirror probe and one sanitized Markdown preview. Exercise writes only on disposable fixtures until Phase 1 integrity gates pass. Do not build a file manager, full settings GUI, runner or Git workbench here.
- Measure blank production Tauri vs minimal app startup/process-tree memory on the primary development OS. Record G0/G8 second-OS results or Not Run and a concrete test-access requirement; add early packaging/CI smoke jobs for both targets where available.
- Bound three architecture experiments to small fixtures: (1) normalized UTF-16 edits + original-byte provenance, save-N/edit-N+1 and a recoverable watcher conflict; (2) CodeMirror hidden-heading/list/table-cell patches with Unicode; (3) why naive chunking fails on forward definitions/open containers, with a chosen Phase 2 parsing strategy. Run 1/10/100 MB range-read/DOM scaffolding measurements without claiming full paged semantics.
- Establish explicit custom-command permissions, backend grants and release CSP before loading document content. Prove an unauthorized path/default-off command fails when invoked directly. Record the same-WebView threat model; no fake `user_clicked` authorization.
- Spike the actual-app automation route and ensure test drivers/evaluation/server features are excluded from normal release builds. Set up targeted fmt/lint/typecheck/unit CI; record native checks that remain manual.
- Record short ADRs, experiment commands/results, baseline measurements and a concrete Phase 1 task breakdown. Leave G4/G5 full renderer/Git experiments assigned to their dependent phases; no requirement to finish all G0-G8 here.

Acceptance criteria:

- A real app launches/opens a scoped fixture offline on the primary OS; G0/G7/G8 evidence and second-platform access status are recorded. No tests/measurements imply production feature completion.
- G1/G2/G3/G6 probes have reproducible findings and explicit next gates. Unknown parser/source-fidelity feasibility is a named risk with a bounded next experiment, not an assertion that the architecture works.
- Dependency/toolchain pins and build/check instructions exist; test automation reaches the actual WebView or its failure/manual fallback is recorded. The release build contains no test listener/evaluator.
- Phase 1 has a reviewable integrity implementation path; production editing/index mutation/command execution remain unavailable until their gates pass.

Status:
- Complete

#### Phase 0 detailed evidence, decisions and handoff

The following records preserve the completed Phase 0 evidence as of 2026-11-12. Subsequent implementation belongs under its own phase. Machine-readable samples remain in `docs/evidence/`; these are evidence data, not separate phase documentation.

#### Phase 0 evidence

Status: Complete for the defined Phase 0 scope on macOS ARM64, 2026-11-12. The locked stack is implemented
as a single Rust crate and one local Tauri WebView. Historical Phase 0 handoff: Phase 1 had not started at completion.

The directory originally contained only `plan.md`; Git has been initialized on
`main` without creating a commit. The prototype provides native file selection,
opaque read-only document grants, bounded source windows, sanitized worker-backed
preview and an on-demand CodeMirror scratch editor. It has no user-file save,
execution, Git mutation, database, remote asset or general filesystem bridge.

##### Environment and platform matrix

Local host: Apple M1 MacBook Pro, 16 GB RAM, macOS 27.2 (26B123), ARM64.
The native WebKit bundle reports 22625.2.8.11.9. The test provider's 605.1.15
user-agent token is not a measurement of the installed WebKit build.

Pinned tools: Rust 1.99.1, Node 26.13.1, pnpm 12.18.0. Node was updated from
26.13.0 on the host during the session; the final pin follows the available patch.
Cargo locks Tauri 2.12.5; pnpm locks Svelte 5.57.3, the Tauri CLI/API 2.12.4,
CodeMirror 6 packages and markdown-it 15.0.3. Both lockfiles are present.

##### Architecture experiments

###### Byte provenance and revision journal

`src-tauri/src/probes.rs` and `probes/journal.rs` compile only for tests. The shared
`tests/fixtures/fidelity.json` is also applied through real CodeMirror transactions.
The fixture includes BOM, LF/CRLF/CR, Arabic, CJK and a non-BMP emoji. Original
byte pieces survive localized edits; half-surrogate and invalid ranges fail.

The disposable journal validates ordered base-coordinate changesets, rejects
revision gaps and conflicting duplicate operation IDs, flushes before ACK and
replays acknowledged rows. An incomplete trailing row is excluded. A save ticket
for N can persist while N+1 remains dirty; external replacement refuses the
fixture save and retains pending bytes plus replayable input.

This is evidence for an approach, not the production save service. The prototype
uses simple scans and a coarse inserted-newline policy. It lacks recovery UI,
generation/session lifecycle, bounded queues/rotation, metadata/link policies,
full undo provenance, directory sync and comprehensive failure injection. Never
expose `persist_fixture` as a user-file IPC command. Atomic rename still is not
a portable compare-and-swap against noncooperating writers.

###### Source-backed formatting

Heading/list decorations use the existing CodeMirror state. Lezer guards keep
Markdown-looking fenced/indented code literal; ambiguous table code spans are
refused. Tests edit headings, nested Unicode lists and escaped-pipe table cells
with localized transactions and undo/redo. The table range helper is not a GUI
table editor. Native input/undo worked in the small fixture; real IME, complete
selection/caret behavior, rich blocks and screen-reader behavior remain G2 gates.

###### Reader bounds and parser strategy

Rust retains authorized read handles and reads UTF-8-aligned ranges. Capturing a
revision currently hashes the whole file twice before returning a grant; this is
O(file size), not a progressive-first-viewport implementation. At 100 MiB the
median grant cost was about 672 ms, while the subsequent range read was about
0.077 ms. These Rust timings exclude IPC, worker parsing, DOM and paint.

The worker limits mounted output to 32 top-level blocks and applies additional
token/line budgets with a localized source excerpt fallback. A full document is
not mounted invisibly. Queued worker work is not yet a production backpressure/cancellation design. The scaffold has explicit range navigation, not final
scroll virtualization, a complete outline or app-wide document find.

The parser tests demonstrate that naive chunks disagree with whole parsing for
forward reference definitions and open fences. Phase 2 will first use correct
whole-input worker parsing for ordinary files. The large-reader experiment must
carry block context and document-wide definitions, then compare finalized paged
output against whole parsing. The low-level Rust index supplies ranges, not a
second canonical grammar. Failure blocks the large-reader claim, not independent
ordinary-reader work. Full G3 has not passed.

###### Filesystem and privilege boundary

The real app has no frontend-path read command or write/runner command. Tests
reject guessed/path-shaped handles, filesystem-plugin reads and absent shell/SQL
commands through actual IPC. HTML/SVG injection and resource-bearing output are
checked at the sanitized renderer sink. See [the Phase 0 security boundary](#phase-0-security-boundary).

The native watcher detects atomic replacement. Read access notifications are
ignored; watcher availability is disclosed and focus revalidates content hashes.
Scratch state is not replaced by disk events. Stronger coherent snapshots,
scoped handle-relative I/O and production conflict choices remain Phase 1 work.

##### Concrete Phase 1 handoff

1. Define versioned session descriptors and ordered UTF-16 changesets, generation
   tokens, idempotency, received/durable ACKs and save barriers in the typed bridge.
   Keep CodeMirror as the working authority; do not mirror every dirty buffer.
2. Replace probe scans with a tested original-byte/provenance model covering BOM,
   local/dominant inserted terminators, undo/redo and saved-base rebasing. Capture
   immutable saved bases so in-place writers cannot alter a session snapshot.
3. Implement private recovery storage, bounded/coalesced journal writes, backpressure,
   checkpoint/rotation, restore/discard UI, quotas and crash/failure diagnostics.
4. Implement serialized explicit save/Save As with native grants, late disk-base
   validation, same-directory temp/flush/replace, metadata/link policies and a
   recoverable failure path. Save N must leave N+1 dirty. No generic write bridge.
5. Add hash-validated watcher coalescing and Compare/Reload/Keep Local/Save As for
   conflicts. Automatic merge is not a prerequisite. Never silently overwrite a
   detected new disk revision or evict unacknowledged human input.
6. Specify and implement the literal `$key=value` settings grammar and trust-safe
   scope precedence. Settings changes cannot authorize execution or rewrite Markdown.
7. Pass G1/G6/G7 with disk-full/permission/crash/symlink/hardlink/newline/Unicode and
   save-order failure tests before offering production editing.

Historical Phase 0 handoff: Phase 1 had not started at completion. Git, shell/SQL/local TeX, PDF, rich Visual blocks and full
large-file editing remain outside this handoff's implementation scope.

The restart interrupted an app bundle and exposed an ineffective pnpm 12 `.npmrc`
configuration. The store/engine/exact-save settings now live in the supported
workspace configuration; an explicit frozen offline install verified it. Final
bundle verification checks the example resource and a sealed ad-hoc signature.

#### Phase 0 decisions

These implement the existing plan; they do not replace its stack or complete
future product gates. Recorded 2026-11-12.

##### P0-001: One reader-first desktop window

Keep Tauri 2/system WebView, Svelte 5/TypeScript and CodeMirror 6. The source
editor and Markdown language code load only when requested. Use OS fonts and
locally bundled assets. No application server, second editor model or UI kit.
The UI direction and tokens are in [the Phase 0 design direction](#phase-0-design-direction).

##### P0-002: Native grants, not frontend paths

Rust opens a native picker and returns an unpredictable document handle. The
frontend can read only bounded ranges of authorized handles. The packaged
example is another explicit app-owned read grant. There is no JS filesystem
plugin permission, path-to-handle command, save command or registered runner.
Custom-command permissions are declared through the Tauri app manifest and a
single explicit main-window capability. Canonicalization authorizes the selected
file target; it does not grant its containing directory.

##### P0-003: Scratch-only input, persistence experiments isolated

The same CodeMirror instance survives Read/Scratch/live-formatting switches.
Decorations reveal active heading/list markers; Lezer checks prevent changing
the appearance of Markdown-looking code-fence contents. Table-cell patches,
byte provenance, ordered journal batches, durable ACK/replay and save tickets
are tested on disposable fixtures. They are not a production editing service.
Do not promote them without Phase 1 offset, undo, checkpoint and save-safety work.

##### P0-004: Correct ordinary parsing before paged semantics

The bootstrap reads at most 64 KiB per range, parses in a worker and mounts at
most 32 top-level blocks with additional token/line budgets. This is scaffolding,
not a full virtualized reader. Chunk tests reproduce forward-reference and open
fence failures. Phase 2 starts with whole-input worker parsing for ordinary files;
large reading needs a context-aware/checkpointed adapter and document-wide
definitions, followed by differential tests against whole parsing. A Rust line
scanner alone cannot supply canonical Markdown blocks. G3 remains open.

##### P0-005: Actual-app tests are a separate build

Use WebdriverIO's embedded Tauri provider in a debug-only `e2e` configuration.
Its Rust plugins, frontend evaluator and grants are absent from normal builds;
the build script rejects the feature in release profiles. The server binds only
to loopback, as verified in the installed provider source. A targeted override
aligns the provider's pinned `@wdio/globals` dependency to the tested 9.33.1 peer
set; no mismatched peers remain. Native OS interactions still need manual checks.

##### P0-006: Attribute macOS XPC helpers explicitly

WebKit helpers have launchd as parent, so Rust-PID/descendant RSS is insufficient.
The measurement script records new WebKit PIDs during a controlled launch,
includes their RSS/physical footprint and checks that they exit with that app.
Ambiguous attribution is marked instead of reporting a falsely small total.
Physical footprint and summed RSS remain distinct metrics. Short runs are
baselines, not evidence of leak freedom or final resource budgets.

##### P0-007: Local packaging and pinned tooling

Use an ad-hoc macOS bundle signature (`signingIdentity: "-"`) for local checks,
following [Tauri's signing documentation](https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/sign/macos/#ad-hoc-signing).
Developer ID/notarization remains a later credentialed release step. CI targets
macOS ARM64/Intel and Ubuntu 24.04, with actions pinned to resolved commit hashes.
Do not call an unexecuted workflow a platform pass.

pnpm 12 moved non-auth configuration to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`; the initial
`.npmrc` settings were ineffective. The corrected workspace pins the local
store, exact-save/engine rules and explicit-install behavior. See the
[pnpm settings reference](https://pnpm.io/settings).

#### Phase 0 design direction

A reading desk, with one document centered under a quiet native-window toolbar.
Left alignment, generous paragraph rhythm, no library chrome until it exists.
The distinctive element is the document itself, not a dashboard or a hero banner.

Tokens: paper `#fafbf9`, ink `#24352f`, quiet `#66736d`, green `#316c57`,
line `#dce3df`, surface `#f0f4f1`. Dark values follow the same subdued green-gray
hierarchy. System UI for controls, Palatino/Georgia/serif for reading, OS monospace
for source. No downloaded fonts, gradients, cards, decorative animation or stats.

The review against the brief removed permanent split panes: Read is the default;
the source probe appears only on request. Controls have descriptive names and
visible focus. Scratch status explains unsaved behavior; backend implementation
details belong in the evidence report rather than the reading surface.

```text
Marktub       document name                 Read / Scratch       Open file
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
                     readable document column
                     headings / paragraphs / local example
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
Read only / scratch status                 source range navigation
```

#### Phase 0 security boundary

The installed UI is local code; Markdown is untrusted data in that same WebView.
There is no DOM-subtree privilege isolation. If arbitrary script executes in the
main WebView, it can invoke that window's granted commands. Sanitization, CSP and
Rust validation reduce the attack surface; they are not an OS sandbox.

- Only native file selection or the packaged example creates a document grant.
  IDs are UUIDs. Reads accept handles, byte offsets and a maximum 64 KiB budget,
  never arbitrary paths. Revoked/unknown handles fail in Rust.
- Custom app commands have explicit generated permissions. Only the `main`
  capability is enabled. Dialogs are invoked from Rust; no broad frontend
  filesystem/dialog/shell permission is granted. The asset protocol is disabled.
- Raw HTML is escaped. A worker produces bounded renderer output; the one HTML
  sink sanitizes it with DOMPurify. Resource-bearing tags/attributes, links,
  event handlers, styles, IDs and names are removed. Document images become
  text placeholders and document hyperlinks are inert during Phase 0.
- Release CSP permits only local app assets and necessary IPC. No external
  script, font, frame or connection origin is allowed. Development adds only
  the fixed loopback Vite origin. Inline styles support CodeMirror; arbitrary
  document CSS is never accepted. No telemetry or updater is installed.
- No production save, shell, SQL, local TeX, Pandoc or Git mutation command is
  registered. An execution-status query returns false; unknown runner calls
  are rejected via actual IPC tests.
- File watchers signal invalidation. They do not overwrite scratch state.
  Content hashes revalidate on focus; unavailable watches are disclosed.
  In-place metadata changes refuse further reads. An open file descriptor is
  not an immutable snapshot against hostile in-place writers; Phase 1 must
  implement coherent saved-base snapshots and stronger scoped I/O.
- Source-window parsing, image blocking and token budgets are admission controls,
  not hard process resource isolation. Worker cancellation and renderer parity
  need further work before large-file reading is a supported product feature.

The optional test build has a privileged WebDriver evaluator on loopback. It is
explicitly compiled/configured only for e2e tests; release builds reject its Cargo
feature. `pnpm check:release` verifies the normal dependency graph, capabilities
and emitted frontend chunks. Test artifacts must not be distributed.

Runtime JavaScript advisory checking reported zero known vulnerabilities for the
locked graph at the recorded check. This is not a universal security guarantee.
`cargo audit` was unavailable locally and has not been claimed as passed. Full
Rust advisory/distribution audits remain outstanding. Schema, persistence,
export, execution and Git checks expand with their phases.

#### Phase 0 performance evidence

The reference host is an Apple M1 MacBook Pro with 16 GB RAM and macOS 27.2.
Measurements use production builds except the explicitly labeled WebView smoke
test. They are baseline experiments, not release SLAs or claims of maximum file
size. [Raw recorded samples](artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-0-macos-arm64.json) include fixture,
runtime and method distinctions.

##### What is measured

- Startup is process launch to the native WebView page-load event, measured for
  20 new processes. It is not input-ready paint, a loaded-document benchmark or
  a filesystem-cache-cold run. Report median and nearest-rank p95, with filesystem
  caches uncontrolled/warm. Do not compare these numbers with an interactive SLA.
- macOS memory includes Rust plus newly created WebKit WebContent, GPU and
  Networking processes, even when launchd is their parent. The script verifies
  helper exit with the measured app. Both summed RSS and native physical
  footprint are recorded; they are not equivalent and shared pages can make
  summed RSS misleading. Unconfirmed attribution invalidates a whole-app claim.
- Native source-range timing distinguishes opening/capturing a grant from
  reading the first 64 KiB. Grant capture currently hashes the entire file twice.
  Neither timing includes IPC, Markdown parsing, DOM insertion or paint.
- DOM scaffolding takes the first UTF-8-aligned 64 KiB from actual generated
  1/10/100 MiB files and renders under JSDOM. Each produced 52 elements and 32
  blocks. This proves the scaffold does not scale its mounted output with file
  size; it is not a native WebKit layout/scroll measurement.
- The final actual WKWebView example smoke recorded 69 DOM elements. Native manual
  checks additionally opened a 100 MiB file and navigated ranges. They do not
  establish a complete 100 MiB rich reader or paged parser correctness.

The native range sample medians were 11.582 / 67.383 / 671.967 ms for grants at
1 / 10 / 100 MiB, followed by 0.076 / 0.071 / 0.077 ms for range reads. Five
samples per tier are retained; caches were warm/uncontrolled. Do not advertise
the sub-millisecond range-read number as file-open or first-viewport latency.

##### Recorded local baseline

Three interleaved trials, each including the Rust process and three lifecycle-verified WebKit helpers, settled for three seconds. Values below are medians in MiB.

| Build     | Summed RSS | Physical footprint |
| --------- | ---------: | -----------------: |
| blank     |     183.22 |              69.88 |
| app-empty |     200.16 |              80.85 |

Median paired app-minus-blank deltas: **17.44 MiB RSS** and **10.97 MiB physical footprint**. These empty-window samples do not establish loaded-reader/editor memory.

| Build | Page-load median (ms) | Page-load p95 (ms) | Runs |
| ----- | --------------------: | -----------------: | ---: |
| blank |                431.66 |             549.76 |   20 |
| app   |                415.90 |             463.70 |   20 |

The small timing difference is not an optimization claim: cache state, launch ordering and ambient OS work remain uncontrolled. Binary SHA-256 hashes and individual samples are retained in the evidence JSON.

##### Provisional budgets and unresolved costs

Preserve the 64 KiB source-window / 32-block scaffold and track DOM/token limits.
The source editor remains a lazy chunk, currently about 514 kB minified before
gzip; it is not part of reader startup. The initial UI chunks total about 76 kB
before gzip, with a separate roughly 101 kB Markdown worker. The large lazy
editor warning is retained as a measured cost, not hidden by raising Vite's
warning threshold. No math, Mermaid or diff runtime is imported yet.

Use the empty Tauri shell as a baseline, not a promised 60 MiB absolute app cap.
Keep the plan's initial incremental empty-workspace RSS target of roughly
60 MiB above baseline as a warning budget on this host; establish a hard
regression threshold only after repeated equivalent runs. Physical-footprint
samples vary with OS pressure/caching; a negative sampled delta is not evidence
that a loaded app improves on an empty shell. No leak/idle-wakeup/frame/input
latency gate is passed by these short runs.

Full-document parsing context, many rich blocks, coherent snapshot capture,
typing during preview, multi-tab eviction, memory after close, CPU wakeups and
both Linux display stacks still require measurements in their phases. The
probe UTF-16 mapping/journal implementation is correctness scaffolding, not a
large-document performance design. Retain negative results and baseline data
before changing any of these budgets.

##### Reproduce

See the commands in [README.md](README.md). Run measurements after builds
finish. `bench:baseline` replaces the normal build output with an empty frontend,
copies its bundle to `artifacts/baseline`, and requires rebuilding normally
afterward. Native inspection permissions are necessary for `ps`/`footprint`.
Artifacts contain process IDs and raw local paths; the repository's evidence
summary retains only the needed measurements and process basenames.

### Phase 1: Rust document services, file integrity, and settings format

Deliverables:

- Rust commands for open/save/reopen, original byte snapshots, source hashes, line-ending/BOM preservation, path identity, symlink/metadata policies, backup and crash recovery, expected-base validation and atomic writes where safe.
- Typed IPC (`OpenDocument`, `DocumentDescriptor`, `ApplyEdits`, `SaveDocument`, `FileChanged`, `Conflict`) with revision checks, error codes, cancellation and no duplicate full-source buffer on each edit.
- Watching open files and their parent directories, rename/atomic-replacement handling, self-write suppression, debounce, delete/move recovery and merge/compare paths.
- `$option=$value` parser/serializer, defaults, scope precedence, validation/errors, atomic persistence, migration, app-managed per-file setting records and optional sidecar export; no user-facing YAML/TOML requirement.
- A minimal real Source open/edit/save/reopen interaction using CodeMirror; byte provenance, normalized-offset changesets, revision barriers and undoable local edits. This establishes integrity before the full Source/Split experience in Phase 5a.
- Durable received-vs-flushed ACK journal, checkpoint/rotation, restore/discard UI, retention and storage quotas. Test disk-full and IPC failure without clearing dirty state; recovery promises cover durably acknowledged input only.
- Safe-save protocol with captured base snapshots, save-N/edit-N+1 handling, explicit symlink/hardlink/ACL/xattr policies, no-op Save without replacement and honest noncooperating-writer limits. Compare/Reload/Keep Local/Save As first; automatic merge is not a prerequisite.
- Separate disposable library-index state from durable bookmarks, reading position, per-file overrides and review records. Use SQLite metadata only if justified; define schema versions, transactional migrations, backup/reset and content-snapshot retention before persistence expands.
- Backend grants for selected files/roots (separate from execution trust), explicit app/plugin permissions, CSP, path-escape denial tests and a safe custom asset URL strategy. These checks do not imply an OS sandbox.

Acceptance criteria:

- Byte-identical no-op saves and localized edits preserve untouched byte pieces, mixed terminators/BOM through undo/redo and save-base rotation. Detected stale disk revisions refuse a normal save; failure/race tests preserve captured base and pending edits with the residual race documented.
- Rust/JS revision journal tests pass including delayed/duplicate/out-of-order messages and Unicode offsets.
- Invalid settings lines fail visibly without partial application; settings syntax is never interpreted as commands.
- G1/G6/G7 pass for the implemented slice. Malicious paths/symlink swaps fail scoped checks; default-off execution rejects direct IPC calls. Native trust policy and the limits of main-WebView compromise are documented.

Status:
- Complete for the tested macOS ARM64 scope, 2026-11-12; other platforms remain Not Run as recorded below.

Implementation breakdown (reviewed against the Phase 0 handoff, 2026-11-12):
- **1a — integrity foundations (implemented):** immutable read snapshots and handle-relative revalidation; reusable indexed byte provenance with exact undo tokens; versioned ordered edits and received/durable journal ACKs; literal settings validation. Production saves are now integrated with the completed gates below.
- **1b — persistence and recovery (implemented):** private storage lifecycle, checkpoint/rotation/quotas, restore/discard UI and flush/backpressure scheduling; same-directory safe saves, metadata/link policy and save-N barriers.
- **1c — Source integration and conflicts (implemented):** typed IPC/CodeMirror adapter, minimal real save/reopen, native Save As, compare/reload/keep-local choices, hash-coalesced watchers and app-managed settings persistence.
- **1d — acceptance (complete for primary OS):** targeted failure injection plus actual WebView tests, normal bundle verification, platform results and G1/G6/G7 evidence. Other platforms remain explicitly unverified.

#### Phase 1 integrity foundation decisions (2026-11-12)

- **P1-001 — phase records:** the owner requires no new Markdown files. The five Phase 0 narrative documents were consolidated under Phase 0 here, preserving its decisions, measurements, failures, platform limits and handoff. `README.md`/`AGENTS.md` remain existing entry points; fixtures and JSON evidence remain separate non-narrative data.
- **P1-002 — immutable read bases:** native selection authorizes the canonical target for reading. Pin its parent directory, open only the single filename with `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW`, reject a changed parent identity, and copy into an exclusive mode-0600 temporary file that is immediately unlinked. Copy/hash with bounded buffers; compare device/inode/length/mtime/ctime before/after and re-open/re-hash through the directory handle. External writes cannot mutate a completed snapshot. This costs a full copy plus two source scans and temporary disk space; it is not a progressive open or a transaction against a malicious concurrent writer. Revalidation of the originally selected symlink chain before **writes** remains required in 1b; read grants retain the originally resolved target.
- **P1-003 — byte provenance:** immutable byte sources and sparse UTF-16/scalar checkpoints map edits without serializing the entire working document per keystroke. Keep exactly one leading BOM outside logical text; an interior BOM is text. New line breaks use the surrounding line terminator, otherwise original dominant style (ties LF, CRLF, CR). Exact deleted-piece tokens produce inverse edits, including combined adjacent changes; replacing identical logical text retains original bytes. Save inputs freeze piece references at revision N without freezing later input. Piece lookup is currently linear in piece count; long-session compaction/measurement remains 1b work, not a performance claim.
- **P1-004 — recovery format v1:** app-private native storage only, UUID session directories (0700), base/journal files (0600), complete JSONL frames with chained SHA-256 checksums and a header bound to the base hash/generation. Validate schema, contiguous revisions, operation payload identity, sorted base-coordinate edits and surrogate boundaries before committing. Append ACK means received; successful sync advances the durable watermark. A partial append poisons further writes until recovery; a sync failure preserves received edits and permits a flush retry. Recovery verifies complete frames and truncates only an incomplete trailing frame. Checksums detect accidental/tampered content but do not authenticate against another process with the same OS-user authority. An exclusive nonblocking advisory lock on each private journal prevents a second application instance from replaying/truncating a live session; this is unrelated to the residual race against external user-file writers. Drop never discards recovery. Schema versions cover EOL/edit/token semantics, not merely JSON shape; changing them requires compatibility tests or migration. Initial **core admission budgets**, not supported editor-size claims: base 32 MiB, log 16 MiB, 1 MiB inserted UTF-8/batch, 1024 changes/batch, 50,000 operations and 100,000 active pieces/restore tokens and 200,000 aggregate retained undo fragments (small restore messages must not bypass the memory bound). Global quotas/retention, checkpoint/rotation, scheduling, cancellation, explicit discard and UI are not yet implemented; no app recovery promise is exposed.
- **P1-005 — literal settings v1:** UTF-8 with one optional BOM, LF/CRLF, blank/full-line `#` comments, `$` plus dotted ASCII key (`[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]*` per segment), optional horizontal whitespace around `=`, boolean, decimal `-?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)(\.[0-9]+)?`, bare enum or double-quoted string. Quoted escapes are only `\"`, `\\`, `\n`, `\t`. No exponents, inline comments, duplicate/unknown keys, evaluation or interpolation. Validate all lines before replacing last-good parsed settings; report line numbers without private values. Current keys/defaults: `schema.version=1`, `reader.width=760` (400–1600), `reader.font_size=18` (12–40), `reader.font="Palatino"` (1–128 UTF-8 bytes, no control characters), `theme=system` (system/light/dark), `editor.tab_width=4` (1–8), `editor.wrap=true`, `recovery.flush_ms=500` (100–1000); all numeric settings are integers. Resolution is defaults → global → workspace → file → session. The closed allowlist contains no execution/trust/file-authority keys. Unsupported schema versions are rejected; persistence, migration, per-file records and sidecar export remain 1b/1c work.
- **Dependency record:** direct `libc 0.2` resolves to already-locked 0.2.191 (MIT OR Apache-2.0); used only for Unix directory-relative/no-follow opens and ownership checks. It already belonged to the runtime graph, adds no new resolved package or frontend/startup import, and is removable with a vetted capability-filesystem replacement. No new Tauri capability or generic path command was added. Blank-baseline builds exclude the integrity services.

Foundation deviation notes (historical, resolved by the integration below):
- Phase 1 was intentionally In progress after the foundational slice; 1b/1c/1d remain required. Production user-file saves remain unavailable until the integrity/metadata/recovery/UI gates pass. This follows the existing iterative-slice rule; it does not reduce Phase 1 acceptance criteria.
- The foundation identified a fidelity edge case: deleting text between a lone CR and LF would concatenate them into CRLF, changing two logical breaks into one on re-open. The foundation refused it with `NewlineJoin`. This is resolved by P1-006 below; native undo-token integration and saved-revision rotation are now verified.

#### Phase 1 completion and integrity gates

Current implementation (supersedes the foundation’s pending items):

- **P1-006 — exact Source edits and undo:** `SourceEditor.svelte` keeps one CodeMirror state across Read/Source/live-formatting switches. Its transaction adapter sends ordered, versioned UTF-16 changesets, not full-buffer keystroke snapshots. The initial text is released from Svelte state after mount. In-session undo/redo uses CodeMirror inverse changes plus Rust-issued deleted-piece tokens; journal rotation and Save/Save As retain those references. Undo retains the last 1,000 transactions, with explicit history-clear/retry when provenance limits are reached; recovery across restart restores text, not frontend undo. Pending DOM input is settled before Save/leave/close decisions. At a new lone-CR/LF adjacency, a bridging LF in the changed span preserves both logical line breaks; the boundary’s old byte pieces are included in the inverse. This is a localized encoding repair, not whole-document normalization. Mixed-EOL/BOM inverse, redo, checkpoint and saved-base tests verify the byte result.
- **P1-007 — save and conflict protocol:** source changes flush through revision N before native commit. Outbound N+1 stays queued while CodeMirror continues accepting input; the N receipt cannot clear N+1’s dirty state. A selected target’s canonical parent is pinned, filenames open relative to that FD with no-follow checks, selected symlink chains revalidate before writes, and expected identity plus hash revalidate after the same-directory temp is written/synced. No-op Save does not replace the selected file or change its inode/mode/mtime. Native Save As publishes a new file exclusively (`linkat`), never replaces an existing file/symlink, and uses owner-only permissions. Failed Save As retains the original editing target even if a copy reached disk before a recovery-record failure. After a fully acknowledged Save As, failure to refresh its reader snapshot reports a warning without reversing the successful save or pointing the editor back at the old target. A detected disk change refuses normal Save and captures a private disk revision for Compare. Compare shows bounded escaped base/local/disk excerpts; Reload, Keep Local and Save As have real behavior. Keep Local never authorizes overwrite. Deleted/moved targets keep the open text and recovery; reselect or Save As rather than silently following a new path. File watches are hints, UI invalidations coalesce for 150 ms, focus/pre-save hash checks validate content, and self-write suppression compares resulting hashes rather than using an ignore-time window.
- **Metadata policy:** preserve ordinary mode, UID/GID, macOS ACL/xattrs via native `fcopyfile`, and Linux xattrs (including POSIX ACL attributes where supported). Permission/ownership/attribute-copy errors refuse replacement. Read-only files, hardlinks, foreign ownership and special permission bits require Save As; no unsafe in-place fallback or implicit hardlink break exists. App-owned resources/private storage cannot become write targets, even through canonical aliases. macOS ACL/xattr preservation is tested; Linux handling is implemented but Not Run on this host. New Save As copies intentionally have new identity/metadata. Network/FUSE/cloud semantics remain best-effort, without local-filesystem support claims.
- **P1-008 — durable recovery and failure states:** private `private-v1/recovery` storage uses mode-0700 directories, mode-0600 files, a store lease and exclusive per-journal locks. A hashed original base plus chained JSONL changes recover through complete frames; a torn tail truncates only after validation. Checkpoints deduplicate byte sources, preserve live undo tokens and idempotency receipts, publish a new locked log header atomically, and retain the old valid representation until publication. The backend repairs a failed partial append before retrying it. Received and durable watermarks are separate; the queue starts sending at 500 ms and targets a durable flush each second during continuous input, with backpressure and visible failures. File sync uses `sync_all` plus macOS `F_FULLFSYNC`; directories sync at publication boundaries. This describes successful OS calls, not a machine-power-loss or hardware certification.
- Before a user-file commit, a durable **pending-commit marker** prevents an interrupted or uncertain save from later being mistaken for a clean recovery. After commit, immutable saved blobs and the new marker sync before the saved receipt. The original base and current plus two previous saved blobs remain available; pending or dirty records never auto-expire. Clean archives expire after seven days or explicit discard; the UI can include saved archives for restore/discard. Restore creates a fresh transport generation and restores text with **no automatically reauthorized filesystem path**; Save As chooses a new native destination. Failed append/sync/IPC leaves text open; retry is idempotent. An explicit Save As can materialize validated pending deltas without requiring a failed recovery write to succeed, and reports a rescue copy without clearing the open dirty state. Native close/quit is intercepted; leave/close waits for a durable barrier or requires explicit discard, and cancellation keeps editing.
- **Resource limits:** private-store logical quota 512 MiB; 32 MiB initial ordinary Source base and normalized-text budget; 64 MiB + 64 KiB retained-source/materialized-byte ceiling; 128 MiB checkpoint serialization ceiling; 16 MiB active log; 1 MiB inserted UTF-8 per changeset, at most 1,024 changes; 2 MiB queued edit payload; 64 operations per normal IPC batch; 50,000 operation receipts; 100,000 active pieces/restore tokens; 200,000 aggregate retained undo fragments; eight native editing grants. The backend also bounds replicated restore-token output. Checkpoint admission includes existing storage. These Phase 1 admission values are historical; Phase 5 P5-011 records the current ordinary/range thresholds and retained-source ceilings. Capacity errors preserve the buffer, offer retry/history reset or Save As, and never evict unsaved text. These are admission controls, not promises that every input below a byte threshold is fast. Larger files remain readable through captured ranges; range editing belongs to Phase 5d.
- **P1-009 — configuration and app records:** the literal grammar/defaults from P1-005 now run in the actual settings dialog, import and new-file sidecar export. Resolution is defaults → global → app-managed workspace → app-managed file → temporary session. Invalid text returns all line diagnostics without applying or persisting any part. Native canonical-path hashes identify file/workspace records; they grant no directory read or execution authority. `metadata.json` schema 1 stores literal settings strings and byte/caret reading positions, with an atomic last-good backup; the strict legacy schema-0 settings shape upgrades on the first successful mutation. Unknown future schemas remain read-only until explicit reset, which preserves rejected original data. Disposable `cache/format` migration/reset never removes metadata or recovery. No SQLite database or generic SQL API was added; bookmarks/review UI remain in their own later phases and must extend this versioned durable-record area rather than the cache.
- **P1-010 — command/asset boundary:** main-window permissions enumerate every edit/save/recovery/settings operation. Backend document/session grants and fresh transport generations reject guessed handles, traversal, stale messages and symlink/root substitutions independently of UI checks. Native dialogs are the only way to select user-file/new-export paths. File-parent FDs authorize the selected filename, not a browsable directory; library-wide root selection/browsing remains Phase 3. Document asset URLs remain disabled and document images/links inert: deny-all is the current safe asset policy, with opaque scoped assets required before later enablement. Markdown stays sanitized data in the same WebView; this is neither an OS sandbox nor DOM-subtree isolation. Execution stays absent/default-off.

Gate ledger for the implemented ordinary-document slice on macOS ARM64:

| Gate | Result | Evidence and limits |
|---|---|---|
| G1 file integrity | Pass | Actual native file save/reopen, exact BOM/mixed-EOL undo/redo across saved revisions, localized edits, no-op inode/mode/time checks, Unicode offsets, checkpoint token roundtrips, symlink/parent/hardlink/readonly policies and metadata preservation. The explicit late-writer test also demonstrates that validation + rename is **not CAS**: a noncooperating writer in the final validation/rename window can still be overwritten. Original base and pending edits remain recoverable; no universal overwrite-prevention claim. |
| G6 recovery/conflicts | Pass | Ordered/retried/generation-bound edits, separate ACK watermarks, injected short-write/sync/storage-full and lost-IPC replies, real process kill after durable input/checkpoint/rotation, pending-commit failure followed by undo to original text, immutable saved snapshots, native conflict/Compare/Save As/restore/discard and close-cancel flows. Physical full-drive, kernel/power-loss and every filesystem fault remain untested; the tests inject OS error outcomes on disposable fixtures. |
| G7 privilege boundary | Pass | Actual WKWebView IPC denies path-shaped read/edit/save/recovery handles, traversal, filesystem plugin reads and absent shell/SQL. Canonical private/resource protection and selected-target no-follow tests pass. Config trust/execution keys are rejected. Normal-release isolation is checked separately below. This is the current slice’s boundary, not permission for future runners/assets/Git. |

Native watch/process/ACL checks require OS access outside the restricted tool sandbox. Tests also verify that failed Save As never silently adopts another editing target, and that private-store aliases cannot become write targets.
- Actual app verification: nine WKWebView tests cover Rust/worker reading, lazy single-editor modes, direct IPC denials, Source save/reopen/undo, N+1 typing during N save, conflicts/Compare/Save As, durable restore and config rejection, 1/10 MiB Source measurements and native close/cancel with pending text. File/save pickers use a deterministic **test-only native fixture**, so this is real WebView/IPC/filesystem coverage, not certification of every OS dialog. The close test issues a real Tauri window close request.
