- Normal `pnpm tauri build --bundles app` succeeds. `pnpm check:release` verifies no test evaluator in normal frontend chunks and no test grants/plugins in the main capability/normal Rust graph. `codesign --verify --deep --strict src-tauri/target/release/bundle/macos/Marktub.app` and the bundled-example byte comparison pass. The executable is 8,128,064 bytes (7.75 MiB), SHA-256 `d27a7a3e3882b34eddcb554cfc5fd6a24052142346f57f6cf03060339e855f6d`. The app is ad-hoc signed, not notarized. The existing lazy-editor warning remains: 515.33 kB minified, with the 500 kB warning threshold unchanged. No new runtime dependency was added beyond the foundation’s already-locked direct `libc` use.
- Normal packaged-executable launch smoke passes: the app remains alive after three seconds and all attributable WebKit helpers exit after probe termination. This checks the production process lifecycle; interactive assertions above use the actual debug e2e WebView. Local file links in `plan.md`, `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` resolve. All phase narrative stays here; only sanitized JSON evidence was added separately, with no new Markdown file.

Primary-OS measurements ([sanitized machine-readable evidence](artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-1-macos-arm64.json)):

| Fixture | Read open | Source open | Mounted DOM / Source lines | Whole-process RSS | Physical footprint |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 MiB | 254 ms | 283 ms | 240 / 35 | 280.94 MiB | 165.50 MiB |
| 10 MiB | 1,265 ms | 1,881 ms | 240 / 35 | 451.62 MiB | 256.50 MiB |

These are one warm **debug e2e** sample per tier, with uncontrolled GC/fixture IPC transients, not release SLAs, peak memory or an input-to-paint result. Timing includes native IPC/initial UI and excludes fixture generation/transfer. The warm Read reference sampled 249.58 MiB RSS / 127.43 MiB physical footprint. Memory includes the app plus all three attributable WebKit helpers, including launchd-parented XPC processes; all helpers exited after app termination. RSS may double-count shared pages. The reader remains the bounded scaffold; these timings do not establish complete large-document Markdown rendering. Release loaded-editor repetitions remain Not Run.

Deviation notes and remaining platform limits:
- No stack change, no phase advanced, and no required Phase 1 implementation slice is left pending. The full Author/Visual, correct large reader, directory browsing, Git and execution remain in their named phases. Bounded undo and byte/queue/storage admission replace any implication of unlimited history or editing capacity; the buffer is preserved when a limit is reached.
- The original `NewlineJoin` refusal is resolved by the tested localized bridging-byte/inverse policy. The test driver appends input at the end; native expectations use that actual behavior. Its title-switch warnings remain recorded. Its HTTP payload ceiling required native-generated large fixtures; fixture transfers are excluded from the recorded open timings. A test-only close request must run off the UI thread; the final close-cancel test verifies the normal handler rather than trusting an injected JS event.
- Not Run: Linux X11/Wayland and its ACL/xattr/non-UTF-8-filename test, Intel/minimum macOS, CI execution, real CJK composition/VoiceOver and full keyboard/accessibility matrices, native Save As dialog manual automation, network/FUSE/cloud filesystems, physical-disk-full/power-loss tests, release loaded-editor latency/memory/idle/close-leak repetitions and distribution signing/notarization. APFS rejected creation of a non-UTF-8 filename; that positive raw-filename case is Linux-only, not falsely passed on macOS. Working-tree changes remain uncommitted.

### Phase 2: First-class Read mode, Markdown rendering, math/Mermaid and focus

Deliverables:

- Reader-first Svelte screen with a distraction-free column, configurable typography, reading focus on by default, TOC, heading search, find, relative links, semantic reading anchor restoration and clear per-block errors.
- markdown-it/CommonMark/GFM extension setup, code highlighting, footnotes, tasks, callouts, image sizing and front-matter preservation. Build an explicit syntax compatibility table and golden examples.
- Worker-backed ordinary parsing, revisioned source spans/block IDs, app-level find/copy/navigation, bounded virtual DOM/cache, image decode limits and semantic scroll anchoring. Minimal light/dark/width/font/focus controls and a discoverable exit from focus ship here; full preferences wait for Phase 4.
- Separate large-reader slice: context-aware range parsing/indexes, provisional context completion, forward definitions/footnotes, segmented scrolling and per-block fallback. Pass G3 differential tests before claiming complete paged Markdown support; document the actual fixture envelope and outstanding parity failures.
- Locally bundled **lazy** KaTeX fast renderer, lazy MathJax 4 fallback with broad TeX math grammar and diagnostics, and locally bundled lazy Mermaid.js with strict security. No remote imports; measure load, render and cache costs.
- Reusable sanitized HTML/SVG resource adapter for renderer output; tests for HTML injection, SVG references, unsafe URI schemes and large/hostile diagrams.
- Read-only file opening does not allocate a full CodeMirror editor or eager all-document DOM; content only enters edit state when requested.
- **Phase 8a delivered here:** built-in standalone no-script HTML export of an explicit saved/dirty revision, all sections (not mounted DOM only), approved embedded assets/fonts, static KaTeX HTML or MathJax SVG and sanitized Mermaid. The Phase 8 PDF/runner work cannot block basic HTML export.

Acceptance criteria:

- Real Markdown opens straight into Read focus, navigates headings/links, renders representative equations and Mermaid, restores reading position and works offline.
- Tests show first viewport does not wait for all diagrams/equations/images, rendering stays responsive around an unsupported/oversized block, and DOM and cache counts are bounded.
- Actual 1/10/100 MB fixtures have honest recorded first-visible-section results, with no imposed whole-document Source fallback solely for file length.
- Malicious render fixtures cannot execute scripts, request external network resources or invoke native commands.
- Finalized paged output agrees with whole-input parsing on the cross-boundary corpus; provisional sections and locally source-only fallbacks are visibly marked. Document-wide find and accessible section navigation reach unmounted content. G4 proves local assets and safe math/diagram policy.
- HTML export opens offline in a separate browser, includes offscreen sections and current selected revision, has no executable script, and handles missing/denied assets without hidden fetches.

Status:
- Complete for the tested macOS ARM64 scope, 2026-11-12; other platforms remain Not Run.

Implementation decisions and work log:
- Reviewed Phase 0 and Phase 1 completion/deviation notes before work. Preserve their byte provenance, grants, journal/save barriers, untested-platform labels and release isolation. No new Markdown files.
- P2-001: keep the current paper/green palette (paper #fafbf9, ink #24352f, quiet #66736d, accent #316c57, line #dce3df), Palatino reading type and system UI type. Default focus hides auxiliary chrome with a visible exit. A collapsible outline and find panel navigate a bounded block window; no full-document DOM.
- P2-002: use markdown-it block parsing with retained unfinished blocks and a separate global context scan, not arbitrary page rendering. Publish provisional context explicitly; re-render after finalization. Oversized blocks receive local source segments and diagnostics. Differential tests against whole-input parsing gate finalized results.
- Dependency selection (registry checked 2026-11-12): KaTeX 0.19.1 (MIT code; OFL 1.1 fonts), Mermaid 12.3.0 (MIT), @mathjax/src 4.1.3 and @mathjax/mathjax-tex-font 4.1.3 (Apache-2.0). Renderer imports are lazy, locally bundled and removable through their adapters; no runtime CDN, document-directed module loader or Node runtime. Resolved transitive/license/build-size and measured hydration costs are recorded below. Direct MathJax modules omit loader/autoload/require; expression-local state, bundled TeX SVG font and package allowlist. KaTeX trust=false, bounded expansion/size. Mermaid strict, HTML labels off, serialized jobs and source/edge admission. Primary references: [KaTeX options](https://katex.org/docs/options), [Mermaid security](https://mermaid.js.org/config/schema-docs/config-properties-securitylevel.html), [MathJax direct modules](https://docs.mathjax.org/en/v4.0/server/direct.html).



#### Phase 2 reader completion and gates

Implementation record (2026-11-12):
- **P2-003 — context-aware bounded parsing:** the saved descriptor's immutable Rust snapshot is the byte authority. A cancelable module worker requests 64 KiB ranges through that opaque grant, maps markdown-it line spans back to original UTF-8/BOM/CRLF/CR bytes, and retains the complete final top-level block between reads. Nested containers, fences, Setext candidates and continuation titles are not cut at arbitrary byte boundaries. The pinned markdown-it reference rule is instrumented to emit zero-output definition spans; custom footnotes do the same. This is a tested dependency on its internal rule table and must be revalidated on parser upgrades. Definitions/footnotes are indexed independently of mounted pages; output is explicitly provisional until scanning finishes. Duplicate heading IDs follow document order and are scoped to the captured revision. No claim of stable inline edit identities or O(changed-range) incremental parsing is made.
- **P2-004 — navigation and editing authority:** group complete blocks toward 4 KiB sections, mount three sections in one scroll container, and expose Previous/Next plus direct numeric section navigation. This segmented reader avoids huge browser scroll heights. Outline filtering, heading anchors, literal Unicode find across read/source boundaries and explicit Copy Shown Text reach beyond a single source window. The current outline display and find results are capped at 200; filtering/direct navigation remain available. Reading positions add an optional heading anchor to the existing backward-compatible record, with byte fallback; read-only clean reload revalidates the captured target and retains the anchor. Dirty Source stays authoritative: Read captures an immutable CodeMirror Text revision through range reads, without whole-buffer Svelte state or a serialization-based save. Refreshes are keyed to session generation and working-text revision, so received/durable ACKs cannot restart an unchanged reader or cancel draft export. Read-only disk invalidation waits for file-operation readiness; recovery-list replay is background work. Worker teardown cancels stale results; in-session Source/undo state remains mounted across Read/Source switches.
- **P2-005 — resource and fallback contract:** 100,000 section records and 100,000 headings; 4 MiB document context / 10,000 references / 1,024 footnotes; 16 KiB ordinary footnote bodies; retained unfinished block 256 KiB plus one read. Oversized fences/paragraphs/containers become labeled, bounded source spans, then resume rich parsing only at a conservative closing/boundary condition. Complex blocks over 1,024 tokens use local source spans. A context/index capacity failure preserves indexed sections and disables finalized export; it is not a silently complete index. Rich rendering admits at most four expressions per mounted section, each at most 512 generated nodes / 1 MiB output; additional expressions show expandable source. The rich cache is at most 8 MiB and clears at reader teardown, dropping stale in-flight cache results. These are finite admission controls, not guarantees that every input below a threshold meets a latency target.
- **P2-006 — local rich renderers:** KaTeX loads with trust disabled, strict errors, fresh macros, 500 expansions and 20-em size bounds. MathJax 4 direct modules use only base/AMS/newcommand/mathtools, fresh input/output state per expression and bundled TeX SVG path data; no require/autoload/HTML/URL package or dynamic loader. Additional known syntax selects MathJax; other errors offer an explicit compatibility retry and retain source. Global macro definitions/equation numbering are not supported. Mermaid jobs serialize on the WebView with strict security, HTML labels off, fixed neutral presentation and a family allowlist; directives, HTML, styling/link commands and resource URLs are refused before rendering. Admission is 12,000 characters / 100 edge-like operators. DOM layout already running cannot be interrupted by a Promise timeout; stale results are discarded. Code highlighting lazily loads highlight.js core with JavaScript/JSON/Rust/bash only; other/oversized blocks stay plain code. Renderer output passes the shared DOMPurify plus resource/ID/CSS adapter. Rich IDs and local references are namespaced per instance, including cache hits; unsafe schemes, animations, embedded HTML and external SVG references are removed.
- **P2-007 — grants and images:** opening one document still grants only that file. Relative links request another native file selection (including fragment navigation), not automatic directory reads or root expansion. Images require explicit per-image native selection; remote references never auto-fetch. Rust captures only PNG/JPEG up to 8 MiB, validates dimensions before returning a typed data URL, and refuses SVG/other formats. Per image: nonzero dimensions, sides at most 4,096 pixels and at most 16 megapixels. Per reader: 16 MiB encoded image data, 32 megapixels and 64 approved images. Rejected/failed decodes retain the Markdown reference with a diagnostic. Numeric Pandoc-style width/height attributes are bounded to 1–4,096; no CSS from document text is applied. This is deliberately narrower than future selected-root asset support in Phase 3.
- **P2-008 — standalone export (Phase 8a):** export requires a finalized captured saved/working revision and traverses every indexed section, including unmounted content and footnotes. All admitted rich blocks use the same sanitized adapters; errors remain source with diagnostics. Approved raster data and local KaTeX WOFF2 data are embedded; missing/denied images receive explicit labels. A lazy build-generated font-data module avoids runtime fetch or a broader connect-src policy. Exported HTML has no script, external stylesheet/font/image dependency or native command surface, and embeds a deny-by-default CSP. Native selection grants a new destination only; same-directory exclusive publication refuses collisions, and the pinned canonical target is checked against application/private roots. Assembly budgets are 48 MiB intermediate / 64 MiB final; capacity/cancellation writes no destination and never clears dirty input. Export is an explicit bounded operation, not a promise that a 100 MiB source always fits a standalone HTML allocation.
- **P2-009 — dependencies and licensing:** direct additions are KaTeX 0.19.1 (MIT code), Mermaid 12.3.0 (MIT), @mathjax/src and @mathjax/mathjax-tex-font 4.1.3 (Apache-2.0), highlight.js 11.12.0 (BSD-3-Clause), and already-resolved Rust base64 0.22.1 (MIT OR Apache-2.0). They are removable behind local adapters; no alternate app stack or runtime server was introduced. Renderer/font chunks stay lazy. The installed production JS inventory now contains 181 records; the audit reports zero known advisories at check time. Package declarations alone missed the KaTeX font license: installed TTF name tables identify SIL OFL 1.1, copyright and reserved names. Unmodified font notices and the official OFL text are retained in `font-notices.txt`, bundled `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` and standalone exports. `scripts/licenses.mjs` regenerates sanitized declarations/notices during builds, never installs dependencies. Phase 9 still audits exact shipped artifacts and platform-specific distribution obligations. References: [official OFL text](https://openfontlicense.org/open-font-license-official-text/), installed package LICENSE/name metadata and [MathJax font documentation](https://docs.mathjax.org/en/stable/output/fonts.html).

- **P2-010 — release ACL cleanup:** binary inspection found a stale `permissions/autogenerated/e2e_fixture.toml` left by a debug build. The command/server were not registered in the normal graph, but its generated permission still appeared in the executable. Normal builds now remove that one generated test permission before Tauri ACL generation; release checks reject the file and executable fixture/server/evaluator markers as well as test capabilities/dependencies. This fixes build-profile contamination without expanding any app permission.

Syntax compatibility and golden coverage (`tests/unit/paged-reader.test.ts`, `reader-security.test.ts` and native fixtures):

| Syntax | Current behavior / explicit boundary |
|---|---|
| CommonMark blocks, nested lists/quotes, Setext/ATX headings, fenced/indented code | markdown-it 15.0.3 display grammar; complete final blocks retained across reads; raw HTML is escaped data |
| GFM tables, strike, autolinks, tasks | Tables/strike/linkify; task markers display inert checkbox glyphs, not writable form controls |
| Footnotes | `[^id]` references; definition plus contiguous four-space continuations; document-wide final appendix and anchors; large definitions show source |
| Callouts | NOTE/TIP/IMPORTANT/WARNING/CAUTION blockquote marker receives a callout presentation; source unchanged |
| Front matter | Leading delimited `---` block is preserved and expandable as source; no settings/trust/schema evaluation |
| Math | `$…$`, `$$…$$`, multiline `$$`, `\(…\)` and `\[…\]`; escaped dollars/currency guard; local KaTeX and explicit/known-syntax MathJax fallback; expression-local macros only |
| Mermaid | Flowchart/graph, sequence, class, state, ER, pie, gantt, journey, mindmap and timeline allowlist; representative native flowchart tested; unsupported/rejected/layout-error cases stay source |
| Images | Native-authorized PNG/JPEG only; bounded `{width=N height=N}`; SVG/remote/unapproved assets are not fetched |
| Links and IDs | Revisioned, ordered duplicate heading IDs; local anchors; relative document navigation needs a native grant; external/unsafe schemes remain inert |
| Definition lists, arbitrary HTML, arbitrary TeX packages/diagrams | Not enabled; original source remains unchanged; no claim of universal syntax parity |

Completion notes and verification:
- Reviewed the previous phases before implementation; all phase prose remains in this section. The existing example/entry points were updated for the actual reader. No new Markdown file was created.
- Frontend checks pass 54 unit tests, Svelte/TypeScript, ESLint and Prettier. Differential fixtures include mixed EOL/BOM, Unicode, nested containers, forward references/footnotes, continuation titles, duplicate headings, tables, math, long definition tails and seeded read boundaries; giant fence/list fallbacks resume without allocating a full rich DOM.
- Rust verification: `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked` passes 55 tests, with one top-level crash helper intentionally ignored and executed by its passing parent in a killed subprocess. Cargo fmt and `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` pass. Rust's live-lock denial remains unchanged: one parallel test exposed a transient inherited-CLOEXEC lock during subprocess startup, so only the post-drop test helper now retries WouldBlock for at most 100 ms. The separate live-session exclusion test still requires immediate refusal.
- Separate Safari smoke opened a byte-identical fresh copy of the final local `file:` HTML, showing math, final footnote and embedded-font notices without an app/server. The final export is 853,115 bytes across 21 sections, SHA-256 `b1b4a95c93ba372630f3bfbffa0a3d7e5f11a38dd7312a7fb27bbc33ca9495ab`. Reloading the repeatedly overwritten test-artifact URL returned Safari file-access error -3001; a fresh native file grant/new temporary path succeeded. Normal app export always chooses a new file. The network adapter was not physically disabled.


- `pnpm test:e2e` passes all 17 actual WKWebView cases: Phase 0/1 regressions, Read without CodeMirror, local rich blocks, native image/link grants, unmounted find, clean reload anchors, malicious renderers, full saved/draft export, large reader and 20-open measurements. The anchor test deterministically dispatches a focus revalidation event in the actual WebView; watcher delivery is independently tested as a hint. An additional isolated run of the malicious-renderer case observes an actual `img-src` CSP violation for a remote-image attempt. Resource Timing returned no entries in this WKWebView capture; empty resource lists alone are not used as proof of no network access.
- Normal `pnpm tauri build --bundles app`, `pnpm check:release`, ad-hoc `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, bundled-example/notices byte comparison and executable marker checks pass after P2-010. Executable: 11,772,752 bytes (11.23 MiB), SHA-256 `6139849a6b1c5fe1476e2e82fd11ee371df609e57d07deafb0df963f6b1233be`; whole app approximately 11.61 MiB. The normal packaged executable remains alive after a three-second launch probe, then exits with all attributable WebKit helpers. This is a process smoke, not a release interactive-performance certification. No Developer ID/notarization credentials were supplied.

Primary-OS measurements ([sanitized JSON evidence](artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-2-macos-arm64.json)):

| Structured fixture | First text / controls-ready assertion | Final context | Indexed sections | Mounted DOM | Editors | RSS sum | Physical footprint |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 MiB | 306 ms | 420 ms | 322 | 114 | 0 | 752.88 MiB | 313.22 MiB |
| 10 MiB | 1,184 ms | 2,096 ms | 3,346 | 114 | 0 | 818.44 MiB | 352.54 MiB |
| 100 MiB | 10,389 ms | 36,402 ms | 33,522 | 114 | 0 | 931.84 MiB | 282.89 MiB |

These are one **debug** sample per tier with native-generated structured prose, warm imports, injected picker selection and uncontrolled OS cache/GC. Native snapshot copy/hash, IPC, polling and readiness/text assertions are included; compositor/input-to-paint is not measured. Final-section navigation succeeds at every tier. Memory is sampled in the same mixed workload after Source and rich rendering, not from three fresh reader launches; it includes the app and three attributable WebKit helpers, with helper exit confirmed. RSS can double-count shared pages; different GC timing prevents interpreting the lower 100 MiB physical footprint as better scaling. These figures are material costs, not a low-memory or release-SLA claim.

Twenty subsequent warm 1 MiB opens record median **1,343 ms**, p95 **1,454 ms**, including controls-ready and text assertions plus prior-workload/recovery scheduling. This differs from a renderer-only or first compositor-paint metric. The aspirational 300 ms ordinary-open/low incremental-memory goals are not established by these debug results; release p95/idle/close-cycle work remains due in Phase 9. G8 is measured, not promoted to a performance pass.

Observed visible hydration (import/queue/render/sanitize, adapter entry absent at start): KaTeX **23 ms**, MathJax **128 ms**, Mermaid **218 ms** for the representative expressions/flowchart. This is one warm-workload observation per renderer, not cold-process or engine-only timing. Rich cache size is enforced and cleared on reader teardown; module code remains loaded. The main entry is 58,372 bytes (22,676 gzip), **not** the whole startup dependency closure. All generated JS totals 9,912,274 bytes; large lazy chunks include MathJax 2,799,366, ELK 1,513,016, Mermaid core 639,520, Source 515,780 and export font data 347,541 bytes. These installed/bundle costs are real even when a diagram family/layout is not enabled. Existing 500 kB warnings remain; the threshold was not raised.

Index/context admissions can stop finalization with a diagnostic; this is not universal Markdown/size support. |

Other diagram families and all TeX syntax are not certified; unsupported cases remain source. |

Deviation notes and Not Run:
- The reader uses explicit segmented scrolling rather than a speculative full-height virtual spacer. The tested grammar/paging envelope and source fallbacks above constrain G3; this does not certify every CommonMark extension or every pathological file. Initial native snapshot capture remains a whole-file copy/hash cost before the worker's first section.
- Native picker fixtures are debug-only, not manual certification of every OS dialog. Embedded-provider title-switch warnings remain. The stress suite uses a disclosed 180-second harness timeout; its former 30-second timeout cut off the 100 MiB diagnostic run rather than demonstrating a parser failure. Earlier focus/status/test-selector failures were corrected; screenshot review also corrected nested-container scroll propagation and Mermaid font-metric mismatch. A combined Phase 1/2 run exposed recovery-list readiness and ACK-triggered reader replacement races; both now have native regression coverage. final results below supersede those runs.
- Linux X11/Wayland, Intel/minimum macOS, CI execution, real CJK IME/VoiceOver/full accessibility and keyboard matrices, physical network-disconnection tests, network/FUSE/cloud filesystems, manual OS grant dialogs, release loaded-reader p95/idle/close-cycle distributions, Developer ID/notarization and Phase 3+ functionality remain Not Run/not started. Copy Shown Text uses the WebView clipboard API with a visible system-copy fallback; the system clipboard path has not been certified across OSes.

### Phase 3: Filesystem library, navigation, quick preview and search

Deliverables:

- **Completed admitted slice:** selected-root tree/paged list, name/date/type/size sorting, quick-open, recents, explicit path/asset grants, compact/grid views, type/hidden/ignore filters, bookmarks, metadata and cheap discovered counts. Git badges remain Phase 6; they are not a dependency for browsing.
- Space quick-preview, multi-tab navigation, history, bookmarks, link resolver, quick-open dialog, heading search and workspace search with optional disposable FTS5 index.
- File/folder creation, rename, move/copy, multi-select, trash/delete, bulk confirmation, drag/drop from OS file manager and appropriate permission/symlink/collision handling.
- Directory watching and paged lists from Rust, an indexing job queue with cancellation and path policies; file tree displayed before the workspace index finishes.
- Use `.git` exclusion by default and visible hidden-file/ignore settings; do not traverse symlink directories automatically. Background search/indexing is bounded and cancelable with a clear exclusion policy. Large directories may sort progressively; global sorted pagination must not pretend only the first page exists.
- File operations use a preflight plan and per-path results. Same-filesystem moves may rename; cross-filesystem moves copy/flush/verify before source removal, with a partial-result log and no all-or-nothing promise for bulk changes. Cancellation never deletes the source before a verified destination. Restore selection and handles after moves; detect collisions on case-insensitive filesystems. Link updates are previewed and opt-in. If trash is unavailable, do not silently fall back to permanent deletion.
- Persistent sidebar/session positions with reader focus behavior that does not clutter the main document window.

Acceptance criteria:

- Open a folder with many entries and navigate, sort, filter, preview, open/close and search without constructing/rendering every descendant in the frontend.
- Bulk operations affect only selected paths, and failure/collision/cancellation conditions are recoverable. Symlink loops are not traversed.
- Multiple roots and tabs preserve ordinary on-disk layouts; no implicit vault migration, root rewrite or hidden proprietary document copy.

Status:
- Complete for the tested macOS ARM64 admitted scope (2026-11-12). The limits, safe refusals and Not Run items below qualify this completion; this is not full-product or cross-platform release certification.

Completion notes:
- Review of the Phase 0–2 completion/deviation records completed before implementation. The Phase 2 ACK/reload fixes, immutable reader snapshots, Phase 1 provenance/journal/save barriers and explicit command ACL remain the foundations.
- Native selected-root capabilities, paged directory browsing, list/compact/grid modes, sorting/type/hidden/ignore controls, quick preview, fuzzy filename/literal content search, recents/bookmarks, bounded document tabs and reviewed file operations are implemented. The ledger below is the historical completion/handoff record. Phase 4 had not started at that checkpoint.

#### Phase 3 decisions and implementation

- **P3-001 — Native authority:** at most eight roots, selected through a native folder dialog or an expiring native OS-drop offer. Rust pins directory FDs and validates native selection/identity, then walks individual components with `openat` and `O_NOFOLLOW`. JS receives opaque root/directory/entry/document IDs and display metadata, never a general path read/write API. Application resources/private storage and `.git` are excluded. Mutations also refuse ancestors containing protected application data. Removing a root cancels its jobs and invalidates its library handles; individually opened document grants/snapshots remain available until those tabs close. This remains one WebView, not an OS sandbox against another same-user process.
- **P3-002 — Bounded browsing:** Rust scans 512 entries per request, returns at most 100 visible rows and labels counts/order provisional until the directory scan completes. Native limits are 50,000 scanned names per directory (including excluded entries), 100,000 live entry handles, 10,000 directory handles, 16 KiB per relative directory path and 16 MiB of directory-component bytes. Unchanged refreshes reuse handles; deleted/replaced entries retire old capabilities. Thirty-two cached listing/watch registrations and eight root watches are nonrecursive. Actions/focus refresh revalidate even when watching fails; watcher events are hints, never save authority. Frontend remembers at most 64 visited destination folders. Grid uses metadata/icons rather than eager image/content decoding.
- **P3-003 — Exclusions/indexing:** `.git` is always excluded in this phase. Hidden and ignored entries have visible toggles. The root `.gitignore` is captured as a regular UTF-8 file, at most 64 KiB, then compiled by pinned `ignore` 0.4.33; negations/globs use its upstream matcher. Nested/global Git ignore files are deliberately not interpreted. A malformed/oversized/unreadable/nonregular root ignore file refuses that root/refresh with a diagnostic. No Git invocation/filter runs. One explicit catalog/search/mutation job may run at a time; tree browsing does not await the catalog. Search is disposable/in-memory, with no SQLite bridge or mandatory FTS database. Filename matching is fuzzy; content matching is literal/case-sensitive in UTF-8 `.md`, `.markdown` and `.txt`, at most 8 MiB/file, 256 MiB/job, 100,000 visited entries, 10,000 directories, depth 64, 5,000 page requests, a 120-second cooperative wall-clock budget and 200 results. Loops, special files, binary/invalid UTF-8 input and budget limits never trigger unrestricted recursion. Search/copy check cancellation between entries and 64 KiB chunks; synchronous sorting/syscalls/metadata sync cannot be interrupted mid-call.
- **P3-004 — Preview/assets:** Space opens an ephemeral, sanitized context-limited preview (first 64 KiB, at most 32 blocks) without a document tab/CodeMirror/full-file capture. A full Open still uses the verified immutable document snapshot. Relative links/images may resolve through a currently authorized containing root, with component traversal and protected-path checks; outside-root references retain the native-picker flow. Remote resources never auto-fetch. Raster format/dimension/encoded-memory budgets and HTML export sanitization remain Phase 2 policy.
- **P3-005 — Tabs/navigation:** the existing document owner is now `DocumentPane.svelte`, under a four-tab host. Each admitted tab retains its Source queue, provenance receipts, selection and exact-byte undo. Inactive Reader components/workers unmount; admitted Source views stay mounted/hidden, so this is a four-editor ceiling, not unlimited inexpensive tab storage. Opening an already-open library location activates its tab. Native Open and document links retain their current-tab/leave-barrier behavior. History and bookmarks use private location hints; heading/outline/find remain the finalized Phase 2 reader. Read focus and an optional lazy library preserve the primary document surface.
- **P3-006 — Durable state:** library roots/recents/bookmarks, display/folder/page options, saved tab locations/active index and the last file-operation log live in bounded versioned `library.json`/last-good `library.bak` under the existing private-store lease and quota. At most 100 bookmarks and 100 unpinned recents are retained; recency cannot evict a bookmark. Previous-session hints remain stable while restoring individual tabs; Restore saved tabs reinstates the saved active tab after fresh grants, with current tabs closed first. Restarts restore hints, never native authority: the user must reselect containing roots or use a native document picker. Unsaved Source restores through the existing Save-As-only recovery flow, independently of saved-tab hints. Unknown/damaged library records preserve originals and block durable writes; the backup can supply read-only hints. Per-file positions/settings now record native byte-encoded path provenance so future folder moves can relocate their hashed keys. Historical records without such path provenance migrate when their known file/parent is revisited; opaque historical hashes cannot be reversed.
- **P3-007 — Mutation protocol:** create file/folder, rename, copy, move and Trash take typed opaque selections and a native preflight token (at most 100 entries, 120-second expiry), a review dialog, fresh validation, a durable pending-operation record and per-item results. Destination creation/rename is exclusive; a conservative case-folded collision check supplements kernel no-replace semantics. Active Source sessions, including clean/in-flight sessions, block mutations; a native reservation blocks starting/restoring Source while a mutation runs. Close Source tabs first; Read snapshots may remain. Already-completed items remain after cancel/failure. Directory operations are not a universal transaction or an atomic snapshot across children. Pending/completed/partial/uncertain results survive restart; quitting refuses an active job so it can finish/cancel and report first.
- **P3-008 — Copy/move/metadata:** copy is bounded to 10,000 entries/1 GiB per job, never follows symlinks/special files, verifies destination bytes with SHA-256, revalidates source identity and flushes file/parents. Permissions/timestamps are retained; macOS also copies ACL/xattrs, while copies remain owned by the current user and do not inherit setuid/setgid bits. Unsupported metadata or interrupted copies can leave a clearly reported partial destination for review; the source is retained. Same-volume moves use exclusive rename. Cross-volume file moves copy/flush/verify before attempting recoverable source removal to system Trash; unavailable Trash leaves both copies and a partial result. Cross-volume directory moves retain the source after copying, because a directory stat cannot establish child quiescence. A committed rename whose parent sync fails is reported uncertain, not rolled back. Explicit moves relocate known library records and refresh authorized Read-tab grants/names without retargeting a live editor. A captured Read snapshot whose target validation fails can enter Source as a dirty, Save-As-only recovery; the old path is not reauthorized. Read-tab notifications follow metadata migration, queue behind busy work, retain reading anchors and never retarget an editor. A newer selection made during a job is preserved; admitted move results provide fresh handles and a visible moved-selection action. No arbitrary Markdown links are rewritten; users review/edit references explicitly.
- **P3-009 — Trash/drop:** on macOS, Trash moves exclusively into the current user-owned, non-world/group-writable home-volume `.Trash`; unavailable/other-volume Trash retains the source. No permanent-delete fallback exists. Restore by moving the uniquely named item out of the system Trash; Finder Put Back metadata/automatic app restore is not claimed. Linux Trash is a safe refusal pending platform implementation/verification. Native drops create at most 64 opaque offers for 120 seconds; UI may Open files or Add folder roots. Browser DOM drops/forged offer IDs cannot authorize a path. Copy-on-drop/rich asset insertion belongs to Phase 5c, not this drop flow.
- **P3-010 — Dependencies and release:** `ignore` 0.4.33 adds the locked globset/bstr/Crossbeam graph. Declared-license inventory is refreshed and the five added upstream MIT-option notices are bundled with existing notices. There is still no generic path/shell/network/SQL command. E2E fixtures/drop simulation/server/evaluator remain feature-gated, forbidden in release, with the existing stale-generated-permission cleanup and executable-marker checks retained. This phase does not start Phase 4 or Git/Phase 6.

| Actual macOS WKWebView | **Pass: 23 cases:** all prior 17 regressions plus 6 library cases. Real native filesystem/backend + WKWebView; native picker/drop fixtures inject selections/offers, not physical OS gestures. Covers 3,000 files, paged DOM, preview/search/cancel, two-tab Source retention, native mutation reservation, exact-byte copy/move, collisions, Read grant rebinding, review cancel/confirm/create/rename/restored selection, forged offers and fresh-handle revocation. |
| Directory/native job measurements | **Observed:** 3,003 visible entries (3,000 text notes + destination/link/source), 100 rows, 1,140 DOM nodes and zero CodeMirror during listing. One final debug complete-listing sample: **2,113 ms** including injected native selection/IPC/UI assertion. Physical process-tree footprint **367.8 MiB** at the library and **373.2 MiB** with two tabs/one Source view; summed RSS **935.1/960.4 MiB** double-counts shared pages. These are mixed-workload samples after earlier tests, not isolated baselines or maxima. All attributable helpers retired after exit. |

Deep/strict ad-hoc signature verifies. One process-cold/cache-uncontrolled launch-to-WebView-load sample: **908 ms** (not interactive paint/p95). A separate default-launch whole-tree sample: **77.0 MiB physical / 184.7 MiB summed RSS**, with helper lifecycle confirmed. Developer ID/notarization remains unavailable/Not Run. |

Evidence and repeatable checks:
- Sanitized [Phase 3 machine evidence](artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-3-macos-arm64.json) records the final release SHA-256, admission limits, test counts, measurement definitions and Not Run cases. Raw logs/screenshots/process samples remain ignored under `artifacts/`; no real user document contents are included in the committed-candidate evidence.
- Run `pnpm check`; `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --check`; `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --offline --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --offline`; `pnpm test:e2e` on the native host; then `pnpm tauri build --bundles app` and `pnpm check:release`. Native watcher/WebView/process checks require host access beyond the filesystem sandbox. The default launch smoke uses `benchmarks/startup.py --runs 1` and `benchmarks/process_tree.py`, both with the normal binary passed explicitly; a single startup sample is not a distribution.
- Integration fixes found and verified: use stable tab keys/reactive instances rather than raw-object identity; scope dialog actions to the active pane; wait for actual dialog/job readiness; preserve newer selections during completion refresh; display errors inside the review dialog; show full destination/new filename in preflight; migrate positions/settings before publishing Read grants; queue Read rebinding without replacing Source.

Deviation notes / Not Run:
- Primary-platform scope remains the existing macOS ARM64/WebKit machine. Linux X11/Wayland, Intel/minimum macOS, CI, real IME/VoiceOver/full accessibility/keyboard matrices, network/FUSE/cloud volumes, cross-volume integration, actual OS permission dialogs and physical file-manager drop gestures remain Not Run. The drop fixture proves the native offer/ACL/UI route, not physical OS input.
- macOS/APFS rejects creation of invalid UTF-8 filenames with `EILSEQ`; Arabic/emoji filename operations run there, while the raw-byte filename branch requires a supporting filesystem/Linux run. Metadata calls/collision/symlink/partial-result logic receive automated coverage; full ACL matrices, real system Trash round trips and external-volume restore are not certified.
- Limits above are admission policies, not evidence that every maximum-size combination has been benchmarked. Full four-editor memory/close-cycle distributions, idle/p95 release measurements and watched huge/deep directory stress remain Not Run. Source snapshots retain the Phase 2 full-copy/two-hash cost; opening a root does not remove it from full document Open.
- Safe refusals are intentional: root-only ignore semantics, `.git` copy traversal refusal, case-only rename refusal (use a distinct intermediate name), no automatic link rewrite, no permanent deletion, no cross-volume directory source removal, no Linux Trash claim and no auto-reauthorization of session/resource/recovery paths. These avoid stronger integrity/platform guarantees than the implementation and evidence support. Larger syntax/editor, Git and shortcut/theme work remains in its own later phase.

### Phase 4: Settings GUI, command palette, shortcuts and theming

Deliverables:

- **Basic controls are already in Phase 2; Alpha slice:** searchable common preferences, basic command palette/mode switcher and key actions. Finish contextual/fuzzy commands and rebindable shortcuts with macOS/Linux conflict validation in the remaining Workbench slice.
- At least two polished light and two dark themes, follow-system default, CSS variables and live preview, custom theme editor/import using `$key=value` grammar and readable error feedback.
- UI/reading/source font chooser, font fallback for Arabic/Hebrew/CJK/emoji, line spacing, text width, font size, code style, syntax color, link/selection/diff colors, high-DPI scaling and OS appearance updates.
- Global/workspace/file overrides and import/export of human-readable settings without modifying Markdown. Session-only changes and reset-to-default actions.
- Minimize startup bundle: Settings, theme preview, library grid, Git and Review components loaded only when activated; one WebView only.

Acceptance criteria:

- Every common setting is editable through GUI without manually editing a file; command palette and shortcuts provide the documented actions; invalid custom themes fail safely.
- Default appearance is readable and calm in Read focus; theme changes do not lose document position or unsaved edit state.
- Source syntax/theme contrast, diff markers, MathJax/KaTeX fonts and RTL fallbacks are actually examined on macOS and Linux.

Status:
- Complete for the tested macOS ARM64 scope, 2026-11-12. Cross-platform/input/accessibility release gates remain Not Run below.

Completion notes:
- Reviewed Phases 0–3 before implementation. Prior source/grant/recovery, reader ACK/invalidation, tab identity and release-isolation requirements remain binding.
- Implemented the searchable settings GUI, typed catalogue/native validation, scoped draft preview/apply/import/export/reset, 2 light + 2 dark/custom themes, OS font names/fallbacks and typography/code controls, contextual fuzzy/recent palette and rebindable commands. Existing Source state/caret/undo/dirty bytes and the visible reading paragraph survive appearance changes.
- Final checks pass: 60 frontend tests, 85 Rust tests (one ignored subprocess helper executed by its parent), strict Clippy/fmt, 27 full native WKWebView cases and a final 4-case Phase 4 rerun. Normal release isolation, ad-hoc signature and bundled resource byte checks pass. No new Markdown files, commits or Phase 5 work. Machine-readable summary: `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-4-macos-arm64.json`.

Deviation notes:
- P4-001 — Use one native closed settings catalogue for GUI metadata/validation, literal imports and effective previews. No settings key can grant filesystem, execution, network or SQL authority.
- P4-002 — Keep the existing green/Palatino direction. Meadow and Parchment are light; Forest and Ink are dark. Colors are typed hex tokens, not CSS; custom palettes must meet text/control/syntax contrast checks. Preferences/palette load lazily, with a compact category list and a live local specimen. No new runtime dependency is planned.
- P4-003 — Reconfigure the existing CodeMirror view and CSS variables without replacing its state/undo/provenance. Preview is temporary UI state, canceled without persistence; backend preview validates the effective scope cascade. Notify all tabs after committed changes and use selected native roots for workspace overrides, falling back to the selected file’s parent when no root is granted.
- P4-004 — Shortcuts are window-local commands, not global OS hooks. Validate canonical chords, cross-platform collisions, and reserved OS/editor bindings; suppress composition and modal conflicts. Native menus/IME/global shortcuts are not claimed to be overridden. Installed-family enumeration returns bounded names only; font fallback never fetches a file or URL.
- P4-005 — Primary verification remains macOS ARM64. Linux/Intel/real IME/VoiceOver/physical keyboard matrices must be marked Not Run when unavailable. Phase 5 is not authorized by this task.
- P4-006 — Scope editing shows explicit overrides and the effective inherited value. Reset scope is a cancelable draft requiring Apply. Advanced offers an explicit all-preferences repair/reset, backing up the previous record and preserving positions; it also clears session settings. Imported text remains a version-fenced draft (late results after cancel/scope changes are ignored); export uses a new native-selected destination. No action rewrites Markdown or grants a path.
- P4-007 — CoreText on macOS and Fontconfig on Linux enumerate family names only, sorted and capped at 512 returned families, 128 bytes per name; candidate scans are bounded at 4,096/8,192 respectively. The native macOS run returned 456 families and 52 catalogue entries. Generic/system/Arabic/Hebrew/CJK/emoji fallbacks use installed fonts; they are not bundled or downloaded. Native font enumeration and CSS selection were examined on macOS; Linux code/linking remains Not Run.
- P4-008 — The palette exposes 21 existing app/document/tab/library actions with contextual disabled reasons, fuzzy filtering, keyboard selection and ten session-local recent palette actions. Quick-open/search share the bounded Phase 3 dialog. Defaults use Mod+Shift+F for workspace search, Mod+Shift+O for outline, Mod+Alt+F for reading focus, Mod+Alt+B for the library, and Mod+Shift+D for compare; window Close remains OS-owned, while Close tab uses Mod+Shift+X. Bindings are global/session only. Conflict checks cover both canonical platform maps, collisions, clipboard/undo/window/menu and common desktop/screenshot chords. They cannot discover every user/desktop global binding; native menus and OS input methods can still consume keys first.
- P4-009 — Effective custom colors must reach 4.5:1 for text/panels/selection, links, Source/Read syntax against both paper and code, and added/removed specimens. Invalid proposals cannot replace saved settings or a valid live preview. If a later global change invalidates another document’s inheritance, apply safe defaults with diagnostics and retain all original literal layers for repair. Root selection/revocation and commits notify every tab; workspace metadata records the actual authorized root for later relocation. Grants themselves remain session-only.
- P4-010 — Preferences and command-palette components are dynamic imports. Small shared appearance tokens and command definitions are eager so the default document can render and dispatch keys; preview controls/native font enumeration load on opening Preferences. CodeMirror retains its existing state, custom undo/provenance and journal barriers; only existing compartments, measure requests and CSS change. Recovery batching remains bounded at 100–1,000 ms with the existing continuous-flush/save fences. Existing lazy renderer chunk-size warnings are not suppressed.
- P4-011 — Native verification caught and corrected number-input coercion of an empty draft to zero and inherited confirmation-dialog width clipping the preferences footer. GUI controls now keep incomplete input editable until native validation, and the preferences body scrolls with visible actions. A concurrent Cargo/Tauri feature build also replaced the shared un-hashed library during rustdoc; serialize native builds/checks and rerun the complete suite before claiming a pass.

#### Phase 4 verification and gates

- **Pass — Frontend:** all 60 tests, zero Svelte warnings/errors, lint and formatting. `pnpm check` covers Svelte/TypeScript, ESLint, unit tests and Prettier. The added presentation/command suite checks four palettes, typed CSS/font quoting, portable physical keys, composition/AltGraph suppression, literal edits and fuzzy filtering.
- **Pass — Native:** 85 tests plus the intentionally ignored subprocess helper run by its parent; strict Clippy and fmt. `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --offline` checks catalogue defaults, OS/cross-platform shortcut conflicts, custom-color inheritance, non-persisting previews, workspace scope revocation, read-only bounded font names and all earlier integrity/recovery/library tests. `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --offline --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --check` remain required. Native watcher/unusual-name cases run on the host, not under the filesystem-only sandbox.
- Actual WKWebView: `pnpm test:e2e` passed 27 cases, including all 23 Phase 0–3 regressions. Four Phase 4 cases cover rejected settings/contrast/bindings, non-persisting native preview, four live themes, number-field search/edit, canceled preview, applied session settings, the same CM content node/caret, dirty preservation, BOM/mixed-EOL byte-exact save/undo, contextual fuzzy actions, rebound key dispatch/composition suppression, and reading typography position. A final targeted rerun also checks the dialog footer and captures Source/Read palettes. Native fixture-injected selections and JavaScript-dispatched key events are not physical OS dialog/keyboard/IME evidence.
- Visual evidence: `artifacts/phase-4-preferences.png`, `phase-4-reader-{light,paper,dark,midnight}.png` and corresponding Source captures are inspected in the native WebView. The final targeted position run reports devicePixelRatio 1; an earlier full run reported 2. These are observed runs, not a physical monitor-transition certification. Read specimens include Arabic/Hebrew/CJK/emoji, JavaScript highlighting, local KaTeX and the MathJax compatibility adapter. Added/removed colors are preference specimens, not implemented Git or Review features. `artifacts/phase-4-reading-position.json` records preservation of the same visible paragraph within the 2 CSS-pixel rounding tolerance.
- **Pass — Normal release:** `pnpm tauri build --bundles app`; `pnpm check:release`; `codesign --verify --deep --strict`; bundled specimen/notices byte comparison. Binary **13,854,496 bytes** (13.21 MiB), whole app **13.60 MiB**, SHA-256 `b834d3a305252c22e645ec03f410479581785461ca076cffb5071855cf60b887`. Normal frontend/config/generated ACL/executable/Cargo graph exclude test fixtures, WDIO evaluator and server; stale E2E permissions are removed by the normal build. Ad-hoc signed only; Developer ID/notarization Not Run. No runtime dependency was added. Preferences/palette are separate lazy chunks (~11.6/2.4 kB minified); Source remains lazy (~520.4 kB, warning threshold unchanged).
- **Observed — Normal default launch/resources:** one initial process-cold, cache-uncontrolled WebView-load smoke **1308 ms**, followed by **20** process-cold/cache-uncontrolled relaunches: median **369 ms**, nearest-rank p95 **470 ms**. These measure the native page-load event, not first interactive paint; each trial restarts the app process without enforcing OS/XPC-service cooldown. A separate three-second whole-process sample is **68.2 MiB physical**, **185.6 MiB summed RSS** (shared pages double-counted); no footprint errors or attributable helpers remain after exit. The first sample and relaunch distribution are both retained; neither certifies the <800 ms interactive-UI aim. `benchmarks/startup.py --runs 1/20` and `benchmarks/process_tree.py` use the final normal binary explicitly. No snapshot/open/typing performance claim is inferred from default launch.
- Machine-readable evidence is `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-4-macos-arm64.json`; raw logs/screenshots remain under ignored `artifacts/`. Use the commands above serially for native builds/checks, with `pnpm test:e2e` followed by a normal release rebuild/isolation check.

Not Run / retained limits:
- Linux X11/Wayland Fontconfig compilation/linking and actual theme/font/math/RTL rendering; Intel/minimum macOS; CI; real IME sessions, VoiceOver and full keyboard-accessibility matrices; physical native settings import/export dialogs, full hardware shortcut/desktop-override matrix, physical OS appearance changes and alternate monitor/DPI transitions. OS appearance uses a subscribed media-query change event; unit/runtime logic does not certify the OS control-panel interaction.
- Default launch results include one initial smoke and a 20-sample page-load distribution; they are not input-to-paint/loaded-document release SLAs. Phase 2 full-snapshot open costs, bounded large-file fallbacks and Phase 3 admission/file-operation limits remain unchanged. Full release/audit/notarization work remains Phase 9. Phase 5 is not started.

### Phase 5: Author experience — separately accepted slices

Deliverables:

- **5a Source/Split (Alpha foundation):** complete the minimal Phase 1 source editor with syntax, line numbers, folding, multi-selection, find/replace, undo/redo, list continuation, writing focus and keyboard formatting. Add synchronized source/preview anchors, click-to-source and resizable split. Reconfigure the same EditorState across modes; reader workers consume revisioned derived inputs, not a new editing authority.
- **5b core Visual (Alpha):** a source-range adapter validates supported heading/emphasis/link/task/list/quote/code operations; CodeMirror decorations/widgets hide inactive syntax and reveal necessary tokens around selection/composition. Keep composition ranges intact, handle selection crossing hidden markers, and reveal source for ambiguity. Real input/caret/accessibility gates matter more than matching a static screenshot.
- **5c rich blocks (Workbench):** GFM table cell/row/column/alignment patches, escaped pipes/code spans, image paste/drop/copy/link/alt/title/path/move/resize and opt-in sizing attributes; source-backed math/Mermaid popovers/zoom. Group text changes as one undo action. File copies are separate side effects: undo removes the reference but does not silently delete a possibly shared asset; show orphan cleanup explicitly. On drop failure/cancel, do not insert a broken link. Handle duplicate names, non-ASCII paths and denied grants.
- **5d range editing (complete core):** bounded CodeMirror range sessions on top of the verified large-reader index/provenance journal. Display range boundaries and limited undo/find/selection scope; acknowledge edits before switching ranges, stream saves from original pieces, recover after a crash, and block stale overlapping operations. No implied cross-window full-document Visual mode. This is independently tested, not a checkbox bundled with table widgets.

| Slice | Status | Evidence / known limits |
|---|---|---|
| 5a Source/Split | Automated primary-OS gates pass | Same view/Text/caret/folds/undo, native find/replace, paired preview and final narrow-pane controls; platform/input gates open |
| 5b core Visual | Automated primary-OS gates pass | Parser-backed spans, localized mixed-EOL/Unicode edits, composition guards and reversible writing focus; real IME/accessibility open |
| 5c rich blocks | Automated primary-OS gates pass | Native cell/shape, rich Apply, selected copy/collision/move/drop review/undo and height-only preservation; physical OS input matrix open |
| 5d range editing | Automated primary-OS gates pass | Native/WKWebView 100 MiB distant exact-save windows, old-grant/bounds refusal, SIGKILL recovery, full working Read/section entry; performance/platform gates open |

Acceptance criteria:

- 5a: Source -> Split -> Read -> Source preserves current revision, selection, folds and undo; two-way source mapping does not create scroll feedback loops. Save while typing retains later edits dirty.
- 5b: Source -> Visual -> Read -> Source preserves source and undo; golden fixtures and G2 prove localized patches for supported constructs, including Unicode and mixed line endings. Ambiguous/unsupported syntax remains editable as source.
- 5c: table/image/math/diagram controls pass minimal-diff/undo/reopen/export tests. Native OS drop and copy collision/failure cases work; text undo does not unexpectedly delete shared files. Resizing changes source only when its attribute format is enabled.
- 5d: edit two distinct ranges of a 100 MB fixture, switch back, save/reopen and verify a diff containing only the intended changes. Recovery, concurrent disk replacement, cross-range restrictions and giant-line handling are exercised; resource failure leaves input recoverable.
- Every shipped editing slice passes actual WebView Arabic mixed BiDi, CJK IME, emoji, keyboard and focus tests on advertised platforms. Full Phase 5 completion requires all four slice gates; Alpha does not claim 5c/5d complete.

Status:
- Implementation and tested macOS ARM64 automated acceptance complete (2026-11-13), all four slices. Full advertised-platform/real-IME/accessibility/physical-input acceptance remains open as listed below. Phase 6 is not started.

Completion notes:
- Reviewed Phases 0–4 completion/deviation notes before changing scope. Source byte provenance, opaque grants, expected-base saves, Save As-only recovery, bounded reader fallbacks, metadata migrations and release isolation remain binding.
- Delivered the same-view ordinary Source/Split/Visual author modes, source-backed formatting and rich controls, native selected/copy/pasted image handling, and bounded large-file range sessions over the existing provenance/journal service. Range switching resets only local UI undo/find, retains durable native edits and rotates stale transport grants.
- Verification passes: 71 frontend tests, 89 Rust tests (one crash helper executed by two acceptance parents), strict fmt/Clippy, the 34-case actual WKWebView suite, then rebuilt affected-case checks for final image/CSS-only fixes (1 image + 3 mode/table/focus + 1 constrained library-pane case). Normal release isolation, strict ad-hoc signature and bundled-resource byte checks pass. Machine-readable summary: `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-5-macos-arm64.json`. No new Markdown files or commits; Phase 6 remains pending.

Deviation notes:
- P5-001 — Extend the existing palette/paper/green interface with a narrow author toolbar, a resizable Source/Split divider and source-backed contextual controls. Keep the same CodeMirror view/state/undo for ordinary modes. Use parser-validated spans; unsupported or ambiguous syntax stays visible.
- P5-002 — Large range editing will keep full immutable/native byte provenance and stream saves, while sending only one bounded logical window to CodeMirror. Switching requires durable ACKs and rotates the transport grant; undo/find/selection are window-local and labeled. This does not make native whole-snapshot capture progressive or free.
- P5-003 — Rich text operations are minimal source patches, not Markdown serialization. Asset copy/link require native selections/opaque drop offers and exclusive destinations; canceled/failed imports insert nothing, and undo never removes copied files. Math/diagram editing reuses local safe renderers.
- P5-004 — All four slice gates must have real evidence before full completion is claimed. Linux/real IME/accessibility/physical OS input not available on this host remain explicitly Not Run. Do not start Phase 6.
- P5-005 — Source adds upstream CodeMirror folding/keymaps, multiple and rectangular selections, list continuation, indentation, find/replace, formatting actions, optional line numbers, writing focus and best-effort WebView spellcheck. `@codemirror/search` 6.7.1 is the only new runtime package (MIT); the production inventory is 182 packages. API reference: https://codemirror.net/docs/ref/. Preserve the custom receipt-backed undo and native edit queue rather than adding a second independently authoritative history.
- P5-006 — Split/Visual admit ordinary buffers up to 2 MiB, including current working-buffer growth; larger documents retain Source/Read and explicit range editing. The same CM view, immutable text authority, selections and fold state survive ordinary mode changes. The resizable split uses 25–75% widths and keyboard arrows, with a stacked narrow-window layout. Synchronization uses normalized UTF-8 line checkpoints plus parser-generated block line maps in indexed sections, never scroll percentages. Programmatic preview initialization is muted; user scroll intent and feedback guards prevent a loop or caret reset.
- P5-007 — Visual hides only recognized inactive markers; active lines, crossing selections and the whole composing state reveal source. Common heading/emphasis/code/link/list/task/quote operations are source patches; table/math/Mermaid/image controls retain revisioned source spans. Unsupported/ambiguous syntax remains Source. Visible widgets are capped at 32; rich sources reuse the safe local renderer/sanitizer and existing admissions. This is in-place Markdown editing, not an HTML layout engine.
- P5-008 — Table editing admits outer-pipe GFM tables with at most 101 total rows (header, delimiter and up to 99 data rows), 16 columns and 8,192 characters/row, with matching native-display/Lezer context. Escaped pipes in inline code work; unescaped code pipes, ragged/ambiguous shapes and nested code/HTML contexts refuse. The table overlay supports cell Tab/Shift Tab, rows/columns/alignment; inactive table widgets display at most 20 data rows / 16 KiB, else Source. Cell edits preserve padding; column edits patch each row separately and preserve its untouched terminator. Each operation is one journal/undo transaction; no whole-table reserialization.
- P5-009 — Image actions are explicit native selection, native opaque drop offer, or user-pasted PNG/JPEG bytes. Native file admission remains 8 MiB, 4,096-pixel sides / 16 MP; clipboard bytes are capped at 2 MiB. Copy uses an exclusive new native-selected destination (existing assets folder proposed, otherwise document parent), validates app-owned paths and never overwrites; link produces a local relative reference. Handle one image per drop and retain the Phase 3 library route for folders/other files. Cancel/failure inserts no broken reference. Source undo never deletes copied files; cleanup is a separate user action. Save As does not copy/rebase assets, so review relative paths after moving a document to a different folder. Read outside selected roots still requires image authorization.
- P5-010 — Image controls edit inline local image spelling with escaped alt/title and opt-in width/height attributes. Reference/angle-path/other ambiguous spellings stay Source rather than guessed rewriting. Numeric/pointer sizing supports ratio lock when dimensions are known; image blocks can move up/down or drag before a source line with insertion feedback. A per-view cache (at most 8 entries / 8 MiB of data-URL characters) displays only explicitly authorized raster data in Visual; it cannot load arbitrary Markdown paths or URLs. Math/diagram popovers offer source, safe local preview, zoom/pan via scroll and one Apply transaction.
- P5-011 — Fresh ordinary source sessions admit at most 16 MiB of backing bytes (and 16 Mi UTF-16 units); already-open ordinary buffers may grow to the existing 32 MiB frontend budget. Larger admitted bases up to 128 MiB use a 131,072-unit window; the frontend refuses growth beyond 1 MiB encoded active text. Native source/retained-piece output is capped at 160 MiB + 64 KiB; the existing 512 MiB private-store, 16 MiB journal, 1 MiB insert/batch, 1,024 changes and operation/token quotas still apply. These are resource admissions, not performance guarantees. Large-session checkpoints are disabled to avoid serializing the full backing store. Native replay receipts/tokens are retained across window switches and limited identically in live/replay state; only frontend window undo is cleared. This can refuse capacity earlier and requires independent Save As/reopen, not a misleading clear-undo repair. Ephemeral independent-copy previews can release history unneeded by pending restore operations without changing the journal. Bounded checksummed replay retains the original schema/semantics.
- P5-012 — Native range grants validate global UTF-16 edit bounds and expected revisions, flush durably before switching, and rotate transport generations. Old batches cannot mutate another window. The new local view resets window-only undo/find/selection explicitly; previous edits remain in full native provenance/recovery. Source edits and exact-byte restore receipts use global coordinates; CodeMirror holds only the active local window. Reader sections can enter a range through native byte-to-UTF-16 checkpoint mapping (including BOM/CRLF/Unicode and inserted pieces). Normal saves stream all byte pieces with expected-base/late hash checks and metadata handling intact; a failed/missing/recovered target still needs Save As. Pending restore-token rescues must ACK first, rather than bypass range validation. Native immutable working-snapshot tokens let Read consume the full selected working revision in 64 KiB ranges, without exporting only the active editor window; the Phase 2 full-context/HTML budgets and refusals remain.
- P5-013 — Bounded frontend windows do not imply bounded total app memory or progressive capture. Native begin/recovery still read/index full admitted bases; the Phase 2 snapshot copy/hash costs remain. The native 100 MiB test exercises BOM/mixed EOL, distant edits, old-generation rejection, out-of-window rejection, save/reopen and Save As-only restore. A separate subprocess is SIGKILLed after durable unsaved edits in two distant ranges; replay and independent-copy bytes are checked. The intentionally ignored crash worker is invoked by both parent acceptance tests, not an unrun gate.
- P5-014 — Native UI checks found and corrected: preview startup feedback moving the caret; cell clicks not carrying row/column identity; opaque native errors losing their message; and whole-table patches normalizing untouched row terminators. Regression fixtures exercise these failure paths. Fold testing uses the explicit upstream Fold action (and the app also exposes its gutter/keymap). GUI fixture selections and dispatched composition events do not certify physical OS input/IME candidate conversion.

- P5-015 — Final source review also corrected table dialogs loading the first column instead of the clicked column, image moves rewriting the inter-line terminator, and math/diagram popovers replacing their delimiters/fences. Table controls now initialize the chosen cell, image moves swap only the two content spans, and rich controls patch verified expression/fence bodies. Unsupported fences and unrelated code contexts refuse; inline code cannot acquire a math widget. Undo remains one transaction. Image edits also retain omitted width/height attributes until that dimension is explicitly edited, so changing alt text cannot add an unwanted size. The UI explains that copied files survive undo; intentional orphan cleanup uses reviewed Library file operations after closing the Source session, never an automatic delete.

- P5-016 — Full working-range Read is asynchronous. Native regression caught an old duplicate-heading ID jumping to the next heading after the first was edited. Heading IDs/duplicate counters are snapshot-scoped: clear old IDs for derived working inputs while retaining the byte position. Hide the previous reader while capturing the selected native working snapshot, show a preparing status, and keep a failed preview explicit/retryable rather than labeling older input as current. Large-file native tests wait for the selected input, not merely for an article left from the previous snapshot. Native visual inspection also corrected doubled gutter padding so line numbers track their content. Final narrow-library Visual inspection found header overflow letting focus horizontally scroll the whole document host; wrap toolbar controls and clip the non-scrolling host/tab while retaining the editor/reader’s own scroll surfaces. The native table case asserts an unshifted shell and the affected mode/table/focus cases are rerun.

Existing reader parity remains exact; source-map attributes are enabled only for the interactive worker, not standalone rendering/export parity.

Footprint reports no errors; all attributable helpers exit with the app. This is a warm debug mixed workload, not an incremental window-memory measurement or release SLA. `artifacts/phase-5-webview-memory.json` retains the full-suite sample before subsequent targeted runs reset the generic memory probe. Native full capture/indexing and retained provenance remain substantial costs.

Binary **14,019,312 bytes** (13.37 MiB), whole app **13.76 MiB**, SHA-256 `bf827a8113cd6a658078d79cf0369373e12eb2547e5b22e79953d02beef6d15e`. The normal frontend/config/generated ACL/executable/Cargo graph exclude E2E servers, evaluators and fixtures, including stale generated permissions. These measure WebView page-load, not interactive paint or loaded-document latency. No OS/XPC-service cooldown or filesystem-cache reset is enforced. Both the initial sample and distribution are retained; the <800 ms interactive-UI aim is not certified. `benchmarks/startup.py --runs 1/20` uses the final normal binary explicitly.
