Not Run / retained limits:
- Linux X11/Wayland, Intel/minimum macOS, real CJK IME candidate conversion/composition, VoiceOver/full keyboard-accessibility matrices, physical Finder/Nautilus/Dolphin image drop/paste/native pickers and pointer-resize/drag matrices, diverse filesystem/ACL/external-volume combinations. Synthetic native-WebView composition guards and multilingual glyph fixtures are not real IME certification. Full advertised-platform acceptance remains gated on these runs.
- Split/Visual are ordinary-document modes; there is no full-document Visual tree over a 100 MiB file. Range selection/undo/find remain local and boundary splits can occur within giant lines. A closed recovery restores text, never an original path grant or cross-restart undo. Existing full-capture/open costs, giant-render fallbacks, asset restrictions, source quotas, residual save races and Phase 3 file-operation policies remain in force. Loaded-document input-to-paint/scroll latency certification, long editing plateaus, and full distribution/audit/notarization remain later gates. Phase 6 is not started.


### Phase 6: Git workbench and Pierre-quality diffs

Deliverables:

- Read-only repository status/branch-name/files list first, bounded history/selected-commit baselines and Git badges; then explicit stage/unstage/destructive-discard controls. Keep commit/checkout/branch mutation out of scope; show unsaved-buffer labels and warnings for untracked/binary/renamed paths.
- Measure `@pierre/diffs` vanilla integration under macOS WKWebView and Linux WebKitGTK; if fit, render unified/split/intraline changes with syntax, accessible navigation, line numbers, folded context and virtualization. If it fails the gate, implement a smaller local virtualized view with equivalent essential interactions.
- Build Rust staging with expected hashes, index-lock acquisition followed by fresh index validation, exact selected patch construction and atomic commit. Prove this sequence with concurrent-lock/index tests; a hash check before a generic index write is insufficient. Detect unsupported attributes/filters/index entries; never spawn configured filters/textconv/hooks. Whole selected hunks first; arbitrary line selection is optional after equivalent correctness evidence.
- First verify whether the pinned `git2`/libgit2 API exposes the needed lock lifetime; reference transactions do not lock the index. If necessary, use an exclusive real index lock plus isolated temporary-index serialization and an audited flush/rename protocol, without a nested write to the live index. Prove preservation of unrelated entries/extensions; detect and refuse split/sparse/unsupported index formats. Do not invent an `Index::write` CAS guarantee or delete someone else's lock. G5 blocks mutation until a correct supported path exists; read-only Git may ship earlier.
- Source diff and rendered Markdown diff (optional semantic comparison) are clearly distinct surfaces; user chooses baseline, navigates changes and sees target of staging. Handle file changes between preview and action.
- Tests for index/worktree divergence, stale/disjoint edits, lock contention, tested CRLF conversion, attribute/filter denials, unmerged entries, symlinks, rename/binary/submodule and unusual contexts in disposable repositories. Git CLI verifies expected index bytes/diffs; unsupported mutation cases assert refusal, not fabricated staging support. Disable external networking/filter execution in test fixtures too.

Acceptance criteria:

- One selected hunk can be staged without unrelated edits; a stale or concurrently modified index refuses or recomputes safely and cannot stage wrong bytes.
- Diff UI is navigable with keyboard and remains responsive on substantial real diffs, including a 10k-line benchmark fixture.
- The implementation documents Pierre license/cost/compatibility findings or the measured reason for replacing it.
- G5 passes; clean/smudge/process filters and external textconv do not run on open/status/diff/stage. Native confirmation/trust is required before any future extension that would run them.

Status:
- Implementation and automated acceptance complete for the tested macOS ARM64 admitted scope (2026-11-13). G5 passes on the supported native path; full platform/input/performance certification remains open below. Phase 7 is not started.

Completion notes:
- Reviewed Phases 0–5 completion/deviation records before implementation. Preserve Source grants/provenance, revision barriers, Save As-only recovery, native mutation reservations, local lazy renderers and release-test isolation.
- Delivered explicit repository selection/revocation, status/history and scoped Library badges; native captured Disk/Index/HEAD/commit source comparisons; unified/split/intraline/folded-context virtual rows; whole native-hunk stage/unstage; reviewed whole-file discard with exact recovery and exclusive Save As export. No generic bridge or configured executable is exposed.
- Verification: **74 frontend tests**, zero Svelte diagnostics, ESLint/Prettier; strict Rust fmt/Clippy; **103 full Rust tests pass** plus one crash helper executed by its two acceptance parents, then **14 affected Git/config tests pass** after final config subsection/condition corrections. **37 full shipping actual WKWebView cases pass**, plus an independent **3-case Git sequence**; one rejected, opt-in Pierre experiment is skipped. Its successful probes and warm-suite timeout are recorded separately, not called a shipping pass. The native suite also caught and fixed selection retaining the previous Recovery tab. Embedded-driver option clicks require a DOM change-event test adapter; no physical input certification is inferred.
- Normal release: ARM64 app **15,874,508 bytes / 15.14 MiB**, binary **15,379,568 bytes**, SHA-256 `0c68d8bab2fa2d2a0c9902c3334276222655eb515c9e567c719be3166728b4de`. Normal frontend/config/generated ACL/executable/Cargo-graph isolation and deep/strict ad-hoc signature pass. Example and generated notices are byte-identical in the bundle. The lazy workbench chunk is 19,033 bytes plus existing shared modules; no Pierre/e2e probe chunks ship. Rust inventory and added Git/libgit2/zlib/SHA-1 notices are refreshed; final distribution/advisory audit remains Phase 9.
- Machine-readable summary: `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-6-macos-arm64.json`; raw logs/measurements are listed there. All phase planning/decisions/completion/deviations remain here. No new Markdown files or commits; Phase 7 remains pending.


Deviation notes:
- P6-001 — Keep the paper/forest palette, Palatino document type, system UI and source monospace. Git opens as an optional workbench surface with a bounded changes/history rail and an expansive source diff; baseline names, saved-byte scope and hunk actions remain visible. Reuse theme tokens, syntax colors and local assets.
- P6-002 — Explicit native repository selection grants only a validated ordinary working tree and its in-tree Git metadata. No generic path, revision-expression, Git CLI, network or filter bridge. Read raw bytes through bounded coherent captures; mutations require a supported index/config/attribute policy and the existing no-live-Source reservation.
- P6-003 — Pin git2 0.21.0 (libgit2 1.9.7) without SSH/HTTPS transports. Its high-level Index write API does not expose lock-held revalidation. Implement an exclusive real index.lock protocol and preserve raw unrelated v2 entries, invalidating cache-tree data deliberately; refuse unsupported versions/flags/extensions instead of guessing. Stage selection uses captured native hunks, not renderer patches.
- P6-004 — Pierre 1.6.4 is a development-only candidate experiment (Apache-2.0, 11,457,889 unpacked bytes, Shiki/Highlights graph). Actual WKWebView 10k-line probe: 19 ms import, 22,171 ms render/settle including fixed 2.1-second waits and scrolling, 94 initial / 2,202 final shadow-inclusive nodes. The source fallback displayed at most 200 rows / 1,070 nodes with an observed 1,191 ms fixture+commit+capture+split/intraline/scroll-assert sequence (not input-to-paint). Select the smaller native-hunk renderer to avoid the extra highlighter/editor graph and observed native stall. A standalone Vite production probe produces 405 reachable JS chunks totaling 11,184,207 bytes (2,251,470 summed gzip bytes), including dynamically reachable language/theme modules; this is the candidate graph cost, not the shipped app payload. Repeated warm WKWebView runs also observed a 23,344 ms settle and an executor timeout. The reproducible Pierre probe stays debug-only, opt-in with MARKTUB_PIERRE_PROBE=1, and is removed from normal chunks; the failed experiment is not a normal product regression. Linux WebKitGTK remains Not Run on this host; no cross-platform claim.


Implementation policy:
- P6-005 — Read ordinary SHA-1 indexes v2/v3/v4, with v3/v4/extended/conflicted/unknown-extension states read-only. Mutate only supported v2 entries under exclusive index.lock, revalidate index/HEAD/worktree/config/attributes after lock acquisition and again after temp sync, preserve unrelated raw entries and index metadata, and invalidate TREE intentionally. A replaced/foreign lock is never removed.
- P6-006 — Capture bounded standard system/XDG/global configuration before disabling libgit2 automatic config loading; repository config is vetted before opening. Repository includes/redirected Git environments, metadata aliases, alternate/shallow/linked worktrees and alternate object formats refuse. Standard system/user config aliases and bounded include chains are captured as native preferences, including gitdir/worktree/onbranch conditions and effective value precedence ([Git config semantics](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-config#_conditional_includes)); only gitdir patterns admit symlink spellings. Unknown conditions, cycles, budgets or unsupported transforms yield raw read-only comparisons; repository-directed includes remain refused. No configured filter/textconv/hook/tool or transport is invoked. Root/nested ignore and info-exclude rules are local parsers; external ignore policies make untracked candidates read-only. Noncanonical CR indexes under autocrlf refuse mutation; supported canonical-LF clean converts CRLF only.
- P6-007 — A preview captures native bytes, hunks and expected bases. Renderer pages reference those captures, never accept patch text. Whole hunk stage/unstage first; whole-file discard requires a reviewed confirmation, the existing no-live-Source reservation, an exact durable backup and the audited expected-base atomic writer. Recovery exports use a new native-selected destination; restarts restore no repository/path authority.
- P6-008 — One repository and one active comparison; 20,000 status/index/tree entries, a 16 MiB index, 50,000 metadata entries / 64 levels, 4 MiB per text side, 50,000 lines/side, 1,000 hunks, 200 rows/page and 1 KiB visible text/line. Status content/time limits are 256 MiB / 30 seconds with incomplete labels; these admissions do not certify latency or strict decompression peak memory. Up to 50 first-parent commits have native history file lists. Binary/type/mode/possible rename pairs and ambiguous transforms remain diagnosed/read-only.


Admitted behavior and limits:
- **Implemented:** explicit repository selection/revocation; branch/status and staged/working badges; 100-row file pages, first-parent history and clean historical files; Disk/Index/HEAD/captured-commit source baselines; unified/split/intraline changes, line numbers, expandable context, hunk selection/navigation, keyboard scrolling and current-Disk Source navigation. Library badges are cached display hints labeled “at last refresh”; status refresh, selection and revocation invalidate them. Unsaved editor content is excluded explicitly.
- **G5 supported path:** selected whole native hunks stage or unstage without changing disk or unrelated index entries. Index ownership/mode/ACL/xattrs use the existing audited metadata copier. A durable lock file is published only after fresh expected-base checks; directory-sync uncertainty is reported for inspection. Tests use disposable repositories and Git CLI as an oracle. Runtime exposes no Git CLI, revision expression, path, shell, remote or configured-executable bridge.
- **Discard/recovery:** whole supported existing regular files only; explicit review/cancel/confirm, no live Source, exact durable recovery bytes before expected-base atomic replacement. Export is exclusive Save As. Recovery is capped at 100 records and shares the existing 512 MiB private-store quota. Binary files, absent/deleted-file restoration and untracked deletion are not admitted. No restart restores a repository or document path grant.
- **Conservative refusals:** linked/bare/shallow/grafted/alternate-format repositories, Git metadata symlinks/special files/alternates, repository-directed config includes and redirected Git environments refuse. Supported v3/v4 indexes and real merge conflicts have read-only comparisons; sparse/split or undecodable indexes may refuse opening. Unknown extensions, entry flags, type/mode changes, submodules/symlinks, binary/non-UTF-8 content, possible rename pairs, attributes/filter/encoding policies and unsafe EOL transforms cannot mutate. External ignore policies make untracked candidates read-only; bounded local ignore rules still filter candidates. Existing tracked regular files remain available under supported effective configuration.
- **Renderer scope:** the fallback uses native hunks and plain text, a validated span-only adapter for the existing lazy highlighter on context rows, and Unicode prefix/suffix intraline marks. Changed rows retain theme-validated added/removed contrast. No second editor, external resources, worker/highlighter graph from Pierre, arbitrary-line staging, rendered/semantic Markdown diff, commit, checkout or branch mutation is implemented. Line display is bounded; exact mutation buffers retain all admitted bytes. Index/worktree changes after capture require refresh rather than silently applying a stale preview.
- **Residual platform/OS limits:** macOS ARM64 is the tested host. Linux WebKitGTK, Intel macOS, VoiceOver/screen-reader traversal, physical OS keyboard/select interactions, actual IME, filesystem-specific locks/ACLs and long Git scroll/edit plateaus are Not Run. Embedded-driver buttons/composition/select change events do not certify physical input. Git status time/content budgets are admissions, not latency guarantees; input-to-paint p95, strict hostile-object decompression peak bounds and a dedicated Git-only whole-process memory plateau are not certified. The usual small noncooperating-filesystem interval between final validation and rename remains; cooperating Git processes are excluded by the real index lock. Phase 7 is not started.


Verification and measurement ledger:

| Gate / scope | Result | Evidence and qualification |
|---|---|---|
| G5 native index integrity | Pass, admitted macOS ARM64 path | Selected-hunk Git CLI oracle, unrelated raw entries/write-tree cache, real foreign/replaced lock and noncooperating-index races, stale worktree/HEAD/config/hunk rejection, CRLF, conflicts/v4 read-only, filter/attribute denials, exact discard and backup-failure tests. Final config tests cover alias-relative includes, subsections, gitdir versus realpath-only worktree conditions. Unknown state refuses mutation. |
| Fallback native UI | Pass, automated scope | Full 37-case WKWebView run includes source navigation/live-Source denial, selected-hunk stage/unstage, discard cancel/confirm/recovery export, 10k-line split/intraline/history, scoped Library badges and revocation. Pierre has separate successful probe measurements and a later timeout; opt-in experiment, not shipped. |
| Prior phase integrity | Pass, automated scope | Full native/Rust regressions retain exact saves, Source/Split/Visual state, recovery, 100 MiB ranges/Read, scoped library and preferences. This does not expand earlier phase platform admissions. |
| G7 and normal release isolation | Pass, current command surface | Opaque native captures/grants, no generic path/revision/executable/transport bridge. E2E server/evaluator/fixtures and Pierre probe absent from normal release; ad-hoc signature/resources verified. |
| Linux / Intel / physical input / accessibility | Not Run | No matching host/input/screen-reader matrix available. Native-WebView synthetic events and injected native chooser fixtures do not certify those cases. |
| G8 performance certification | Open | Point samples below; no interactive-paint, long plateau or hostile decompression peak certification. Slower final page-load timing is recorded rather than counted as an SLA pass. |

- **10k native fallback:** final full-suite observation 200 rows, 1,068 workbench DOM nodes, 480,000 px virtual extent; 1,717 ms fixture/commit/capture/split/intraline/scroll-assert sequence. Independent targeted observation was 1,489 ms. These include setup/assertion overhead, not input-to-paint latency.
- **Whole-process memory:** normal default release at 3 seconds **68.7 MiB physical / 179.3 MiB RSS sum**. Debug targeted warm Git sequence (Source previously opened/closed, Pierre not invoked): **173.0 / 338.6 MiB**. Full-suite warm Git surface after all earlier renderer/100 MiB workloads: **779.1 / 1,184.0 MiB**. Both debug samples have uncontrolled GC/transients; neither is an incremental Git cost or plateau. All measured helper lifecycles confirm exit; physical and RSS are distinct metrics. Long retained-memory growth remains G8 work.
- **Normal launch:** initial 492.2 ms; 20 subsequent process-cold/cache-uncontrolled page-load samples median **446.9 ms**, nearest-rank p95 **834.1 ms**. Final timing run followed completed frontend checks; no explicit OS/XPC cooldown. This is slower than Phase 5's 354.3/409.0 ms on this host; attribution is unresolved and a controlled cooldown/interactive-paint comparison remains open. It is not first interactive paint and does not certify the 800 ms aspiration. Normal default physical footprint remains close to Phase 5's 68.2 MiB.

### Phase 7: Optional agent-document review and external changes

Deliverables:

- First-party opt-in review perspective with captured last-viewed versions, Markdown-aware changed-block highlighting, source Git/disk diffs and section-level navigation. Read remains uncluttered if Review is disabled.
- Define the baseline explicitly: while enabled, start with a captured saved revision; viewing a new revision does not automatically acknowledge it. An explicit Mark Reviewed advances the baseline. Dirty editor text has a separate labeled comparison. Enable-review-with-no-baseline starts from current saved bytes and says there is no prior comparison; do not invent change history or infer whether an agent or human wrote the file.
- Separately track last-viewed: freeze the previous view's saved revision at session entry for the changed-since-last-viewed comparison, then record the last saved revision actually displayed when leaving that view. Auto-reload may update current content but does not move the frozen comparison or Mark Reviewed baseline. Snapshotting is enabled only with Review and its retention policy; “viewed” means displayed, not every paragraph read.
- Source-backed anchored comments, status badges, reply/edit/delete, orphaned/reanchored indicators, approve/request-changes and review navigation.
- Clipboard/portable Markdown + JSON feedback export containing actual file path (subject to user consent), source ranges, line/heading fingerprint, hash, comments and optional snippets; redact sensitive paths when requested.
- Correct external atomic replacement and rename recognition, source/read snapshot preservation, disk-versus-dirty conflict handling and optional 3-way compare/merge.
- Durable review data stored separately from disposable index; explicit clear/delete/export controls; no cloud account or embedded AI.
- Reanchor only when quote/fingerprint/context has a unique verified match; identical repeated paragraphs or deleted text become ambiguous/orphaned. Hashes identify revisions, not semantic intent. Capture size/retention limits are visible; pruning old baselines never removes active comment records without an explicit resolution/export choice.

Acceptance criteria:

- When Review is off it adds no material background work or visible UI; newly created review data requires an enabled workflow.
- An agent replacing a file externally produces an accurate since-last-viewed comparison without modifying unsaved human edits.
- Comment export contains verifiable anchors and marks stale/orphaned comments explicitly; no invented source positions.

Status:
- Implementation and automated acceptance complete for the tested macOS ARM64 admitted scope (2026-11-13). Advertised-platform, actual-input, fault-matrix and performance certification remain open below. Phase 8 is not started.

Completion notes:

- Reviewed Phases 0–6 completion/deviation notes and retained Source grants/provenance, native snapshots, no-live-Source Git mutation, recoverable save failures, and normal-release isolation.

Deviation notes:

- P7-001 — Keep the existing paper/forest theme tokens, Palatino reading type and source monospace. Review adds a quiet optional margin with clearly named saved baselines, changed Markdown blocks and anchored conversations. Read has no margin or capture work while Review is off. This follows the established reader brief; no new dashboard styling or separate editor.
- P7-002 — Durable review records live in app-private storage independently of the library index. Explicit enable captures native saved bytes; Mark Reviewed alone advances the reviewed baseline. A view freezes the previous displayed saved revision on entry; leaving records only a saved revision actually displayed. Auto-reload and disk capture do not acknowledge content.
- P7-003 — Bounded UTF-8 captures (2 MiB), eight retained snapshots per record, 200 comments and 20 replies per comment, 128 records and the shared 512 MiB private-store quota. Active comment anchors pin their snapshots; capacity refuses with export/delete guidance. No automatic deletion of comments. Unknown schemas/corrupt records refuse writes and remain available for explicit deletion.
- P7-004 — The shared Markdown grammar proposes block/heading spans; Rust verifies exact native byte ranges/quotes and stores SHA-256 provenance. Reanchoring requires one exact quote plus matching context; repeated/deleted/changed quotes remain ambiguous/orphaned. Manual reanchor is explicit. Feedback defaults to redacted paths and omitted snippets; including an actual path requires explicit consent and a current native document grant.
- P7-005 — Retain existing dirty-buffer conflict Compare/Reload/Keep Local/Save As; no automatic merge or editor replacement. Add saved-review/disk source comparisons using the native hunk computation. Git comparisons remain the Phase 6 workbench. Same-parent external renames are recognized by unique original device/inode identity; following a new path requires native selection, with no automatic Source path reauthorization. Three-way merge remains optional and deferred.
- P7-006 — Implement native record/capture/anchor tests, parser/block/privacy tests and actual WKWebView external replacement/comment/export/dirty-conflict cases, then full regression, strict native/frontend checks and a normal release rebuild. Linux/Intel/physical input/accessibility and sustained performance certification remain explicitly Not Run where unavailable.


Implementation and admission record:

- **Native authority and storage:** Review is a lazy optional module with closed Tauri commands and ephemeral session tokens bound to native document identities. Enabling captures the immutable saved document grant; Capture disk reads only the scoped current file. Neither writes document bytes or opens Source. Stored UUID records live separately from the disposable library index. The closed stored-review manager exports or deletes records without reauthorizing original paths. Deletion is explicit and also discards associated UI drafts after confirmation. No cloud, account, AI or execution bridge was added.
- **Baseline semantics:** A view freezes the previous completed view's actually displayed saved revision on entry. Only the ordinary native saved Read view qualifies for displayed history; Source's working Read/Split stays explicitly separate even when clean. Disk capture cannot acknowledge a displayed revision. Mark saved capture reviewed alone advances review acknowledgement. Approve/request changes is separately bound to a captured hash and becomes outdated when that hash changes. Missing initial history is stated rather than fabricated.
- **Anchors and changed blocks:** Shared Markdown parsing proposes top-level block/heading ranges; native code verifies exact UTF-8 byte ranges, original quote/hash, raw CR/CRLF/LF line coordinates, heading span/hash and up to 96 bytes of surrounding context. Native load revalidates stored snapshots and anchors. Reanchoring requires a globally unique exact quote with matching context: repeats are ambiguous; deletion/context changes are orphaned, with null current positions. Explicit manual reanchor retains up to eight original anchors. UI comments select whole source blocks up to 64 KiB. Changed-block matching uses exact sequence alignment; this does not assign semantic identity to repeated blocks.
- **Bounded presentation:** Captures admit at most 2 MiB UTF-8, no NUL, and 50,000 raw logical lines. Block parsing admits 4,000 blocks; changes show 30 per page; the filtered comment-block selector shows the first 200 matches. Changed-block HTML uses the shared grammar/sanitizer; links/images are inert text/placeholders, math and diagrams remain source, and large/token-heavy blocks use labeled excerpts. No image grants, navigation or rich jobs originate from Review. Saved source comparison uses native zero-context hunks, 100 lines per side/page and 1,024 displayed characters per line; Phase 6 Git retains its ordinary three-context hunks.
- **Retention and failure:** Eight snapshots per record; 200 threads; 20 replies/thread; eight previous manual anchors; 128 records; 32 MiB serialized record bound and the shared 512 MiB private-store quota. Current/reviewed/displayed/frozen revisions and all live or previous comment anchors pin captures. Capacity refuses with export/delete guidance instead of pruning feedback. Private writes use atomic replacement and sync. Quota traversal tolerates an entry disappearing during metadata lookup as other services replace records; symlinks and other errors still refuse. Unknown/corrupt record schemas/provenance refuse writes and preserve originals. The valid catalogue permits explicit removal of an unsupported record; a damaged catalogue needs compatible recovery/manual repair.
- **Draft and lifecycle safety:** Failed or stale comment/reply saves retain their text; refresh and block reselection do not erase drafts. Tab suspension preserves drafts in memory. Leaving asks Keep editing or explicitly Discard review draft and leave. Drafts are durable only after Save comment/reply, and are not crash recovered. Every update validates the clicked version and capture hash. Review lifecycle operations serialize; failed native end can be retried. Open/reload/close reserve the pane before their first asynchronous leave decision, preventing watcher reload and mode-start races. Tab close locks the editor while awaiting end/release and removes the captured tab key, preventing async index drift or duplicate close races.
- **Portable feedback and privacy:** JSON and a portable Markdown envelope are generated natively from saved records. Reports include original byte/line/heading provenance, captured hashes, reviewed/displayed roles, decision hash, replies and current anchored/ambiguous/orphaned status. Paths and quote/context snippets default off; comment/reply text is exported verbatim, with an explicit warning to remove sensitive user-entered text. Actual path inclusion requires consent and a live native grant; a non-UTF-8 native path refuses that option instead of inventing a lossy path. Archived reports always have a null path and not_evaluated current anchors. Copy uses prepared text and reports clipboard refusal. File export uses a new native-selected exclusive destination, never a frontend supplied path or text payload.
- **External changes:** Atomic replacement preserves the frozen comparison and existing dirty Source; saved native captures, working editor and disk are labeled separately. Existing Compare/Reload/Keep Local/Save As remain authoritative, including expected-base save refusal. A unique same-parent device/inode match within 4,096 entries reports a rename only. A held read-only original inode prevents inode reuse; displayed bytes still come exclusively from the immutable private snapshot. Selecting a renamed path requires the native picker, and review history stays archived under its original path identity rather than silently migrating grants or records. Three-way merge remains optional and deferred.
- **Crash/durability limits:** Review record/catalogue creation and deletion span two files and are not a multi-file transaction. Interrupted first enable may leave an unindexed private snapshot; interrupted deletion can leave a stale catalogue entry requiring repair. These operations never overwrite Markdown or silently discard saved comments from another record. Power-loss, filesystem fault and adversarial multi-service quota stress beyond the tested failures remain unverified; the quota is a preflight estimate rather than a globally reserved allocation across all services.

Verification and evidence:

- **Frontend/native checks:** `pnpm check` passes with 79 Vitest tests, zero Svelte errors/warnings, ESLint and formatting. Rust fmt and strict all-target Clippy pass. Full Rust suite passes 113 tests, zero failures, plus one ignored crash-harness helper invoked by the small/large process-kill parent tests. Eight review tests cover baseline/display acknowledgement, durable anchor validation, ambiguous/orphaned outcomes, context/manual reanchor, hash/version fences, retention refusal, raw EOL line ends, reply/status CRUD, privacy and context-free native source diffs. Two document tests cover immutable saved/disk captures, rename identity and large/app-owned refusals without losing Read.
- **Actual WKWebView:** Final full shipping suite passes 40 tests, zero failures, with one rejected opt-in Pierre probe skipped (`artifacts/phase-7-native-shipping-verified.log`, 7m22.3s). The three Review cases exercise opt-in UI, comments/replies/edits, stale-save draft preservation and explicit discard; native clipboard equality with prior clipboard text restored; consent-scoped JSON new-destination export; archived redaction and explicit deletion; frozen displayed/reviewed comparisons across atomic replacements; same-task Reload/Source reservation; dirty Source preservation and expected-base save refusal; unique same-parent rename and forged-grant denial. Picker fixtures inject debug selection only: WebView, IPC, bytes and file operations are real. Synthetic DOM/select/composition events certify neither physical input nor OS IME.
- **Failures investigated:** Earlier full runs produced 38/40 then 39/40 passes. Test setup clicked while operations were disabled or closing; close helpers now await actual removed tab identity and enabled controls. A transient private-store quota metadata scan could encounter an atomically removed entry and now tolerates only that disappearance. A reload/mode-start gap before the first await was closed by synchronous pane reservation; the final native suite includes a deterministic regression. Saved Read completion and native capture completion are distinct and are awaited separately. Earlier raw logs remain in the machine-readable evidence; they are not counted as passing full suites.
- **Layout and memory:** Actual WKWebView captures show no page overflow at 1,080 px document width (436 px margin, 39 Review nodes) or 713 px with Library (696 px stacked Review, 39 nodes). A targeted early debug small-Review point sample is 136,891,992 bytes physical footprint / 266,862,592 RSS, with helper lifecycle confirmed; it predates final metadata-scan/reservation hardening and is not an incremental cost or plateau. The final full-suite warm candidate has an extra EnhancedSecurity WebKit helper surviving exit; lifecycle attribution fails, so its raw candidate total is rejected as a certified app-memory measurement. No sustained Review memory/latency SLA is claimed.
- **Remaining certification:** Linux WebKitGTK, Intel macOS, actual OS IME/physical keyboard and select behavior, VoiceOver/screen-reader traversal, maximum-capture sustained scrolling/edit latency and memory plateau, power-loss/filesystem fault and adversarial shared-quota stress, Developer ID/notarization and portable distribution are Not Run. Phase 9 retains the distribution/performance/accessibility gates.
- **Evidence location:** `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-7-macos-arm64.json` summarizes the admitted scope and references ignored raw logs/screenshots/profiles. All phase plans, decisions, completion/deviation notes remain in this section; no new Markdown files, external dependencies or commits. Phase 8 is not started.


Normal release and completion:

- Rebuilt `pnpm tauri build --bundles app` after the final e2e suite. `pnpm check:release` passes normal frontend/config/generated ACL/executable/Cargo graph isolation; no evaluator/server/fixture permission or Pierre probe is shipped. Strict deep ad-hoc signature verification passes. Bundled specimen and third-party notices are byte-identical to repository resources. ARM64 executable SHA-256 `e735612eedb37650dc92f42b2938ebdb621a718ffcf2c4776864d8c84fec83d7`, 15,857,536 bytes; bundle 16,352,476 bytes (15.60 MiB). Normal output has 95 JavaScript chunks. Lazy Review panel/archive chunks are 22,387 / 3,407 bytes (8,782 / 1,635 gzip); shared grammar/UI modules are additional. No new external dependencies; 182 production JavaScript and 446 resolved Rust license records remain, with final distribution audit in Phase 9.
- Serial normal default launch measurements (Review off) use the same executable hash and run after native/frontend checks. First smoke launch: 2,195.8 ms; next 20: median 656.5 ms, p95 834.1 ms, nearest rank. This is launch-to-WebView-page-load with uncontrolled filesystem cache and no explicit OS/XPC cooldown, not first interactive paint. Phase 6 was 492.2 ms first / 446.9 median / 834.1 p95: the slower first launch and median remain unresolved, and no startup SLA pass is claimed. Default physical footprint is 72,715,216 bytes (69.35 MiB), RSS sum 188,973,056 bytes (180.22 MiB) after three seconds; helper exit/lifecycle attribution confirms the total with no footprint errors. G8/Phase 9 owns controlled latency and sustained memory certification.
- Phase 7's optional Review implementation and automated admitted-scope acceptance are complete. Default Read remains lazy and uncluttered; Source integrity/recovery, native grants and expected-base conflict handling retain regression coverage. Optional three-way merge and automatic record migration are deferred explicitly, with no implicit path authorization. Cross-platform, physical-input/accessibility, durability fault and sustained performance limits above remain open rather than being relabeled complete. No Phase 8 scope has been started.

### Phase 8: Independent export and optional-execution slices

Deliverables:

- **8a HTML:** implemented and accepted in Phase 2; this phase keeps regression coverage. Built-in no-script offline export never depends on shell/SQL/local-TeX trust or Pandoc installation.
- **8b PDF:** user-selected local Pandoc + Typst/TeX, version/path validation, allowlisted AST/templates/options, scoped staged assets, cancel/timeout and actionable missing-tool/unsupported-feature diagnostics. Explicitly choose saved or dirty revision; never consume arbitrary repository defaults, filters, raw code or tool paths from Markdown. Test real formulas, diagrams, image dimensions, footnotes/TOC/UTF-8 and system Open/Print of output. PDF engine authorization is separate from block execution.
- **8c shell:** app-managed global enable + workspace trust + explicit Run + Rust/native confirmation bound to current command, revision and cwd. Execute an intentional script using a user-selected shell/interpreter (e.g. a temp script), not an accidental shell constructed from settings. Show interpreter/cwd/command and limit captured output/time. A shell script has the user's normal OS privileges; trusted-root validation is not filesystem/network sandboxing. Process-group cancel is best-effort for descendants that deliberately detach; document it honestly.
- **8c SQLite:** explicitly selected local DB, separate readonly connection by default, authorizer/defensive mode, untrusted schema policy, forbidden extensions/ATTACH/file-writing functions/unsafe pragmas, bounded rows/bytes/SQL complexity and progress-handler/interrupt cancellation. Separate from metadata DB. An explicit native-confirmed write mode uses transaction/rollback boundaries; readonly is an enforced connection policy, not a keyword filter. Verify DB/WAL behavior and never mark a mutable DB immutable to bypass locks.
- **8c trusted local TeX:** separately enabled and native-confirmed for the exact job, chosen tools, private temp directory, shell escape disabled, local preinstalled packages only, finite output/time and diagnostics. No package/network bootstrap or document-selected executable flags. Without an actual OS sandbox, TeX may still read files or consume resources: only explicitly trusted input may use this route. Untrusted preview/HTML stays with bundled math renderers.
- Regression tests for malicious Markdown, shell snippets, SQL writes, unsafe LaTeX flags, path traversal and export-induced network/script execution.

| Slice | Status | Evidence / known limits |
|---|---|---|
| 8a HTML (owned by Phase 2) | Complete on primary OS | [Phase 2 evidence and limits](#phase-2-reader-completion-and-gates); other platforms Not Run |
| 8b PDF | Complete for tested primary-OS admitted scope | Real Pandoc/Typst/TeX output, native selected revision/export, Preview/Open/Print dialog, regressions and normal release pass; platform/tool/physical-print matrix remains |
| 8c optional shell/SQLite/local TeX | Complete for tested primary-OS admitted scope | Default-off permissions, root trust, native confirmation, actual output/rollback/cancel, regressions and normal release pass; normal OS privileges and limits below |

Acceptance criteria:

- Document block/local-TeX execution never runs without its own enable/trust/manual/native-confirmation path. Calling a disabled runner via IPC fails. SQLite readonly refuses writes and side effects under real authorizer/connection tests, and cancellation rolls back unfinished writes as documented.
- Standalone HTML exports offline without Pandoc; correctly configured local Pandoc produces real PDF and responds clearly to unavailable tooling or unsupported math.
- Resource and security limits are documented and tested; claiming shell or local TeX execution is sandboxed requires evidence of a real sandbox.

Status:
- Implementation and automated acceptance complete for the tested macOS ARM64 admitted scope (2026-11-13). Advertised-platform, physical-input, security/fault and sustained-performance certification remain open below. Phase 9 is not started.

Completion notes:
- Reviewed Phase 0–7 completion/deviation notes: immutable native snapshots and exact byte provenance, explicit grants, durable Source ACK barriers, recovered Save As, local lazy renderers, conservative review identity, default-off execution and normal-release isolation remain binding.
- Local host has Pandoc 3.13, Typst 0.15.1 and TeX Live 2026. No tool installation is assumed or performed. Actual PDF/converter tests will use explicitly selected local tools; other platforms and physical Print/IME/accessibility remain separate evidence.

Deviation notes:
- P8-001 — Keep the existing paper `#fafbf9`, forest ink `#24352f`, accent `#316c57`, quiet `#66736d`, line `#dce3df`, surface `#f0f4f1` and Palatino/system/source font roles. A lazy “Export & run” dialog uses a revision summary, separate PDF and execution sections, and plain bounded output. Left-aligned existing controls and separators express actual workflow groups. No dashboard/cards or new visual identity. This was reviewed against the existing reader brief before implementation.
- P8-002 — Native-selected tool/database/root handles and native-captured revisions only. No frontend path, executable/options, arbitrary SQL or shell string bridge. Root trust and global enablement are separate for shell, SQLite and trusted TeX; default off on every restart. Literal settings/Markdown cannot grant trust. Every execution requires a new native confirmation displaying exact captured code/revision/cwd and selected tool/database; approval is single-use.
- P8-003 — PDF authorization is independent of block execution. Pandoc parses the selected bounded native snapshot to JSON with raw/metadata extensions off; a native closed AST policy removes unsafe links/raw content and rewrites only native-selected image grants into private staging. An app-owned Typst template and fixed argument vectors produce PDF under a private root. TeX PDF/local TeX needs the additional trusted-TeX enable/root/per-job path; no package bootstrap, document-selected templates/filters or shell escape.
- P8-004 — SQLite uses bundled native SQLite via rusqlite, a separate explicitly selected local DB, readonly open/query-only by default, defensive/untrusted-schema settings, an authorizer with a closed function/action policy and bounded work/output. Optional DML writes use a native-confirmed transaction with rollback on failure/cancellation. No ATTACH, extensions, user pragmas, schema changes or metadata-DB reuse; never immutable mode on a mutable DB.
- P8-005 — Shell and trusted local TeX use fixed tool roles and a deliberate script/job file, bounded output/time/process resources, process-group cancellation and private temporary files. They retain normal user privileges; root trust, TeX flags and Typst/Pandoc restrictions are not claimed as a general OS sandbox. Detached descendants and noncooperating filesystem races remain explicitly qualified.
- P8-006 — Finish targeted hostile-input/rollback/cancel/tool/PDF tests, real native UI flows, full regressions and normal release isolation/resources/signature checks. Keep all evidence and deviations here, with non-Markdown fixtures/JSON allowed. Phase 9 remains unstarted.


Implementation and admission record:

- **Scope and presentation:** `Export & run` and its palette command load the new dialog on demand. PDF and Document fences are separate sections with an explicit saved-open-snapshot/working-editor capture selector, native revision/hash label and bounded output. The native service constructor does no tool discovery, DB opening, capture or job I/O. Source/Split/Visual retain their ordinary CodeMirror state, undo and provenance. No second editor or background runner was added.
- **Native captures:** At most four immutable UTF-8 captures of 2 MiB, without NUL, 50,000 raw CR/CRLF/LF logical lines. Saved bytes come from the existing private immutable document grant; working bytes come from native provenance at the exact received and durable-ACK revision. Original target/name come from the editor, and any supplied document grant must match that target. Recovered/app-owned captures can export to a new PDF destination but cannot execute before Save As. Runnable offers are native verified closed top-level backtick/tilde fences (`sh/bash/shell/zsh`, `sql/sqlite`, `tex/latex`), up to 100 offers and 64 KiB per body; execution confirmation further admits 8 KiB. Nested/indented/blockquote and unclosed fences are source-only. No code string is accepted by a Run IPC command. Executable fence copies normalize raw CRLF/CR to LF, explicitly stated in the native confirmation; the original capture bytes/ranges/hash and Markdown file remain unchanged. A real CRLF shell flow verifies the expected output filename and exact original file bytes.
- **Authority:** Shell, SQLite and trusted TeX have separate app-session global enablement, native-selected trusted-folder handles and a new native confirmation for each exact captured job. Enablement and trust expire on restart and cannot be imported from literal preferences or Markdown. There is one current folder per kind, one selected executable per role and one selected DB. Replacing a selection invalidates pending uses of its previous opaque handle. Native preparation/one-shot tokens bind code, source byte range, full capture hash, tool/version, DB, folder and actual process cwd; tokens must be submitted within five minutes and new preparations refuse captures older than 30 minutes. After confirmation the service rechecks capture authority, current native source proof, enablement, selected handles and path identities. Saved replacements and stale working ACK revisions refuse execution. Working Run first settles and durably flushes pending frontend edits, so unacknowledged N+1 cannot silently validate an N capture.
- **Selected tools:** Native executable picker plus a native trust prompt precedes the version command. Supported role names are `sh/bash/zsh`, `pandoc`, `typst`, `pdflatex/xelatex/lualatex`; installed aliases/formats are resolved and validated, with fixed argument vectors. Tested host: Pandoc 3.13, Typst 0.15.1, XeLaTeX/TeX Live 2026. Admission checks Pandoc 3.x / Typst 0.x and required CLI behavior; other minor versions are not certified. Executables admit up to 512 MiB. The selected inode is held, and device/inode/size/mtime/ctime and original selected path revalidate around version use and before launch. A 128 MiB snapshot limit initially rejected the real 276 MiB Pandoc; copying/repeatedly hashing it was replaced by native identity pinning. This is version/path/identity validation, not code signing or an atomic guarantee against same-user binary modification in the final validate/exec interval.
- **PDF pipeline:** PDF tools do not enable any runner. The selected native capture is parsed by fixed Pandoc Markdown extensions to JSON in a private directory, with raw HTML/TeX/attributes and metadata blocks disabled. A native closed AST policy removes external links, renders raw/unsafe math as source, clears attributes except bounded image dimensions, and replaces unapproved images with explicit placeholders. JSON is bounded to 16 MiB, 100,000 nodes and depth 64. App-owned Typst/TeX templates and fixed CLI options avoid repository defaults, filters, arbitrary flags, includes or tools. Typst compiles under the private root with private package paths; no package import is introduced from document data. PDF uses Pandoc's admitted Markdown interpretation, not a promise of exact parity with every custom Read extension.
- **Assets and formatting:** Up to 16 native-selected/validated raster assets, 24 MiB aggregate per capture, and ten local Mermaid render jobs. PNG/JPEG retain the existing 8 MiB / 4,096-side / 16-megapixel admission. Mermaid uses the existing strict local renderer/sanitizer, then a bounded canvas PNG (2,048 pixels per side); native code validates the raster and binds it to the captured source hash. Unsupported diagrams remain labeled source with diagnostics. Image dimensions admit positive bounded pixels (4,096) or percentages (100). The tool input includes no remote/unapproved resource path. Templates bound TeX tables/images, keep figures in source order, wrap long code, and support TOC, Unicode and footnotes. Typst is the ordinary PDF path; Unicode TeX PDF uses xelatex/lualatex and requires separately enabled/trusted TeX, including a new job confirmation. `pdflatex` remains available for explicit standalone trusted TeX fences. Installed fonts/packages remain tool prerequisites; no font/package installation or broad cross-engine typography parity is claimed.
- **Output and system viewer:** A result PDF must start with `%PDF-` and fit 32 MiB. Native export uses the existing exclusive byte writer and a new native-selected destination, refusing existing files and app-owned/private paths. It never overwrites Markdown or restores an old path grant. Open accepts only an opaque produced/exported PDF handle and revalidates exact bytes/hash, then invokes the fixed system viewer (Preview on macOS, system xdg-open path on Linux). Preview's actual two-page A4 Print dialog was observed and canceled; no printer was selected and no physical job was submitted. Test viewer documents were closed. HTML's existing standalone CSP, sanitizer and embedded-font notices remain independent and regression covered.
- **SQLite enforcement:** `rusqlite 0.41.1` with bundled SQLite 3.54.1, hooks and limits; a separate native-selected regular single-link DB up to 256 MiB, never the app metadata store. Canonical original paths, pinned inode/parent validation and no-follow SQLite open are checked before/after open and before commit. Readonly flags plus query-only mode enforce the default; mutable DBs are never labeled immutable. Defensive/untrusted-schema settings, disabled views/triggers and a closed authorizer deny ATTACH/DETACH, user pragmas, extensions, schema mutations, transactions/savepoints and unlisted functions. Writes admit DML only and refuse a DB containing triggers rather than silently bypassing them; foreign-key enforcement stays on. Recursive CTEs are allowed under the budget while views stay disabled. A lexical single-statement admission precedes prepare to bound recursive tail parsing; permissions are enforced by the connection/authorizer, not keyword filtering.
- **SQLite bounds and cancellation:** 64 KiB SQL, 64 columns, 1 MiB value/output, 500 rows, expression depth 32, eight compound terms, 250,000 compiled VM ops, 32 function arguments/variables, zero attached DBs/worker threads, 256-character LIKE pattern, 250 ms lock wait and a five-second progress deadline/cancel flag checked every 1,000 VM instructions. Query output pages 25 rows and 1,024 displayed characters/cell. Write mode starts an immediate transaction, revalidates before commit and rolls back on error, output capacity or cancellation before commit; completed commits are not undone by later cancellation. Tests verify real WAL reads and actual DML mutations rolled back after output capacity and a late cancel signal. Filesystem/WAL bookkeeping is not a claim of immutable DB bytes or a hard whole-process memory limit.
- **Shell/TeX process bounds:** One running native job and eight retained job results. Deliberate private script/TeX files, fixed tool argument arrays, cleared inherited environment, private HOME/temp/package paths, no TeX shell escape and no mktex format/font bootstrap. Shell cwd uses the pinned trusted-folder FD; TeX/PDF work in private staging, explicitly shown in the native confirmation. Output is capped at 256 KiB per stream, shell/TeX processes at 30 seconds each, Pandoc passes at 20 seconds, version probes at five seconds; CPU/file-size/core limits supplement deadlines. TeX runs twice for references/TOC. Timeout/output capacity/cancel stop the attached process group and avoid waiting indefinitely on detached pipe holders. Quit refuses until local jobs are canceled/completed. These controls are not an OS sandbox: trusted scripts/TeX may use normal filesystem/network privileges, detach descendants, or leave completed changes. Cancel does not undo user-script file changes.
- **Storage and failure limits:** Staging is owner-only and removed on normal job completion; job results/captures are ephemeral and clearable, with no automatic restart/resume or restored trust. A crash can leave a private `jobs` staging directory requiring manual cleanup; no crash recovery of local jobs is promised. The 512 MiB private-store preflight used by durable app records remains, but trusted external jobs are not a globally reserved storage/memory sandbox. Native path checks cannot provide atomic compare-and-swap against a noncooperating process replacing an executable or SQLite namespace at the final open/commit boundary. Capacity, tool, permission and conversion failures retain Source/review/recovery authority; results are not reported as saved until the exclusive export succeeds.

Verification ledger:

- `pnpm check`: 81 frontend tests, zero Svelte errors/warnings, lint and formatting pass. Strict all-target Clippy and Rust formatting pass. Full Rust suite: 119 passed, zero failures, two ignored helpers; the crash worker is exercised by both crash parents and the installed-tool helper was separately invoked successfully. Six new ordinary native tests cover raw fence coordinates/LF execution copies/authority, real process output/timeout/cancel, closed AST policy, SQLite readonly/WAL/DML and rollback after actual mutations/capacity/late cancel.
- The explicit ignored local-tool test passes with installed Pandoc/Typst/XeLaTeX: real rich/hostile captured Markdown produces PDFs; the source secret does not appear; TeX shell escape does not create its marker. Poppler text/structure/render inspection covers formulas, table/TOC/footnote, image dimensions, Arabic/CJK/Greek and no PDF JavaScript. Both native templates were rendered and visually inspected; actual local Mermaid raster output was verified in the WebView-exported PDF and Preview.
- Full shipping WKWebView suite passes 43 cases, zero failures, with one development-only Pierre probe skipped (7m17s, `artifacts/phase-8-native-full.log`). Three targeted actual WKWebView flows also pass in 10 seconds (`artifacts/phase-8-native-targeted-verified.log`): independent PDF/default-off/approved-image/diagram/export/collision/system-open; shell enable cancel/trust/single-use/stale saved/working/cancel and unchanged dirty Source; SQLite readonly denial/confirmed DML/canceled rollback, separately trusted TeX and forged handle refusals. Native picker/confirmation choices are injected by the debug-only fixture; WebView, filesystem, process and SQLite work are real. This does not certify physical selection or the app's actual native confirmation-dialog usability at maximum code size.
- Earlier targeted runs passed 1/3 then 2/3. Fixes retained: start clears the prior result before awaiting preparation; CTE access is distinguished from disabled views; working execution settles pending local Source edits before native proof checks. PDF tests exposed real installed-tool size, TeX format/program-name and missing template helpers, and staged diagram revalidation issues, all corrected before final targeted acceptance. The earlier logs remain diagnostic evidence rather than passing suites. After the full 43-case pass, a final capture/result display reset was verified by all three affected native flows (10.1s), followed by the explicit LF execution-copy policy and a real CRLF shell fixture (three native flows, 10.9s; full Rust/Clippy repeated successfully). Selecting a new capture clears the previous job result/export action, so it cannot appear under a newer revision label. These final scoped checks are distinguished from the earlier full-suite build. Final visual QA corrected an inherited 420 px dialog cap and a user-visible deprecated Pandoc flag: the dialog now measures 841 px content width at a 1,080 px viewport without overflow; the modern syntax-highlighting option passes the installed-tool helper and all three affected native flows (11s).
- New Rust dependency inventory has 454 records, including target-specific support crates; production JavaScript stays at 182. MIT/public-domain notices were collected for rusqlite, libsqlite3-sys/SQLite and support crates in `src/power-notices.txt`, included by the ordinary notice generator. Pandoc/Typst/TeX remain separately installed tools, not bundled executables or automatic downloads. Final advisory/license/distribution audit remains Phase 9.
- Not Run: Linux WebKitGTK/X11/Wayland and Intel macOS; physical keyboard/OS IME/VoiceOver; maximum-length native confirmation layout; physical printing (no printer selected); detached-descendant containment and app-crash cleanup/recovery; hostile SQLite schema/WAL peak-memory and cross-filesystem fault/mount matrix; installed-tool/version/font/package matrices beyond this host; sustained job/reader interaction latency/memory plateau, Developer ID/notarization and portable distribution. No broad sandbox, product latency SLA or advertised-platform acceptance is claimed.


Normal release and completion:

- Final normal `pnpm tauri build --bundles app` succeeds. `pnpm check:release` passes normal frontend/config/generated ACL/executable/Cargo graph isolation; test server/evaluator/fixture hooks and the Pierre probe are absent. Deep strict ad-hoc signature verification and ARM64 architecture checks pass. The bundled specimen and complete generated notices are byte-identical to repository resources. Final executable SHA-256 `492170a56327973c7b36aba4813e3b26332123698c23b7e4edd91b292eab06f9`, 18,072,992 bytes; app bundle 18,576,724 bytes (17.72 MiB). Normal output has 99 JavaScript chunks; the lazy Export & run panel chunk is 15,948 bytes / 6,601 gzip, with existing shared grammar/rich/UI modules additional.
- Final serial timing uses that same binary hash after all builds/checks/rendering: first smoke 1,229.3 ms; next 20 launches median 365.9 ms, nearest-rank p95 440.7 ms. Process cold, filesystem cache uncontrolled, no explicit OS/XPC cooldown; page-load event only, not first interactive paint. Earlier overlapping/pre-final timing trials are excluded. Phase 7 was 2,195.8 / 656.5 / 834.1 ms under its recorded conditions; this observation is not an attributed optimization or a startup SLA pass. Final default three-second footprint is 72,436,736 bytes (69.08 MiB) physical / 196,788,224 bytes (187.67 MiB) RSS sum, with helper lifecycle confirmed and no footprint errors. No optional job is active in that point sample; it is not a job peak/idle plateau. G8 remains open.
- Evidence: `artifacts/publication-history/docs/evidence/phase-8-macos-arm64.json`, with raw logs, native UI screenshots, valid PDF fixtures and measurements listed there. The final sequence is explicit: full 43-case native regression; scoped lifecycle/LF/presentation corrections followed by all three affected native cases, full Rust checks for the execution-policy change, actual installed-tool verification and final normal release isolation. Frontend checks remain 81 passing tests; full Rust 119 passing with both ignored helpers exercised through their documented paths; all target Clippy/fmt pass.
- Phase 8's admitted implementation/automated acceptance is complete. Source exact-byte saves/undo, durable journal barriers, recovered Save As, explicit backend grants, independent HTML/Review/Git paths and release isolation retain coverage. PDF/runner captures are bounded and do not silently discard or reauthorize editor state. Native selections and explicit confirmations are real controls; debug fixture injection does not certify physical input or full native prompt layout. Remaining host/tool/fault/accessibility/security/performance limitations above are retained. No new Markdown files, tool installation or commits; Phase 9 is not started.

### Phase 9: Performance hardening, accessibility, packaging, release review

Deliverables:

- Profile production application on macOS and Linux across empty shell, Read, Source, Split, Visual, Git diff, math/diagrams, huge files, multi-tab, tab-close and idle scenarios. Record whole-process memory, PSS/footprint, startup bundles, CPU wakeups, DOM nodes, IPC traffic and frame/input latency.
- Apply targeted optimizations: lazy chunks/workers, cache eviction, paged reader, selective code grammars, one WebView, per-view rendering budgets, release of images/diagram resources and background-worker cancellation. Avoid time-consuming rewrites without profile evidence.
- Accessibility audit: keyboard navigation and traps, screen reader labels, aria/focus on file manager and diffs, contrast and reduced motion, math alternatives, Arabic/Hebrew BiDi and CJK IME in actual WebKit.
- Packaging begins in Phase 0 and repeats per milestone. Baseline deliverables: macOS `.app`/`.dmg` for ARM64/Intel and one declared Linux `.deb` distribution/architecture with WebKitGTK dependencies, `.desktop`/MIME association. `.rpm`, AppImage and Flatpak are additional evaluated formats, not simultaneous release blockers. Test Linux Wayland and X11, HiDPI, clipboard, file drag/drop, OS dialogs, CLI/file-association open with spaces/non-ASCII, and delivery to an existing app instance. App Store sandbox distribution is out of scope unless explicitly added.
- Public macOS distribution requires a deliberate signing/notarization decision and credentials, which are not assumed present. Build/test unsigned local artifacts first; do not label them signed/notarized or broadly distributable. Record ownership of release credentials and any blocked release step. Linux packages need clean-machine install/launch tests; a successful compile is insufficient.
- Complete dependency/security/license audits, reproducible CI, README/quickstart, compatibility/security/performance docs, recovery guide, credits, changelog and known limitations. Disable/fix broken actions rather than ship fake controls.
- Full user-journey acceptance on actual files, including no-op save, rich visual patches, file bulk operations, hunk stage, external agent replacement, execution-off invariant and HTML/PDF export.

Acceptance criteria:

- All 20 requirements have a supported status and evidence; incomplete scope is explicitly marked, with owner-approved exceptions rather than silent omissions.
- Startup/typing/large-file/per-block/render/idle memory measurements are reproducible; overhead relative to empty Tauri and regressions relative to prior app releases are recorded. A loaded app is not expected to consume less than its empty shell. No false "native means no WebView" or unsupported tiny-memory claims.
- Regular use is offline, no remote scripts/fonts/telemetry, optional runners remain default-off, and malicious document content does not acquire privileged commands.
- Installed production builds smoke-test successfully on available macOS and Linux targets, and untested OS variants are disclosed accurately.

Status:
- In progress for release qualification. The available-Mac hardening checkpoint below passes 787 frontend, 148 Rust, 14 Node and six Python tooling tests, strict Clippy, 53 actual WKWebView cases (before the final narrow PDF catalogue correction), installed converter checks, isolated actual SIGKILL/Restore/independent Save As, and final normal ARM64 packaging/native four-tab lifecycle checks. Final PDF-policy changes pass the complete native suite and installed converters. Strict advisories, unavailable platforms, owner-declined OS-setting tests and sustained performance/distribution qualifications remain open. The new final-binary Git UI check awaits explicit repository-grant approval after automatic review rejection. Paid Apple signing/notarization is excluded by owner choice; no public release waiver.

Implementation breakdown and decisions:

- P9-001 — Re-read the Phase 0–8 completion/deviation ledgers. Retain all provenance, native grant, recovery, closed runner, Review/Git and release-isolation limits. No new Markdown files or new architecture stack.
- P9-002 — Audit production npm and the complete Rust lockfile against current advisories, including informational unsound/unmaintained notices. Generate complete available Rust license texts as a conservative resolved-graph superset, record missing notices explicitly, and verify shipped resources. No legal-clearance claim from declarations alone.
- P9-003 — Implement bounded native OS/CLI document-open requests and single-instance handoff with the official Tauri plugin. Only native-origin local Markdown/text files; no frontend path command or URL scheme. Register editor associations without claiming the default. Preserve dirty tabs, refuse app-owned targets, serialize UI delivery and expose queue/capacity failures.
- P9-004 — Audit keyboard semantics/focus, dialog behavior, reduced motion, contrast, local math/diagram alternatives and BiDi. Fix concrete issues and exercise actual WKWebView automation. Synthetic keys/composition and DOM checks are not real VoiceOver/IME certification.
- P9-005 — Collect release artifact/startup/whole-process measurements against a refreshed blank baseline, plus admitted native interaction/close-cycle observations. Profile results govern optimizations; unavailable production workload/CPU/wakeup/frame/input measures remain open rather than inferred from debug samples.
- P9-006 — Build local ARM64 app/DMG, inspect/mount/copy/launch disposable installation, verify associations/handoff and normal release isolation; prepare pinned CI for declared Ubuntu 24.04 amd64/X11 and ARM64/Intel macOS. No Linux host/Docker daemon or signing credentials are assumed. Update existing README/NOTICE and the 20-requirement ledger with actual status.

- P9-007 — Follow-up authorized by the repeated Phase 9 instruction: retain the previous release as an A/B control, trace native startup stages with explicit local opt-in timing only, and separate initial queueing from existing-instance window focus. Compare interleaved same-host page-load timings; do not replace interactive-paint certification with page-load claims.
- P9-008 — Cancel queued rich jobs when their owning view becomes stale, retain KaTeX MathML/decorative SVG semantics, and correct concurrent cache accounting. Exercise stale queued jobs and repeated native open/mode/close cycles while preserving ordinary CM view/undo/grants. Record bounded observations and unresolved physical/platform gates here.

- P9-009 — Empty-shell dependency review found Reader -> parser/sanitizer/export code was statically imported even with no document. Load the Reader component only when a document/working Read needs it, with the existing busy reservation and render-generation fence. Close/failure rejects pending and future reader requests and clears approved assets; no Source/CM state reconstruction or provenance change. A/B timings evaluate the combined startup/lazy-load change, not an individual causal claim.

- P9-010 — Production close-cycle testing exposed repeated native snapshot refusals after 15 successful opens, while fixture bytes remained exact. The file carries OS-maintained last-used metadata; the specific interleaving is not proven. Add at most two retries for an observed ctime-only race during read-only snapshot capture, retaining the same original FD and requiring all strict metadata/path/two-hash checks on the successful attempt. Never retry save fingerprinting, adopt a replacement inode, or retry changed length/mtime/content silently. Preserve the failed run and certify the change through the native integrity and UI suites before rebuilding.

- P9-011 — WebKit-content retention review found the compatibility math path rebuilt its complete SVG font data for every expression. The pinned MathJax `CommonOutputJax` explicitly supports a FontData instance. Share that glyph-metric instance while retaining fresh TeX input, SVG output and document per expression; fontCache remains none and no macro/label state is shared. A cross-expression local-macro regression plus affected native renderer/export/author cases gates this allocation change. This is an allocation optimization, not an attribution of all observed WebKit growth.

- P9-012 — Separate native selection node identity from content-version validation. Keep a no-follow canonical file descriptor alive while comparing device/inode through the pinned parent, preventing inode reuse during this binding. Metadata-only OS bookkeeping may proceed; replacement nodes still refuse. The subsequent snapshot retains its strict metadata/two-hash checks and bounded ctime-only retry. No new path authority, write retry or generic bridge is introduced. Test metadata change and replacement at this exact binding boundary.

- P9-013 — Renewed Phase 9 review expands whole/paged differential checks to complete heading metadata, rich-placeholder semantics, CRLF boundaries and adversarial block pairs. New tests reproduce premature finalization of a reference continuation title and duplicate heading/footnote IDs. Hold partial terminal lines (including a terminal CR), allocate collision-free ordered heading IDs, reserve final footnote anchors and replace provisional heading metadata on the final worker message. Include the appendix in index capacity checks. Preserve byte ranges, bounded source fallbacks and finalized-only export; no Source authority or new command. Record initial failures and verify native outline, late-footnote navigation and actual standalone export before claiming the fixes.

- P9-014 — Extend the external production profiler with opt-in macOS `proc_pid_rusage` CPU/package-idle/interrupt counters, retaining raw per-process values, Mach timebase and process incarnation IDs. Reject incomplete interval attribution rather than zero-fill missing/reused PIDs. Cross-check CPU conversion against POSIX getrusage on this host before app sampling. Keep this code entirely outside the app: no evaluator, new IPC command, app trace endpoint or resource grant. Compare normal blank/empty/loaded traces without concurrent builds; distinguish these kernel wakeup categories from physical energy, UI events or compositor latency.

- P9-015 — Continue Phase 9 against the current handoff. The unowned helper is the same historical process, but native `launchctl procinfo` requires OS root; prior interactive totals remain inconclusive. Independently remediate verified development advisory versions with narrow exact overrides: Mocha → serialize-javascript 7.1.3, get-uri → basic-ftp 6.2.4, vulnerable glob 9–14.0.1 → already-used 14.0.4, and vulnerable minimatch 7–11.0.0 → already-used 11.0.8. Review the changed APIs/Node requirements and exercise actual consumer boundaries, Mocha parallel workers, frontend checks and the native suite. Compare the production dependency inventory before/after; keep driver auto-download/build disabled, retain raw audits and strict unresolved-advisory failure. Do not claim FTP/network/download functionality or a platform waiver from these tests.

- P9-016 — Add optional, development-only contemporaneous helper-responsibility proofs to the external sampler. The private read-only `responsibility_get_pid_responsible_for_pid` SPI is feature-detected and bracketed by kernel process/owner incarnation checks. Exclude only positively foreign owners; unknown, changed, new-during-capture, ancestry-conflicting or pre-existing same-owner helpers refuse admission. Shared responsibility is not sole inclusion proof: prelaunch birth and post-exit helper checks remain mandatory. Keep the default legacy method and historical rejected records unchanged; no app command, root privilege, entitlement, runtime dependency or release hook is added. Validate classifier failure cases and collect fresh actual production-view observations before admitting their totals.

- P9-017 — Fresh native UI profiling exposed an obsolete success message after repository revocation and a later cancelled picker. Clear the old diagnostic and explicitly report session access revocation after the native revoke succeeds. Extend the existing actual-native Git journey at that boundary. Do not infer any grant persistence from the stale text or claim the stalled native folder-picker retry as a successful Git profile; no repository mutation was performed.

#### Phase 9 renewed parity and external profiling (2026-11-13)

- Reviewed the Phase 0–8 completion/deviation notes and the current Phase 9 evidence before advancing this remaining work. Only the owner-confirmed Apple Silicon Mac is available. No platform waiver or later-phase scope is inferred.
- The expanded differential suite initially fails three cases: a reference continuation title prematurely committed from a partial last line; a literal heading such as `Same-1` colliding with the suffix of repeated `Same`; and a late footnote `a` colliding with heading `Note-61`. Those failures are retained in `artifacts/phase-9-parity-initial.log`. P9-013 fixes the observed paths, includes complete heading metadata and rich-placeholder semantics in comparison, and exercises 300 block-pair/read-width combinations, 35 BOM/Unicode/CRLF widths and real section crossings. A 100,000-heading/footnote-appendix capacity regression refuses finalization instead of exceeding the index budget. The documented oversized-source fallbacks and expression-local math limit remain.
- All **93 frontend tests** pass, with zero Svelte diagnostics, typechecking, lint and formatting. The targeted actual WKWebView suite passes **10 cases** (19.2s), including native outline/late-footnote navigation and finalized standalone export with unique IDs/title context and exact original source bytes. The initial native run passed the product assertions but failed its final byte assertion because the debug fixture getter intentionally omits text above 64 KiB; the test now reads its owned fixture path using the test runner. #### Local release-gap closure (2026-11-14)

- P9-018 — Target the sole native `main` WebView explicitly through the pinned driver's window-label API. Its automatic focus recovery currently compares the changing HTML title to the constant native title and repeatedly searches/fails. Keep all functional/byte/grant assertions and conditional waits; compare complete-suite timings and retained wait distributions. Removing harness noise is not a production input-latency optimization.
- P9-019 — Enforce the existing expression-local math contract at the shared adapter. Context-dependent labels/references/global definitions/automatic numbering must retain source with a specific diagnostic in Read, Visual and exports rather than silently appear as correctly numbered/resolved output. Local definitions within one expression and explicit tags remain supported. Ordered document-wide math is not invented from regex substitution or shared mutable viewport state; retained feature scope remains explicit.
- P9-020 — Current authoritative npm advisories still publish no patched extract-zip/deepmerge version. Apply narrow reproducible local patches: refuse archive symlinks/existing output-file replacement and filter prototype-mutating keys on both deepmerge inputs. Exercise real consumer resolution, malicious archive fixtures, ordinary extraction and Svelte config/build behavior. Strict registry audit stays nonzero for upstream affected versions; local mitigation is not an ignored advisory or release waiver. Rust GTK3 advisories remain external-stack constraints unless a compatible supported fix is available.
- P9-021 — Extend feasible native fault checks for SQLite hostile schemas/WAL, Review record quota/failure isolation and Git unsupported/stress states; preserve native ACL, exact byte/history/grant behavior and bounded failure paths. Fix concrete defects revealed by those checks. Exercise a disposable second local APFS filesystem; physical/removable/network and other-host cases stay Not Run.
- P9-023 — A faster native input path reproduced cold Preferences draft loss: metadata discovery left controls editable, then a late scope load overwrote entered text. Reserve loading synchronously, disable editing fields while loading, ignore stale/cancelled metadata and select scope explicitly before loading it. Preserve the existing CM state/undo/journal barriers. Native recovery/preferences/theme/caret regressions gate the fix.
- P9-024 — The fixed driver removes warning noise but late 23–35s waits persist. Measure repeated native recovery-list replay; if confirmed, cache only non-authoritative listing summaries behind bounded complete folder/file identity fingerprints. A changed/tampered archive must invalidate; live sessions stay excluded; restore/Save As always replay/revalidate. No marker-only trust, disk garbage collection, grant change or Source/history shortcut. Baseline/after measurements and full native replay remain required.
- P9-025 — Receipt-backed Undo marks Source temporarily non-editable but did not participate in the pane's operation guard. The faster full run exposed Save overtaking that pending undo. Include native-receipt undo state in synchronous pane/command/close guards and delayed operation feedback; preserve the same CM view/Text and existing ACK/save barriers. Native tests retain original exact-byte assertions and wait for the real enabled controls. Test cleanup must await reserved tab close; the final math case owns its clean Read setup.
- P9-026 — Fast sequential tab closes exposed a shell-level reservation gap: the pane finished before session persistence, while the shell silently ignored a second still-enabled close. Make the shell closing key reactive, disable close controls through the complete close/cancel lifecycle, and exclude a closing empty pane from native-open reuse. Navigation tasks for already closed grants must not surface spurious authorization errors. Preserve dirty cancellation and prior active-tab selection. Test cleanup awaits the actual modal or retired tab and re-queries current nodes; native library mutation waits for its provisional search to finish, retaining the no-running-job guard.
- P9-027 — Native search results can be opened before scanning finishes. If a completed job is replaced before its final UI poll, the old job handle becomes stale and the Library previously retained Working/disabled mutation controls indefinitely. Poll generations must ignore superseded responses and reconcile a stale poll with the canonical native active job (or completed/no-active state); preserve the native no-running-job/no-live-Source mutation gates and selection ownership. Real native search/direct-mutation/UI-create/revoke cases gate the fix. Readiness assertions re-query current pane/tab nodes after close, rather than observing detached nodes.
- P9-022 — Re-run relevant checks/full native regression, normal release isolation/package smoke and bounded same-host resource observations after final code changes. Production measurements must remain bound to their executable, with helper ownership/shutdown and unchanged fixtures. No evaluator or profiling endpoint enters normal release. Classify every remaining item by local work completed, measured with limits, or external/physical access required.
