#### Publication preparation (2026-11-14)

Status: Complete for the stated local publication-preparation scope; Phase 9 release qualification remains In progress. Owner requests all feasible local follow-up and a clean source repository for publication. Retain the closed command/grant, exact-byte/recovery and native verification contracts. Public source preparation does not waive unsigned/cross-platform/physical-input/security/performance release gates.

- P9-028 — Supervise optional native tools in a separate exec-created copy of the same binary, before Tauri/WebView initialization. Use an anonymous inherited Unix socket with a bounded token-checked request and an ephemeral selected-directory FD; expose no new Tauri command, path grant, named socket or network listener. Socket EOF on app crash must stop the attached job group. Revalidate executable node/timestamps after the helper handoff before launch. Observe child exit without reaping before group cleanup to avoid recycled-PID signaling. Preserve output/time/resource bounds, native trust/confirmation and default-off behavior. Deliberately detached/hostile same-user processes remain outside an OS-sandbox guarantee. Test real parent SIGKILL, cancellation, output/time limits and normal completion. Apple's fork/exec restriction rules out a long-lived fork-only guardian inside the Cocoa app ([Apple threading guidance](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Cocoa/Conceptual/Multithreading/AboutThreads/AboutThreads.html)).
- P9-029 — Archive superseded Phase 0 prototypes, rejected Pierre probe/dependency, temporary click tracing and host-specific phase reports outside the publishable source inventory. Keep maintained tests, reproducible benchmark tools, actual native fixture support gated out of release, required notices and security rationale. Remove phase-history comments from runtime code; keep phase decisions and evidence summaries here. Gate startup timing output behind explicit profiling opt-in.
- P9-030 — Add a deterministic publication inventory check and source archive excluding build/cache outputs, generated ACL files, local agent/credential files and historical machine reports. Scan candidate text for private keys/tokens and the local home path without echoing secrets. Verify a fresh extraction/frozen install/build and normal release isolation. Preserve local historical evidence and verified packages. The source snapshot itself does not commit or publish anything. A local initial commit may record the verified source for the requested clean working tree; no push, public upload or release waiver is authorized.

Completion/deviation notes:

Root/socket descriptors are duplicated above standard streams before exec and restored to CLOEXEC before tool launch. No new frontend command, path authority or listener. Native waitid observes exit without reaping before group termination, retaining PID ownership through cleanup.

This supersedes the earlier attached-job app-crash limitation only; hostile detached jobs and externally killed supervisors remain outside the guarantee and tools retain normal user privileges.

### Phase 10: Everyday workflow and interface polish

Owner authorization: the request to start Phase 10, clarified as finishing and polishing the app (2026-11-13). This phase improves the existing product; Phase 9's unresolved release qualifications remain in Phase 9 and are not waived or reclassified.

Status:
- Complete for the defined initial and modern UX polish checkpoints on tested macOS ARM64. All 20 requirements are accounted for; Phase 9 release acceptance and the explicitly listed unfinished checks remain open.

Deliverables and acceptance:
- A compact document toolbar with visible modes and save actions, and an accessible More disclosure for secondary document actions. Escape/outside interaction closes it; dialog focus returns to the disclosure. Controls remain reachable at narrow pane widths and with the library open. Preserve Source view/Text, selection, byte provenance, undo and journal barriers.
- A useful New tab screen with Open file, Open folder, the existing example and at most six recent-file shortcuts. Only native-authorized recent handles may open; remembered unauthorized paths lead to explicit folder selection. No new filesystem permission or automatic reauthorization.
- Predictable tabs: native/library/recent opens reuse an existing empty tab before capacity refusal, closing a background tab preserves the active document, cancelled close preserves the prior selection, and persisted session selection is mapped through filtered real document IDs. No changes to the four-tab resource ceiling or document release/save semantics.
- Native regressions for home/grants, tab/session state, same-editor undo/provenance and disclosure keyboard/focus; screenshots/geometry at ordinary/narrow widths and with a sidebar. Full frontend/tooling and native regression checks, final normal ARM64 packaging/isolation and copied-app smoke. Other-platform and physical accessibility/IME qualification remains Not Run here.

Implementation decisions:
- P10-001 — Re-reviewed Phase 0–9 statuses, completion/deviation notes and final qualification handoff. Baseline native inspection confirms Source's primary toolbar wraps and stacked editing controls consume substantial vertical space at 1080 CSS pixels. Keep the existing document-first identity and local font/theme system. No dependency, stack, backend grant or Source transport changes.
- P10-002 — Design tokens remain the existing six typed theme tokens: paper `#fafbf9`, ink `#24352f`, quiet `#66736d`, accent `#316c57`, line `#dce3df`, surface `#f0f4f1` in Light, with existing dark/custom equivalents. Retain system UI fonts, Palatino reading and configured monospace Source. Main alignment is left; the start screen centers its bounded reading-width column. The document and its title supply identity; avoid decorative cards, marketing text or a new color system. Toolbar concept: `title | Read Source Split Visual | Save Save As | More`; editing tools use one bounded scrollable row. The home screen adds a simple recent-file list beneath native file/folder actions.
- P10-003 — Use semantic disclosure/buttons, not a faux ARIA menu. Secondary action selection closes the disclosure before invoking an existing handler. Accessible labels, keyboard focus and light/dark/custom token inheritance remain. Retain native Read focus/writing focus and command-palette access.
- P10-004 — Existing `library_state` supplies bounded recent hints; `library_open` rechecks native authority. Missing grants are visible and require native folder reselection. Reuse current library chooser instead of adding a path endpoint. Tab changes preserve stable keyed panes and never reconstruct an existing CodeMirror instance.

- P10-005 — Bind incoming opens to a stable tab key and reserve empty targets while they load. A later session-persistence failure must not release an already adopted live document grant. Keep cancellation/failed-close selection tied to the preceding active key. Persisted sessions map selected UI indices through real-document IDs; recovery and empty tabs grant no path authority.
- P10-006 — Actual-native checks reproduce two defects in the first implementation: disclosure closure hid the previously focused action before Preferences captured its return target, and the document main element lacked a definite flex layout for short Source content. Await the closed disclosure DOM before focusing its summary/opening the existing dialog; make document main a bounded flex column. A subsequent run confirms focus and 507/488 CSS-pixel editor heights at 960/640 widths, but rejects outside dismissal. Observe pointerdown and click activation in capture phase without consuming either event: editor handlers can stop bubbling and the embedded driver can activate through click-only events. Do not replace CodeMirror or weaken focus/geometry assertions.

- P10-007 — Full regression rejects a lost synchronous Reload reservation introduced by the universal disclosure tick. Only modal launches await the closed DOM/focus transfer; nonmodal Reload enters its existing reservation synchronously, before any competing Source handler. Preserve the original same-task Reload/Source native assertion. Four late-session home tests also failed to reach their document; add explicit ready/enabled/rendered conditions and failure snapshots, not unconditional waits or timeout relaxation. The focused Review → PDF/jobs → polish sequence passes all ten cases (21.1 s); final full regression follows.

- P10-008 — The next full run passes all 47 inherited native cases but rejects the four new tests in a loaded session. Failure snapshots show clicks while document-open/Source reservations are pending, and heading visibility precedes operation completion. Disable a tab’s close button while its existing pane/open reservation is busy, and make new journeys wait for native operation completion after visible content/Source, including the existing 60-second conditional readiness envelope. Do not treat a visible heading as an idle promise, add sleeps, waive failures or claim a latency SLA. Preserve per-case failure snapshots and rerun the complete suite.

- P10-009 — The reserved-close rerun passes 49 native cases and rejects a native-handoff assertion that used the default ten-second matcher before the expected document arrived; its late arrival then contaminates the next test. Express the unchanged expected-heading assertion as a bounded 60-second active-document condition, then await its completed reservation. Record the measured conditional wait durations; this is not a production latency pass. Keep the original byte, tab-count, selection and privilege assertions. Full-suite replay remains mandatory. New disposable Phase 10 file fixtures use `.txt`; no new repository Markdown documents are introduced.

- P10-010 — Disclosure Escape must not consume modal-dialog cancellation or IME composition keys. Ignore composing/229 events and open dialogs; close the disclosure when focus leaves it. Extend the native keyboard journey with synthetic composition guarding and palette-open/Escape cancellation. Synthetic composition is not physical IME or accessibility certification.

Baseline copied Phase 9 app was inspected and quit without edits or saves. Frontend initial check passes 97 tests plus four Node/six Python tooling checks and typecheck/lint/format. An initial TypeScript use of ES2023 findLastIndex was removed rather than raising the browser/library target.

### Agent working protocol (applies to every phase)

1. **Orient first:** inspect repo and this plan; do not remove existing work or invent preexisting features. Maintain Goal/Scope/Constraints unless the owner changes them. Record phase-specific implementation breakdown.
2. **Implement vertical slices:** prioritize *real file -> Rust file service -> typed IPC -> Svelte/CodeMirror or reader -> save/reopen* rather than large mockups. Keep source and UI changes reviewable.
3. **Stay inside locked stack:** Rust + Tauri 2 + Svelte 5 + TypeScript + CodeMirror 6; avoid introducing another full editor, browser runtime, app server, database-as-vault or plugin marketplace. Add dependencies only after license/security/perf check.
4. **Measure and verify:** every phase performs targeted tests then wider fmt/lint/typecheck/workspace checks. Benchmark release builds on named hardware and inspect memory of Rust and WebKit helper processes. Don't claim macOS coverage after running Linux only.
5. **Keep the plan current:** do not create new Markdown files. Keep each phase’s implementation breakdown, decisions, evidence, completion and deviation notes under that phase in this `plan.md`; link non-Markdown fixtures/results as needed. Existing repository entry-point documentation may be updated, but do not split phase records into separate documents. Fill `Status`, `Completion notes`, `Deviation notes` with actual commands/results, commit/file references, benchmark records, blockers and ADRs. Update the requirement traceability table as milestones pass.
6. **Fail honestly:** when a source-fidelity, UTF-16 mapping, external-file race, privilege boundary, Git-index mutation or large-file-reader gate fails, stop that unsafe path, save diagnostics and implement a correct small fallback. Never silently delete user text, weaken CSP, allow untrusted execution or stage the wrong hunk to satisfy a test.
7. **Honor feature scopes:** SQLite is the only SQL backend; broad TeX means broad *math* plus explicitly trusted local TeX, not arbitrary automatic code compilation; Visual uses source-backed edits; Tauri is explicitly WebView-based. Owner-approved exceptions should be recorded in ADRs.
8. **Use iterative phase handoffs:** follow the dependency table and advance a slice after its own prerequisite gates pass, unless the owner requires a checkpoint. A blocked optional feature does not freeze independent reader work. Keep partial phases In progress, report the smallest genuine blocker and never attempt the entire roadmap in one patch. Reviewing this plan does not itself authorize starting implementation.
9. **Expose known limits:** if a 100 MB document can be read progressively but not visual-edited in full, document it. If a TeX package or Mermaid diagram is unsupported, show the original source. If a review anchor is stale, mark it orphaned.
10. **Handoff clearly:** list completed features, paths/commits, tests and measurements, not-run checks, deviations and exact next steps. No promises of future background delivery.
